# Roadmap — master status and completion plan

**Date:** 27 Oct 2026 · **Author:** Claude (Sonnet 5.5) at the founder's request · **Replaces:** the need to read the ~45 earlier planning docs (now in `docs/archive/`)

**Execution plan revised:** 28 Oct 2026. Section 5 now follows the student lifecycle and supersedes the previous A–L build ordering. Section 7 separates recommendations from decisions still needed. No application code or live database state was changed in this revision. The 27 Oct status observations remain dated observations, not fresh production verification.

This is the one document that says where the build stands, feature by feature, what is left, and in what order to do it. Where it disagrees with an older document, trust this one for *status* and `FOUNDER-PRODUCT-DECISIONS.md` for *product intent*.

---

## 0. Read this first

1. **The app is far more built than it feels.** ~240 source files, 49 migrations, 48 API routes, 30 pages, and a 649-assertion offline test suite that passes with 0 failures. Nothing was "lost" — the *documents* sprawled (45+ overlapping plans), so the code's real state became invisible.
2. **The biggest capability gap is reviewed content.** The repository's bank report counts **262 items in its combined eligible-tier total against a 20,000 target (1.3%)**: 243 verifier-passed and 19 reviewed. These are not 262 interchangeable human-approved diagnostic questions. The report covers Ethics/FSA, with 80 of 102 readings having no item in that combined total; it is not a fresh live-database audit. Even those two subjects are not proven capable of repeated, full-scope weekly papers. First reconcile eligibility by assessment purpose, then measure usable coverage and unseen re-check depth.
3. **Second gap: the backend can leave inconsistent state.** Task changes, Coach proposal execution and paper start are not atomic (confirmed in code, §4 "Integrity"). Fix before real users.
4. **Third gap: nothing reaches the student when they are not in the app.** No reminders, no notifications, no email nudges are wired. Your brief asked for accountability mechanisms; the in-app ones exist, the outbound ones do not.
5. **Credential handling:** the earlier audit reports a committed database password in the now-deleted `scripts/legacy/test-db.cjs`. Treat that credential as exposed and arrange rotation through the project owner now; deletion does not remove it from Git history. No remote configured is not proof that it never left the machine. Do not print the credential or rewrite history without a separate reviewed plan.

This subsection records the 27 October author's checks. The new 28 October checks and their limits are recorded at the end of §8; do not read historical counts as a fresh audit.

**Not done, so not claimed:** no live-database run, no browser click-through, no `next build`, no generation call, no check of what is applied in the remote Supabase project. "Built and offline-verified" below means *the logic is implemented and its unit/integration-style scripts pass* — not *I used it as a student and it worked*. Section 8 gives you a click-through script to verify the important ones yourself.

## 3. Feature by feature

Each feature: **what it does and how it works** (so you can check me), then **gaps**, then **to do**. *(T##)* refers to the Oct-13 audit's finding IDs, kept so nothing is renumbered.

### A. Landing and legal
**How it works.** `/` shows the product story (four signals "never blended into one number", "Not teaching. Telling.", the weekly loop, a static `ReadinessDemo` card captioned "not live data"). Footer/legal at `/privacy`, `/terms`.
**Gaps.** Terms/Privacy both open "**MVP notice:** this is a product prototype" (T20). Landing says "Six short screens" — onboarding is five. No CFA Institute non-affiliation footer verified on every page (PRD §14 requires it). No public demo (§3O).
**To do.** Fix the "six" copy; real legal text via counsel; non-affiliation footer sitewide; honest capability claims (see §3H — do not imply full-syllabus testing).

### B. Authentication
**How it works.** `/signup`, `/login`, `/check-email`, `/forgot-password`, `/update-password`, `/auth/{callback,confirm,error}`. `src/proxy.ts` refreshes the session and sends signed-out users from `/app/*` and `/onboarding/*` to `/login?next=…`. `/app/layout.tsx` redirects anyone whose `profiles.onboarding_status` isn't `complete` to `/onboarding`. Safe-redirect helper blocks open redirects.
**Gaps.** Email verification-before-onboarding is a founder decision (#13) not proven enforced; Google/OAuth not proven; `RESEND_API_KEY` exists but Supabase auth email needs SMTP configured in Supabase itself — the key alone does nothing (T20). Expired/reused-link and OAuth-cancel paths untested end to end.
**To do.** Configure Supabase SMTP; enforce verified-email gate; real tests for each recovery path (§5 phases 0/1).

### C. Onboarding  ← your original priority
**How it works (verify this).**
1. **Your exam** — pick a sitting from the `exam_windows` table (the page title is hard-wired "Which 2027 exam…"), say registered/not, exact date only if registered (must fall in that sitting's month), timezone.
2. **How you study** — self-study / self-paced course / instructor-led / hybrid; class pattern none/fixed/variable/mixed; weekly class rows (days, times, optional end date); an optional "next known class" for variable patterns.
3. **Where you are** — a stage plus per-topic status ("topic exposure").
4. **Your week** — quick presets (weekday mornings/evenings/weekends) or custom study windows; fixed non-study commitments; longest comfortable sitting. There is an explicit "**cannot set this yet**" path, so nobody is blocked.
5. **Review** — shows what Roadmap assumed; a server RPC previews, then confirms and creates the first week.
Answers land in **structured tables** (`availability_windows`, commitments, `profiles.*`), not one JSON blob. Settings re-opens any single step. **No step can deny access** — the only validations are "registered needs a date", "date inside chosen sitting", "timezone valid".

**Fixed since my Sept analysis:** structured writes, no gate, planner re-enabled, one design system.

**Gaps.**
- **Possible additional context, not all required questions:** prior CFA attempts, finance/maths background, what usually derails the plan and reminder preferences. Their predictive value is not validated here; the historical effort model is not authority to prescribe hours. `diagnostic_day` existing in the DB is not a reason to ask it—the agreed route defines cadence. Keep only context with a clear consumer and allow optional/unknown responses.
- Hard-coded "2027" in step copy; unsupported sittings are rejected with an error, not offered a path.
- Preview/accept idempotency and failed-activation recovery exist (`/api/activation/recover`) but were only offline-tested.
**To do.** Keep the five-step setup. Collect any approved extra context progressively and optionally, with an explicit consumer; do not add a diagnostic-day choice. Self-study papers remain Sunday and class-led papers follow the class cycle. Prior attempts/background must not silently produce a prescribed-hours target, readiness judgment or feasibility gate. Real browser verification of the full path is still needed.

### D. Home
**How it works.** Server component. Reads *operating state* (activation state, cycle, today's blocks, repairs, attention queue, integrity flags), the four **signals**, the current study week, and the active roadmap. Layout: date + exam countdown (exact if booked, "indicative" if not) → **Next action card** → **Today's sequence** → **week at a glance** → **needs attention** (decisions, minus whatever Next action already shows) → four signals → roadmap strip. Quick "Log learning" button. A failed service shows a banner naming what didn't load; it does not fake zeros.
**Next-action priority** (`nextActionSelector.ts`): recover failed activation → resolve stale/conflict → … → resume/start/place task → paper → closure. Skipped/deferred work is excluded.
**Gaps.** `OperatingState` uses wall-clock `dataVersion` and fixed-false integrity flags (T15); Home loads signals/week/roadmap separately and swallows some errors to `null`, so "service failed" and "nothing yet" can look alike.
**To do.** Real revision numbers; request-level dedupe; distinguish failure vs absence; final-week and post-exam modes (§3M).

### E. Calendar
**How it works.** `/app/calendar?week=&view=&task=`. Desktop week grid, agenda view, task drawer (`?task=<id>`), drag-to-place, conflict dialog, undo toast, commitment add/edit, class "log-class" route, a paper strip for the week's test, draft-vs-accepted labelling. Mutations go through `/api/tasks[/id]` and `/api/commitments[/id]` with revision checks (409 on stale).
**Gaps (all confirmed unless noted).**
- **T03** `setExecution` updates the task, then writes the history event separately; the event write only `console.error`s on failure (`taskService.ts:149-161`). A completed task can exist with no history. Split shortens the original before inserting the remainder; the remainder drops curriculum identifiers.
- **T10** study portions up to 180 min ignore the student's max sitting; partially-allocated modules drop out of the week's pool.
- **T11** one placed repair stops further repairs that week.
- **T13** three different "which week is this" resolvers (next confirmed class vs recurring weekday vs week-navigation).
- Mobile parity not verified (Oct-13 note: viewport override didn't change).
**To do.** Atomic task commands in Postgres (task + event + split in one RPC); one occurrence/cycle resolver; sitting-length-aware splitting that conserves remaining effort; unplaced work stays visible; mobile day/agenda; tablet 3-day.

### F. Curriculum
**How it works.** Seeded from `data/curriculum-2027/*.json` into a **102-reading / 10-subject spine** with learning outcomes (343 LOS). Three views: **overview** (subjects → readings, coverage), **roadmap** (phases `first_pass → consolidation → revision → final`, with the last 8 weeks reserved *first*; scope allocated to **weeks not hours**; milestones as ranges), **readiness** (LOS tracker banded `repair / verify / maintain / untested`). Plus per-reading notes (rich-text editor) and flashcards, a confidence override (`ConfidenceControls`), and an exam-target picker. Self-report never counts as evidence.
**Gaps.** Notes/flashcards/override paths I did not trace. "Needs another check / maintain / not yet tested" emphasis after first pass is described in the build track but depends on §3H evidence. Long summaries deferred.
**To do.** Revision-queue views driven by evidence age; exact links into Calendar/Tests per reading; reconcile the legacy `learning_modules` and new `curriculum_readings` models (two live concepts of "module").

### G. Learning capture
**How it works, updated 30 October.** Home, Calendar, Tests, the dedicated `/app/log` page and reading pages share one learning-log form and `/api/log-learning` command. One sitting records explicit outcomes, one optional reported duration and one receipt. A reading is not pre-confirmed. Curriculum, paper scope and shared signals consume those confirmations; study does not create test evidence. The scheduled **Class log** (`/app/calendar/log-class` → `/api/class-log/{context,preview,confirm}`) still handles class-occurrence follow-up separately; this slice does not rebuild that workflow.
**Fixed going forward.** The T18 synthetic-duration paths are retired for students. Unknown time remains null, retries cannot duplicate the sitting, and cancellation removes active coverage/time while retaining history. Historical rows were not backfilled or silently reinterpreted. Full-reading roadmap exposure requires all canonical outcomes, rather than a single logged subset.
**Remaining.** Partial-reading effort/prerequisite planning remains conservative. The separate scheduled-class follow-up command and all historical capture variants are not certified by this quick-log slice. See the dated implementation/QA record below.

### H. Tests — papers, bank, taking, results, repair  ← the central product
**How it works.** Two assessment API families are live:
- **New** `/api/tests/*`: `scope` → `scope/confirm` → `start` → `attempt/[id]/{answer,pause,progress,submit,result}`. `startAssessment` freezes the scope snapshot, writes a blueprint and form, assembles items, and creates the attempt (resumes an open one rather than duplicating).
- **Old** `/api/diagnostic/*`: still used by the Tests page's "More checks (module and concept)" cards, repair proposals, and challenges.
Paper kinds: weekly (diagnostic / revisionary / mixed), reading, subject, repair, mock. Blueprint = subject-weighted allocation capped at **90**; weekly soft cap 60; mock = 90 q / 135 min. Items must be reviewed-tier and in scope. Results show per-reading/subject breakdown, confidence-vs-correctness, time patterns, scoped SWOT, and untested scope. Students can challenge a question; a voided item reverses its evidence.
**Gaps.**
- **CONTENT (blocking):** the dated bank report combines verifier-passed and reviewed items (Ethics 99, FSA 163, other subjects 0); it does not prove reviewed weekly-paper capability. Its aggregate minimum must also be checked per outcome, not just per reading. Correct the report and run a scoped, read-only inventory before making fresh counts or launch claims. See §5's continuous content track.
- **T05** blueprint, form, form-items, attempt and scope snapshot are separate inserts; only the concurrent-open-attempt loser is cleaned up.
- **T07** `assembly.ts` fills a shortage with *any remaining item in the same reading* (lines 76-78), which can test an outcome the student never completed — directly violating the founder rule "never test uncompleted scope".
- **T08** the 90 cap is enforced on the total but not on per-outcome/per-reading allocations the assembler consumes.
- **T16** bank audit lumps verified/reviewed/approved; assembly and evidence policy use different eligibility; selection uses random comparator sorts.
- Two API families to consolidate (T21).
**To do.** (1) Build the reviewed bank through §5.2's continuous track. (2) Fix correctness-critical T05/T07/T08 in phase 0 before expanding delivery. (3) One trust-policy matrix. (4) Seeded selection + exposure tracking. (5) Consolidate API families behind shared services in phase 3. Size transactional changes from their actual database dependencies, not an assumption that they are small.

### I. Weekly closure and next-week generation
**How it works.** `/app/review-week` (not a nav tab, per decision #4). Gate states: `paper_scored`, `paper_pending`, `no_paper_allowed`, `not_ready`. Rendering is a **pure read**. The student resolves each due task as completed / skipped / deferred / **unknown** (unknown stays unknown — never assumed). Accept calls one RPC, `accept_weekly_review`, which closes the old cycle, applies the resolutions and opens the next cycle with its tasks in a single transaction. Self-study: learn Mon–Sat, paper Sunday; class-led: week boundary follows the class pattern.
**Gaps.** **T09** pace is computed from *planned* durations and `generateWeek` always passes empty prior completion rates → week two is not yet driven by observed behaviour, which is the "deviations / actual capacity" idea at the heart of your brief. Irregular-class fallback rule needs your confirmation (Q8).
**To do.** Closed-cycle observed capacity; carryover/repair/drop preview; one policy for capacity.

### J. Coach
**How it works.** `/app/coach` → `/api/coach`. Order: auth → hourly limit (40) → classify intent (`struggling`, `day_was_bad`, `redo`, `next`, `free`) → **tutoring refusal** if the message asks to be taught → load the student's real situation → **code, not the model, decides proposals** (`defer_today`, `recheck_block`) → deterministic reply; free-form questions go to the NVIDIA-hosted model grounded in that evidence, with fallback to the deterministic reply. Changes are *proposals* the student accepts; nothing is silently scheduled. The system prompt forbids teaching, invented page references, readiness claims and hour prescriptions.
**Gaps.** **T04** `decideProposal` marks the proposal `executed` *before* applying the edits, then applies tasks one by one; a mid-way failure leaves a half-applied "executed" proposal (`coach/service.ts:132-165`). **T17** two separate LLM clients (`src/lib/llm.ts` ignores configured base URL/model; Coach has its own) and quota is count-then-write. Only 6 policy phrase tests; no evaluation set.
**To do.** Atomic or resumable-idempotent execution in phase 0; one model gateway with deadline/fallback and a real eval set completed in phase 8.

### K. Settings and account
**How it works.** `/app/settings` shows four sections (exam, how you study, where you are, your week) each with a "Change" link into the matching onboarding step in edit mode; a "classes finished?" toggle for class-led students; password reset link; account deletion.
**Gaps.** No "preview consequences before replacing future work" on edits; no notification preferences, data export, or retention controls; deletion needs re-auth and dependency cleanup verified (T-ops).
**To do.** Impact preview; export; deletion hardening; preferences once §3L exists.

### L. Accountability layer — the missing half of your brief
**What exists in-app.** The weekly paper is the accountability event (decision D5): skipping the work shows up in the result. Execution signal separates done / partial / postponed / skipped / unreported. Weekly closure forces reconciliation of unlogged work.
**What does not exist.** Reminders, notifications, quiet hours, email nudges, streaks/consistency tracking, a "you've gone quiet" recovery flow, public commitments. `RESEND_*`, Sentry and PostHog keys are in `.env.example` with **no runtime integration** (T20). Decision D5 deliberately avoids streak gamification — you should confirm whether that still holds given the stronger "accountability" language in your brief (Q5).
**To do.** Define the mechanism set (Q5), then build: reminder preferences → scheduler/outbox → email via Resend → in-app inbox → quiet hours/opt-out.

### M. Revision, mocks, final week, after exam
**How it works.** Roadmap phases and weights exist; the final 8 weeks are reserved first. A `mock` assessment kind exists in `startAssessment`.
**Gaps.** **T12** the day allocator emits generic "Study" tasks in every non-final phase and returns without planning mock/review work in the final phase — a phase label with no phase-specific work. No executable spaced-revision, mock appointments + review blocks, final-week mode, exam-day logistics, post-exam archive or retake path.
**To do.** Phase-specific strategies (revision driven by stale/weak evidence, not just "easiest"); linked mock sessions (Q7); final-week reduction of decisions; archive on confirmed exam finish; deferred-exam and class→self-study transitions.

### N. Internal operations (item review, manifest)
**How it works.** `checkIsReviewer` fails closed against `user_roles(role='diagnostic_reviewer')`. `/app/tests/review` and `/manifest` are reviewer-only; the "Item review" button shows only to reviewers.
**Gaps.** Decision #10 says move these out of student navigation and into an internal route group; they still live at `/app/tests/*`.
**To do.** Move to `/internal/*` with staff-role middleware; add generation/verification queue visibility.

### O. Public demo sandbox
**Status.** Decision #12 requires an unauthenticated read-only sandbox showing Calendar → paper → analysis → closure → adapted week. **Not built** (landing has a static preview only).

### P. Data layer
**Gaps.** **T01** `scripts/migrate.cjs` always reads `.env.local` regardless of `--env`, its dry-run *creates* `schema_migrations`, checksums aren't compared, TLS verification is off. **T02** `supabase/schema.sql` (20 tables) is stale vs migrations (41 `create table`s) and a note admits `profiles` pre-dated migration tracking → no reproducible fresh install. 49 migrations incl. 9 `fix_*` patches.
**To do.** One reconciled baseline + upgrade path; safe runner (or switch to Supabase CLI); generated DB types; staging project separate from prod.

### Q. Authorization
**Gap (T06, confirmed).** `/app/layout.tsx` is the only place that checks setup completion. 40+ API routes (tasks, commitments, coach, tests/start, roadmap, signals, curriculum/*, weekly-review, class-log, log-learning, diagnostic/*…) check *login* but not *onboarding complete*. Not a data leak (each is user-scoped), but a half-onboarded account can mutate state, and server routes use the **service-role admin client** widely, so correctness rests on each route filtering by `user_id`.
**To do.** One shared `requireActiveCandidate()` guard; audit service-role call sites; direct RPC access tests as anon / incomplete / candidate A vs B / staff.

### R. QA and CI
**Strong:** ~25 pure-domain suites + ~23 `e2e-*` scripts (need a live Supabase and `E2E_ALLOW_RESET`; I did not run them).
**Gaps (T19).** `tests/e2e/truth.spec.ts` has two **empty test bodies** that pass trivially; `verify` omits `coach:policy:test` and every live-DB `e2e:*` script; `lint` is only `tsc`; no CI; `lucide-react`, `react`, `react-dom` pinned to `latest`.
**To do.** Real Playwright journeys; CI running `verify` + build; ESLint; pin versions.

### S. Ops, legal, deploy
No hosting chosen, no Sentry/PostHog wired, no backup/restore rehearsal, no rate limits beyond Coach, no account export. Legal copy is prototype. CFA non-affiliation and no-results-guarantee language must be final before real candidates.

## 5. Lifecycle build plan — revised 28 October

**Outcome:** a candidate can enter midweek, complete a first pass, recover from deviations, revise, sit supported mocks, repair those results and reach the exam without losing scope, evidence or control of their calendar. The existing code is the starting point; phase labels and passing unit tests are not completion evidence.

### 5.1 Rules and delivery order

- **Access:** verified identity and required setup are gates; perceived feasibility is not. Choosing when to launch is separate from rejecting individual candidates.
- **Cadence:** self-study learns/revises Monday–Saturday with the paper on Sunday; class-led/hybrid follows the confirmed class cycle. No arbitrary diagnostic-day question.
- **Three different truths:** recorded learning changes coverage; valid assessments change understanding; time and completion history inform planning. A missed task is not a wrong answer. A reserved study hour is not an actual hour spent.
- **Forecast vs commitment:** the horizon is a revisable projection; only the accepted near-term cycle is committed. No daily silent rewriting of the entire roadmap.
- **Capture simplicity:** unknown time stays unknown; scope needs confirmation where ambiguous. No required extra questionnaire unless its input changes a specific feature.
- **No teaching or sources feature:** guide to topic/reading/LOS, organise and assess; do not turn Coach or task drawers into a course.
- **Safe prioritisation:** shortfall creates a visible decision or unplaced obligation, not inflated hours, invisible omissions or an infinite repair backlog.

```text
Verified setup → first accepted week → record real scope
                                          ↓
                        diagnostic / revisionary / mixed paper
                                          ↓
                         valid result → repair → independent re-check
                                          ↓
                    reconcile logs → preview → next accepted week
                                          ↓
                    first-pass coverage complete, not “mastery complete”
                                          ↓
                    rolling revision → mocks + review + focused repairs
                                          ↓
                          final-week plan → exam → archive / retake
```

| Phase | Student-visible outcome | Primary dependency |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | Changes are saved truthfully and recover safely | Isolated test DB, migration baseline and transactional commands |
| 1 | A usable first week around real life | Phase 0; existing five-step setup |
| 2 | Missed/partial/extra learning has a clear consequence | Phase 1; one scope/session model |
| 3 | A supported weekly paper produces useful analysis and repair | Phase 0–2; reviewed content for the test scope |
| 4 | Week two onward adapts through first-pass completion | Phase 2–3; actual execution and valid evidence |
| 5 | Completed syllabus becomes a purposeful revision plan | Phase 4; evidence-age and coverage policies |
| 6 | Mocks include sitting, analysis, repair and re-check time | Phase 3/5; reviewed full-session breadth and depth |
| 7 | Final week and post-exam states remain useful | Phase 4–6; explicit exam horizon |
| 8 | Coach, Settings and accountability work across every mode | Commands supplied by earlier phases; scoped prompt/evaluation work |
| 9 | A supportable, honestly marketed release | All promised capability gates, operations and legal review |

**Continuous tracks from phase 0:** reviewed question production and automated/browser QA. Home, Coach and Settings receive the relevant connection in each phase; phase 8 completes and hardens them rather than leaving them disconnected until the end. Design is reviewed per feature before implementation, not bolted on after the logic.

### Phase 0 — Reliable commands and a safe test baseline

**Build first:**

- Confirm credential rotation with the owner, without displaying the secret. Preserve the current staged cleanup; no reset, bulk commit or history rewrite is implied by this plan.
- Fix migration targeting, read-only dry-run, checksum comparison and TLS verification; reconcile applied schema and prove both fresh install and upgrade in staging. T01/T02 cannot wait until after feature migrations.
- Introduce shared API/RPC authorisation: verified user, setup completion, ownership and staff role as appropriate. Setup, recovery and privacy/account endpoints need their own explicit policies, not a blanket workspace guard.
- Atomic task mutation + history + split/undo lineage (T03); proposal execution (T04); paper/form/scope/reservation (T05). No LLM/network call inside a database lock. Revalidate computed proposals against current revisions at commit.
- Fix eligible-outcome selection, delivered question ceiling and trust-policy disagreement (T07/T08/T16) before real assessment delivery.
- Replace empty Playwright tests, add CI `verify` + production build, and create dedicated two-account fixtures. Guard destructive scripts by actual project identity, not only an environment name.

**Done when:** injected event/form/task failures roll back; simultaneous retries produce one result; candidate A cannot mutate candidate B; incomplete setup cannot invoke workspace commands; no GET/prefetch/render changes student state. Migrations are not applied to production merely to prove this gate.

### Phase 1 — A useful first week

**Experience:** “Tell Roadmap my target and constraints, review a manageable first week, then open a block and know exactly what to do.”

- Keep five setup steps. Prior attempts may optionally identify previously studied areas; background may inform Coach wording, not unvalidated hour multipliers. Ask derailment/preferences when relevant or later in Settings. Keep an explicit unknown path.
- Resolve tentative target month/year versus booked date and supported curriculum. An unsupported year is honestly labelled, not silently mapped to 2027 content.
- Midweek starts draft only actionable remaining dates, not fabricated past study. Baseline assumptions and insufficient paper scope are visible; sparse evidence is not a 0% ability score.
- One occurrence/cycle resolver across Calendar, Home, paper scheduling and weekly review: recurring, variable and moved/cancelled classes, effective dates, exceptions and timezones. Historical accepted cycles keep their dates.
- Study blocks carry goal, reading/LOS scope, method, estimated length, maximum sitting constraint and what completion means. Small fragments do not silently lose remaining effort.
- Calendar offers timed, day-preferred and unplaced work; protects life commitments. Desktop week, tablet adaptive view, mobile day/agenda with equivalent keyboard actions.
- Home opens the exact task; Curriculum shows its scope; class capture confirms actual taught scope instead of assuming planned class content was covered.

**Routes:** `/onboarding/*` → `/api/onboarding/plan/{preview,confirm}` → `/app`; `/app/calendar?task=<id>`; `/app/curriculum/[subject]/[reading]`; `/api/activation/recover` for explicit retry.

**Done when:** fresh self-study, class-led and hybrid candidates each reach one accepted useful week; no double activation; no prescribed-hours gate; moving a class affects the right future work after confirmation. Auth email/Google flows must be exercised, not only mocked.

### Phase 2 — Daily reality, missed blocks and useful Curriculum

**Experience:** “I did less, more or something different; I can record it quickly and understand the consequence.”

| Recorded event | Required outcome |
|---|---|
| Completed | Record confirmed covered scope; keep actual time optional; expose it to the relevant paper. No automatic mastery. |
| Partial | Keep completed outcomes and an explicit remaining obligation; no rounding to a whole reading. |
| Missed / deferred | Preserve the original event and propose new placement or scope trade-off. Do not overwrite tomorrow or call it completed. |
| Unknown | Remain unknown until reconciled; no invented time or learning. |
| Extra learning | Add coverage once, reconcile any duplicate planned scope and revise the projection. |
| Changed class | Update its occurrence and confirm actual scope; preview cycle/paper consequences without changing history. |
| No room | Show unplaced work with choices; never schedule into protected time or silently drop it. |

- Unify quick Home/Calendar logging, planned-task completion, Curriculum capture and class logging around one authoritative session and outcome references. Do not multiply one session's time by its number of outcomes.
- Splitting preserves scope/repair lineage and total remaining effort; reopening/undo safely recalculates coverage and paper eligibility. A historical correction does not silently alter a frozen paper.
- Return a before/after summary for recovery: changed tasks, carried scope, paper effect and pressure. Use existing confirmed-change direction; no arbitrary new automatic recovery policy.
- Curriculum becomes an action map: studied / not studied, tested / untested, current evidence and confidence shown separately. Reading/LOS rows offer Log learning, Place in Calendar or Check when supported—not only long summaries.
- Distinguish available time, planned effort and recorded actual time. One poor week does not prove a permanently low capacity.

**Routes:** `/api/tasks[/id]`, `/api/commitments[/id]`, `/api/log-learning`, `/api/class-log/{context,preview,confirm}`, `/api/curriculum/*`; existing Home/Calendar capture widgets and `/app/log` detailed route.

**Done when:** one session changes all relevant projections consistently; splits conserve scope/time; multiple remaining repairs can be placed on different days; moved/deferred work is not counted twice or lost. A student can understand a recovery preview without reading algorithm internals.

### Phase 3 — Weekly paper → trustworthy result → repair

**Experience:** “Test what I actually studied, show what the result supports and give me the next practical action.”

- Freeze confirmed completed scope when reserving the paper. Newly studied scope is diagnostic; revisited scope revisionary; mixed papers retain section semantics. No same-reading substitution from uncompleted outcomes.
- Use exam-weight priority, page tie-breaks, breadth and target rotation within eligible scope. Persist selection seed, item/family exposure and actual-vs-requested coverage. Rephrasing a seen item does not make it unseen evidence.
- Enforce ≤90 on the delivered form and every allocation, including repair papers. If all weekly scope cannot fit, show the pending coverage decision (Q9); do not claim everything was tested.
- Bank shortage is a system capability limitation, not a student failure. Keep untested scope visible and use the explicit no-paper closure path where permitted; a supported paper still pending is a different state, not an automatic waiver.
- Mandatory per-question confidence, server-authoritative timing where timed, autosave/retry, refresh, two-tab conflicts, interrupted time, expiry and unanswered handling. Do not force a confidence choice to consume an exam-like mock's running clock; design a separate confirmation capture if needed and label the mode.
- Results show valid raw score, scope/sample size, reading/subject breakdown, confidence and time patterns. Confidence/timing inform interpretation, not arbitrary correctness points. Mark invalid items and denominator changes clearly; no pass probability.
- After submission, Tests provides the key, concise original item rationale and a relevant LOS pointer. Coach uses that evidence to guide the next study action, not expand into chapter teaching.
- SWOT separates supported successes/strengths and weaknesses from inferred opportunities/threats. A correct guess is not secure mastery, and related untested topics are not diagnosed failures.
- A repair proposal states the specific LOS gap, study-method suggestion, estimated sitting, evidence reason and independent re-check. It is guidance, not a lesson. One active thread per issue; another miss updates that thread.
- Student confirmation places repair work; Sunday/end-of-cycle results target the next cycle. Invalidated evidence cancels obsolete future repairs with an audit reason and recomputes summaries.

**Routes:** `/app/tests` → `/api/tests/scope[/confirm]` → `/api/tests/start` → `/app/tests/[attemptId]` → result; existing attempt APIs, repair/challenge APIs and confirmed Calendar proposal path. Consolidate old/new APIs behind shared commands before retiring callers.

**Done when:** supported first-week scope completes start → answer → submit → result → confirmed repair → fresh re-check; retry does not duplicate evidence or work. No out-of-scope item or unreviewed evidence slips in. Do not claim the bank is ready because this passes on fixtures.

### Phase 4 — Week two through first-pass completion

**Experience:** “The next week reflects what happened; the long-term roadmap adjusts without becoming an unpredictable moving target.”

- Quick review surfaces on Home after the paper, or after an explicit bank-limited no-paper state. Reconcile outstanding logs as completed/partial/deferred/skipped/unknown; never infer them to unlock closure.
- Compare accepted plan, recorded scope, optional actual durations, deviations, evidence and available future windows. Replace the empty completion-rate input and deduplicate execution observations.
- Learn rolling operational capacity before making strong topic-pace claims. Planned duration can remain an assignment/proxy, never be renamed measured actual time. Missing durations are not zeros; sparse or contradictory data widens uncertainty.
- Preview next-week repairs/re-checks, carried learning, new scope and maintenance. Track each unplaced obligation individually. Old backlog does not consume every later week indefinitely; present explicit choices with consequences.
- Atomic acceptance closes one cycle and opens one successor. The user can inspect and log activity while a paper is pending; no fabricated paper score or silent closure.
- Re-project the remaining syllabus when sufficient evidence or commitments change. Explain the cause and preserve the protected final-preparation period unless a trade-off is accepted.
- For a nine-month preparation horizon, aim to finish first-pass coverage by roughly seven months and protect the last two months for mocks, revision and final checks. If the horizon or actual progress cannot support that, show an explicit compressed-strategy choice; never promise the boundary regardless of reality. Retention/revision can already run alongside first-pass learning.
- First-pass completion is confirmed study coverage, not “every LOS mastered.” Untested and weak areas become revision priorities. A coverage-complete candidate can progress to revision without a fake all-green status.
- Support slower/faster topics, extra subjects finished early, holidays, interrupted months, class completion and class → self-study transitions. Revisit estimates regularly without noisy daily whole-horizon re-planning.

**Routes:** `/app/review-week`, `/api/weekly-review/{state,accept}`, `/api/roadmap`, `/api/signals`, Curriculum Roadmap and Home. No new review tab.

**Done when:** two otherwise identical candidates with different recorded weeks receive appropriately different proposals; two completed cycles feed later planning; reaching first-pass completion produces executable revision work, not an empty calendar.

### Phase 5 — Revision that changes with retention

**Experience:** “I have finished the portion once. Show what to retrieve, what to repair and what is still uncertain.”

- Replace generic Study tasks with distinguishable retrieval, focused weak-area revision, mixed revision practice and re-check tasks, each with scope and completion intent.
- Ongoing classes remain real protected commitments during revision/final preparation; a phase change cannot silently remove or reschedule an institute's class.
- Build a due queue using valid evidence age, repeated misses, confidence mismatches, exam relevance and prerequisite relationships. Keep maintenance across previously strong scope so the queue is not only failures.
- Schedule retrieval → supported revisionary check → update due state. Retention intervals are versioned, evaluated heuristics, not a claim of scientifically personalised memory prediction from one response.
- Successful fresh checks can lengthen the maintenance interval; unstable results shorten follow-up within available capacity. Stale evidence means “needs another check,” not proven forgetting.
- Include syllabus-wide breadth and item-family diversity over successive papers. Re-reading alone never clears a tested weakness.
- Preserve unfinished first-pass scope if someone enters final preparation late; expose compressed-strategy choices rather than silently labelling everything revision.

**Routes:** existing Curriculum readiness/roadmap views, Calendar, Tests and Coach; no independent revision silo or duplicate progress store.

**Done when:** a coverage-complete fixture receives an actual week of revision; strong, stale, weak and untested scopes receive distinct treatment; success/failure on a re-check visibly changes subsequent scheduling.

### Phase 6 — Mock scheduling, analysis and recovery

**Experience:** “I can rehearse the exam, review it and repair the important gaps before sitting another mock.”

**Recommended format, pending founder approval:** a full rehearsal is a parent mock with **two linked 90-question, 135-minute session papers**, not one oversized paper. CFA Institute currently describes this exam structure and publishes a 2027 session/topic split. A single 90-question option should be called a session mock, not a full exam. Source: [CFA Institute Level I exam](https://www.cfainstitute.org/programs/cfa-program/candidate-resources/level-i-exam), checked 28 Oct 2026.

- Validate the year-specific topic/session blueprint and weights against the official outline. Do not split a mixed 180-item pool arbitrarily; each session and its question exposure must be valid independently.
- Plan sitting(s), optional break, analysis and repair/re-check time together. Suggest another mock only when those demands fit; do not mandate a mock every week regardless of capacity.
- Reserve held-out eligible item families for rehearsals/re-checks. Protect scarce reviewed items from being consumed by every optional practice session.
- Exam-like mode uses server clocks and no answer feedback before submission. Mandatory confidence capture must be supported without falsely claiming an exact exam interface; make simulated conditions clear.
- Show session results and combined valid-item analysis; if one session is incomplete, label the rehearsal incomplete. No misleading average of sessions with different valid denominators.
- Classify response patterns carefully: conceptual gap, confident misconception, slow-but-correct, uncertain success or possible execution issue. Ask the student to confirm ambiguous error causes rather than invent them.
- Limit/prioritise repairs by evidence, recurrence, relevant weighting and available time. Preview the trade-off with maintenance and remaining scope. Do not create a block for every miss.
- Re-check with independent eligible items. Do not use an improved repeat-paper percentage as evidence of new understanding. Challenge invalidation propagates into both session and parent analysis.

**Routes:** existing mock-start/attempt/result services; proposed mock-group identifiers and group result link to be specified in this phase. Calendar and weekly review use the same command/evidence services. These grouping routes/schema do not exist merely because this plan proposes them.

**Done when:** linked sessions reserve/submit/retry safely; combined analysis is truthful; accepting a focused repair changes Calendar; a fresh re-check informs the next rehearsal plan; insufficient breadth blocks the full-mock claim, not access to the planner.

### Phase 7 — Last week, exam and next goal

**Experience:** “I have a calm, realistic final week—not the entire backlog compressed into seven days.”

- Switch the action mix to priority review, already-identified gaps, limited purposeful checks and protected recovery/commitments. Do not schedule tasks past the exam or consume the final days with an unreviewable mock by default.
- If the candidate changes availability, preview a small essential set and explicitly deferred scope. No silent promises to finish everything.
- Optional logistics checklist/reminders should point to current official guidance, not hard-coded legal/test-centre rules.
- Support tentative versus booked target, deferment and resit without erasing evidence. Expired target dates trigger a confirmation/update state, not indefinite scheduling against an old exam.
- After the student confirms the exam is over, stop study nudges, archive future work and offer history/export choices. A retake preserves provenance and evidence-age; another level is offered only if supported.

**Done when:** accelerated, late-start and changed-date fixtures reach an intelligible final week; no future study is silently dated after the exam; archive/retake changes are explicit and reversible where appropriate.

### Phase 8 — Complete Coach, Settings, Home and accountability

Earlier phases must already expose their relevant actions here. This phase completes cross-mode behaviour and operational delivery:

- **Coach:** one grounded context/tool schema/model gateway, bounded deadlines/retries and atomic budgets; evals for no teaching, invented certainty/citations, stale state, malicious requests and outages. Preview/confirm uses the same calendar commands; executed means actually committed. Own examples/evals first; fine-tuning only if justified with rights-cleared data.
- **Settings:** impact preview for exam/route/classes/availability/sitting changes; preserve completed history and frozen papers; preferences, export/deletion and session handling. No silent re-onboarding or whole-plan replacement.
- **Home:** one useful next action, concise Today and the next paper/review/decision appropriate to the phase. Explain unknowns; avoid repeated empty metric cards and inconsistent state between pages.
- **Accountability recommendation:** opt-in session/paper reminder, weekly review email and gentle inactivity/recovery nudge, with quiet hours, timezones and opt-out. A consistency history can be neutral completed/partial/deferred/unknown, not punitive streaks. Exact mechanisms await Q5; do not send anything before preferences/consent are implemented.
- **Outbound delivery:** durable outbox/job leases, idempotent delivery, bounded retry/dead-letter and cancellation when a task/paper moves or a goal is archived. Suppress reminders for completed work; notify at a useful cadence, not for every mutation.
- Supabase auth mail needs actual SMTP configuration; an app `RESEND_API_KEY` does not connect verification/reset delivery by itself. Keep auth email, application reminders and marketing choices separate.

**Done when:** an accepted Coach change matches the preview; editing setup changes all applicable future views; reminder retries do not spam; archived goals and completed tasks receive no stale study nudge; provider outage leaves an honest usable fallback.

### Phase 9 — Release, not just a green build

- Complete internal review/manifest operations under a staff-only surface; authentication/authorisation inside each endpoint/RPC, not only a route middleware.
- Build the read-only isolated public sandbox from synthetic data; label examples. Do not simulate success by writing to real candidates.
- Finalise schema types, obsolete caller retirement, README/env ownership and supported dependency versions. Cleanup is measured by a reproducible working app, not deleted file counts. Keep existing staged changes intact until reviewed.
- Verify deployed-runtime browser journeys, tenant isolation, query/pagination limits for long-lived accounts, load and LLM costs. Monitor request/job failure, not just visits.
- Connect redacted error monitoring/support references, backups and tested restore; rehearse rollback/roll-forward and capability disablement.
- Final legal/privacy/content-rights/non-affiliation review, auth SMTP/Google and account export/deletion tests. Billing and entitlement/webhook verification are required for paid release, not assumed built.
- Choose pilot/full launch explicitly. An invited, clearly scoped pilot is recommended, but a later full launch is also compatible with the no-feasibility-rejection policy. Do not market full-syllabus diagnostics/mocks before capability is proven.

**Release gates:** safe development → transactional/authorised core → supported full weekly loop → life-cycle/revision/mock proof → operational/legal release. Dates depend on measured engineering and human-review throughput, not the old S/M/L labels.

### 5.2 Continuous question-bank and review track

1. Correct tier/purpose reporting and inventory every topic/reading/LOS, unseen family, approved version, verifier type, reviewer and held-out use. Do not relabel verifier-passed as human-reviewed.
2. Name review ownership and measure time/defects on a small batch before projecting dates. Review expertise, not only hours, matters. No reviewer time is assumed committed.
3. Prioritise the chosen pilot's first weeks and a complete assessment/repair slice, then expand by cohort need, curriculum sequence and weight. A random spread of questions across all subjects does not produce a usable weekly loop.
4. Pipeline: rights-cleared references → original item drafting → mapping/format/key/distractor checks → independent solution/numerical recomputation where applicable → human review → publish version → challenge/withdrawal. Rephrased/numeric variants inherit family exposure; a verifier's executable formula does not prove its financial interpretation is right.
5. Define separate breadth and repeated-use gates: a first check, independent repairs, spaced revision and held-out mock forms. “Three per LOS” is at most a provisional engineering threshold, not psychometric validation or proof of enough unseen content for months.
6. Simulate item consumption over the entire promised candidate journey, including retries and difficult areas. Expand the bank before that journey would exhaust its eligible items; report gaps by purpose, not only the 20,000 target.
7. Publish only the supported capability. Unsupported test scope stays explicitly unassessed while planning continues; software tests may use labelled synthetic fixtures, student papers may not.

### 5.3 Concrete acceptance journey

Run this same story through API, persisted state and browser, for self-study and a class-led variant:

1. Join midweek with a tentative window; review and accept one week, then add a booked date later safely.
2. Complete reading A, partially complete B, miss C and log extra D. One real session's duration is counted once.
3. Confirm scope; the weekly paper includes eligible A/D and only confirmed parts of B, never C merely because it was planned.
4. Submit with confidence/timing; repeated submit returns the same scored attempt. Accept one justified repair; no duplicate thread.
5. Close the cycle with honest unresolved states and accept a next-week proposal that retains C/B remainder and fits the actual future windows.
6. Re-check the repair with independent items; change a class and a work commitment; preview and accept the consequences.
7. Progress to first-pass coverage completion; the next week contains real revision/maintenance, not an empty phase band.
8. Sit a supported mock rehearsal, inspect session/combined analysis, accept limited repairs, and independently check them before another mock.
9. Make the final week lighter, then confirm exam completion; reminders stop and history remains accessible.

Variants: bank shortage, invalidated item, no free windows, skipped paper, provider outage, stale second device, changed/expired exam target, month away and late-start horizon. Every variant must have an honest visible state, not fabricated completion.

### 5.4 First implementation batch

Start with a narrow Phase-0/1 slice, not all ten phases at once:

1. Staging/project guard and safe migration baseline; owner handles credential rotation.
2. Atomic task execution + event, with revision/idempotency/failure tests.
3. Split/partial scope preservation and accurate learning-session duration.
4. A study-block drawer that explains scope, objective, approach and completion meaning using those contracts.
5. Browser proof: Home → exact block → partial completion → remaining scope → consistent Calendar/Curriculum/paper preview.

**Definition of done for every batch:** user scenario, contract/route changes, reviewed migration if any, deterministic + database + browser checks, retry/failure proof, downstream-state assertions and a completion note in this document. No phase is marked finished from typecheck or screenshots alone.

### 5.5 Historical model conflicts

`VISION-CONTEXT.md` preserves earlier founder history, including decisions later changed. `EFFORT-MODEL.md` contains old hard gates, 350–1000-hour bounds, background multipliers and planned-hours-as-observed assumptions. It is useful historical design input, **not an approved executable policy**. Apply the later founder register: no prescribed-hours target or feasibility rejection; estimates remain qualified; unknown actual time is not manufactured. Any new estimator needs an explicit contract, evaluation and approval—not automatic reinstatement because the file is active again.

## 7. Decisions, recommendations and when they are needed

These are not twelve blockers to starting development. Existing founder decisions remain in force; the recommendations below are not automatically approved.

| # | Decision | Recommendation / existing direction | Needed before |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Invited pilot vs full-capability launch | Recommend a clearly scoped invited pilot; holding public launch is also valid. Neither changes mandatory setup or introduces a feasibility rejection. | Release claims and cohort recruitment |
| 2 | Content reviewer and capacity | Name a qualified reviewer and measure batch review throughput. AI drafting/verifying is not human approval. | Publishing new evidence-grade items; no invented completion date |
| 3 | Optional context | Keep five setup steps. Offer prior attempts/background/derailment only with useful consumers, progressively or in Settings. Do not add diagnostic-day choice or unvalidated background-to-hours rules. | Phase 1 context changes |
| 4 | Test-fear support | Recommend calm language and optional short supported checks; confidence is not a mental-health/fear diagnosis. Any additional self-report is optional and purpose-limited. | Additional measurement beyond current confidence capture |
| 5 | Accountability channels | Recommend opt-in session/paper reminders, weekly review email and gentle inactivity recovery, with quiet hours. No punitive streaks or external partner sharing by default. | Phase 8 outbound implementation |
| 6 | Commercial access | Historical direction is paid roadmap; decide any pilot pricing/trial and paywall location explicitly. Keep privacy/account/recovery actions accessible. | Billing/entitlement build and charging |
| 7 | Full mocks | Recommend linked 90-question session papers with separate clocks and combined analysis; a standalone 90-question product is a session mock. | Phase 6 grouping contract/schema |
| 8 | Irregular-class fallback | Preserve accepted cycles; explain any temporary default when the next class cannot define a viable cycle. Confirm the exact fallback before changing its policy. | Phase 1 cycle resolver |
| 9 | Scope exceeds one capped paper | Explicit carry-forward vs additional separately capped papers. No silent narrowing; do not overload candidates automatically. | Phase 3 breadth policy |
| 10 | Archive/history | Preserve the current archive. No blanket deletion or rollback is requested or needed to deliver this plan. | Only a later explicit cleanup request |
| 11 | Infrastructure/support ownership | Identify staging/production owners, hosting, content review and support/privacy contacts. | Staging/operational gates and release |
| 12 | Credential remediation | Owner should rotate the reportedly exposed DB credential now, update dependent configuration and verify connectivity. No secret inspection, live rotation or Git-history rewrite was performed here. | Production data work / sharing repository |

We can start the guarded transactional-task slice while decisions about mocks, outbound channels and launch timing remain open.

## 8. Initial smoke checks — not full end-to-end certification

With `npm run dev` and a fresh email account:
1. **Onboarding:** complete all 5 steps; on "Your week" try the explicit unknown path; on step 1 try a registered date in the wrong month (should be refused with a clear message). Confirm incomplete setup is gated, completed valid setup enters the app, and there is no feasibility rejection.
2. **Home:** with no accepted week you should see an activation/next-action state, never a fake 0% dashboard.
3. **Settings → Change (exam):** confirm it reopens the right step and returns to Settings.
4. **Calendar:** open `?task=<id>` with a bogus id → expect the "unavailable" fallback, not a crash; drag a block onto a class → conflict dialog; undo.
5. **Tests:** in the supported staging fixture, check a scope with an eligible bank and one without. The former should assemble only if scope, tier, exposure and length requirements are met; the latter should show an honest limitation. FSA membership alone does not guarantee sufficiency.
6. **Coach:** ask "explain duration to me" → it should refuse to teach and redirect; ask "what should I do next" → a grounded, short answer; accept a "defer today" card and check Calendar.
7. **Weekly review:** try to accept with unresolved tasks marked "unknown" — they must stay unknown.

If any of these behave differently from what I described, that is a bug in my reading — tell me which and I'll trace it.

### 28 October verification note

- Re-read the supplied status document, founder decisions, historical vision/effort model and relevant current planning, assessment, task, Coach, migration and browser-test code.
- Reconfirmed separate task/event writes, early Coach execution status, multi-write paper start, same-reading out-of-outcome fallback, empty completion history, generic phase allocation, unsafe migration-runner behaviour and empty browser test bodies in source.
- `npm run verify` exited **0** during this revision. The route audit now lists **80 routes and 171 resolving internal links**, reflecting the retired stub/cleanup; the earlier 81/177 values above belong to the 27 October report.
- No fresh bank/database inventory, production build, browser walkthrough, live model call, migration or account-data change was performed in this planning turn. This note updates the plan, not feature completion status.

### 28 October implementation — first foundation slice

**Status: implemented and tested locally, then activated and verified in the user-confirmed disposable development project. Phase 0 and the first implementation batch are not yet complete.** The pending cleanup/archive changes were preserved. Initial work was local; the separately authorised hosted activation is recorded below. No real student account data, question bank, provider model or Git history was changed.

**Changes now in code**

| Area | Implemented behaviour |
| --- | --- |
| Migration target | Explicit `local`, `staging` or `production` file; no fallback to `.env.local` for hosted targets or ambient app variables. Local means loopback Postgres, not a hosted project carrying a local label. |
| Migration preview | `BEGIN READ ONLY`, history/checksum reads and rollback. No ledger creation, advisory lock or pending SQL execution. A preview is not a rehearsal of the migrations. |
| Migration apply | Explicit target confirmation, verified remote TLS, shared advisory lock, checksum/missing-file/order checks, per-file SQL+ledger transaction, rollback/cleanup. Historical outer BEGIN/COMMIT wrappers are removed in memory; original checksums/file bytes are retained. |
| Untracked schema | Preview reports it; apply refuses it. No guessed baseline or invented migration history. Fresh-install and hosted-upgrade reconciliation remain open (T02). |
| Task execution | `set_task_execution` commits the execution, activity event and retry receipt atomically. Row lock + expected revision prevent conflicting device updates; identical retries reuse the event and skip the service's repeat redistribution call. |
| Actual time | Missing time stays null. Explicit zero is distinct from unknown; invalid, fractional or out-of-range values are rejected rather than silently rounded/clamped. Recording an observation advances the revision even if execution is unchanged. This does not yet solve session-duration accounting across multiple different progress updates. |
| Access | The task GET/PATCH/DELETE API now requires sign-in, verified email and completed setup through a reusable server guard. The RPC independently checks identity, ownership, email and setup. Other app APIs still need the same audit/integration. No feasibility rule was introduced. |
| Failure states | Missing RPC returns an explicit 503 with no unsafe fallback. Failed task/history reads return unavailable, rather than false missing/empty states. Cancelled-origin tasks cannot be resurrected through execution updates. |

**Files and contract**

- Additive migration: `supabase/migrations/20261028_atomic_task_execution.sql`; new `task_execution_commands` table with owner-only read policy and RPC-only app writes.
- Entry points: `src/app/api/tasks/[id]/route.ts` → `taskService.setExecution` → request-scoped `executionCommand` → owned RPC. Existing execution request fields remain compatible. Unknown actual duration may be omitted or null.
- Task state changes remain **activity**, not demonstrated mastery. This command does not manufacture evidence, completed LOS coverage, repair success or a preparation score.
- Local verification tools: `npm run migrations:test`, `npm run task:execution:test`, `npm run foundation:db:test`. The first two are included in `npm run verify`.

**Verification so far**

- Final `npm run verify`, `npm run build` and `git diff --check` exited **0** after the implementation. Route audit: **80 routes, 171 resolving internal links**. This is build/offline verification, not authenticated browser certification.
- 7 migration-runner tests and 8 execution/access tests pass.
- 14 real Postgres scenarios pass in a newly created temporary loopback-only cluster, stopped and removed after testing. Tests do not load `.env.local` or accept a hosted URL.
- Database checks include empty-schema dry-run, first-migration rollback, upgrade rollback with retained prior history, every execution/event type, null/zero actual time, exact and conflicting retries, concurrent devices, forced event/receipt failures, cross-user isolation, incomplete setup, unverified/anonymous access and cancelled tasks.
- The fixture uses the real existing revision trigger and the new migration against explicit command dependencies. **It is not proof of a fresh installation of all migrations, hosted schema parity, full app E2E, notification delivery or model quality.**

**Rollout order and remaining work**

1. The user confirmed the configured project is disposable development. Its 49 recorded checksums match this checkout, and the command's real table dependencies and revision trigger were inspected. This verifies the existing upgrade target; a reproducible fresh-install baseline is still open. Credential rotation remains the owner's separate action.
2. The new SQL is now applied and verified in that development project. Apply and verify it in any separate future release environment **before deploying the changed execution service** there. Until the RPC exists, that service intentionally returns 503 for execution saves.
3. Move/split/delete/undo still have separate legacy writes; Coach proposal execution and paper-start atomicity also remain open. The existing post-save redistribution is neither durable nor part of the execution transaction. Retry suppression prevents one repeated-request path; it does not certify redistribution's scope, capacity or recovery logic.
4. Next local build: preserve split/partial scope and session identity, then make the study-block drawer and Home → Calendar → Curriculum → paper scope journey consistent. Confirmed partial LOS may enter scope; untouched remainder must remain visible.
5. Run authenticated browser proof and staging failure/retry checks for that connected journey. No browser walkthrough or visual change is claimed by this backend slice.

### 29 October — scope-preserving splits and partial progress

**Delivered in development:** a current-cycle Calendar → sitting progress → explicit covered outcomes → Tests scope loop. This is not completion of the full weekly rollover, calendar or assessment programme.

| User action | Connected behaviour now implemented |
| --- | --- |
| Split unfinished study/review work | One owned, revision-guarded transaction shortens the original, creates the second sitting, writes both history events and a retry receipt. Curriculum/LOS, objective, method, purpose and provenance are copied; planned minutes are conserved. Tests, completed/cancelled work and protected commitments cannot be split. |
| Place the new sitting | It appears under **Not yet placed**, with no invented clock time or next-day spill. Its drawer requires an explicit day before Apply is enabled. It retains its original cycle and shared learning scope. |
| Finish one sitting | Calendar records that sitting as done and displays the shared count. Tests does not infer that the whole outcome is complete while another sitting remains. Deleted, missing, partial, deferred or cancelled siblings do not silently complete the group. Re-splitting a sitting retains the same group. |
| Record partial learning | A direct LOS mapping alone no longer makes unconfirmed partial work test-eligible. The Tests link offers only the outcomes belonging to that task. Explicit confirmation adds the selected scope; it does not mark its remaining sitting finished or create diagnostic evidence. |
| Confirm covered outcomes | A second owned RPC validates the open cycle, task mapping and displayed revision, then replaces confirmations with a history event atomically. Failed replacement restores the previous set. Repeated identical confirmation is harmless. Direct authenticated writes to the confirmation table are revoked. |
| Complete all sittings | They produce one full outcome, with merged sources rather than duplicated topics/questions. Group members are read across cycles. Earlier explicit partial confirmations count as revisited outcomes later, without labelling the rest of that reading as previously studied. |
| Log actual minutes | Home and Calendar ask for **new minutes since the previous update**, preserving blank as unknown and accepting explicit zero. Invalid values are not rounded into invented observations. Each accepted progress update is protected against exact duplicate requests. This defines logging semantics; it does not yet certify the planner's observed-rate calculation. |
| Inspect history and paper status | History has loading/error/empty states and shows recorded actual minutes. The Calendar paper strip prioritises **Confirm test scope** over an inaccurate “nothing completed” message and links to the exact Tests section. |

**Implementation and activation**

- Additive migration `20261028_task_scope_and_split.sql` was applied to the already-confirmed disposable development project, after isolated database tests. It adds `task_scope_groups`, `task_split_commands`, `plan_blocks.scope_group_id`, `split_study_task` and `confirm_task_scope`. Previously applied SQL/checksums were not edited.
- Primary connections: `/app/calendar?task=<id>` → `PATCH /api/tasks/<id>` (`split` / `execution`) → owned RPCs; `/app/tests#scope-h` → `POST /api/tests/scope/confirm` → owned confirmation RPC; `getCompletedCycleScope` supplies scope to Tests, Home/Coach consumers and closure. Calendar uses the same group-completion reducer for its drawer count.
- This fixes new splits going forward. **Historical split rows were not assigned invented lineage or silently remapped.** Unsafe legacy “undo only one half” is refused; split merge/undo needs its own complete command. Move/delete/ordinary undo are still legacy non-atomic commands.
- Scope reads now fail rather than interpreting a failed curriculum/history/confirmation read as an empty successful result. No source uploads, teaching responses, compulsory hours or evidence scores were introduced.

**Verification**

- `npm run verify`, `npm run build`, and `git diff --check` pass. Route audit: 80 routes, 171 resolving internal links; this is not a walkthrough of every page.
- **22 isolated Postgres scenarios** pass, including concurrent split retries, repeated splitting, metadata preservation, forced failure after child creation, failed confirmation replacement and cross-user RPC/receipt isolation. **13 execution/access/scope model cases** pass, alongside the existing domain suites.
- **13 real Auth/Next/API scenarios** pass against the development project, including the actual Tests scope preview before and after each sitting and after explicit confirmation. Their dedicated identity, cycle, tasks, events and receipts were cleaned up.
- **Browser:** Chrome through the Browser/Computer runtime, `http://localhost:3101`, desktop 1710×861 and phone 390×844. A separate dummy account split 90 minutes into two 45-minute sittings, logged 20 partial + 25 additional minutes, followed Calendar → Tests, confirmed the one mapped outcome, used the mobile navigation/drawer, and finished the second sitting with unknown duration. Persisted checks found two completed sittings, one shared LOS, 90 planned minutes, **45 reported minutes with the other sitting's duration unknown**, and zero diagnostic evidence. No framework overlay or relevant warning/error appeared in the browser logs. Phone viewport override was reset.
- Screenshots are temporary QA artifacts outside the repository (`/tmp/roadmap-scope-desktop.png`, `/tmp/roadmap-scope-mobile.png`). Browser fixtures and the owned temporary port-3101 server are removed after verification; the existing port-3100 server and real learner data are not reset.
- The Tests reading link was followed into Curriculum: the worked LOS still displayed **Untested · Worked — awaiting retest** and the student's confidence remained unmarked. Study completion did not promote it to demonstrated understanding. The final drawer check showed **Choose a day** selected and Apply disabled for an unplaced sitting.
- A post-sign-out browser observation hit a CDP runtime timeout; the temporary tab was then closed using the documented API. Sign-out UX itself is not certified by this slice. The Calendar/Tests/Curriculum interactions and their console checks completed before that automation timeout; account/data cleanup was independently verified through the server.

QA used one consistent `localhost` origin and verified the dummy account name before mutating anything. No other learner's tasks were changed and no production authentication policy was altered.

**Next required slice: weekly rollover, before claiming unfinished work survives week closure.** The existing closure builds a new week without carrying ordinary unfinished study-task identity/scope, and floating tasks are omitted from its due-task filter. Merely preserving a group across dated tasks does not fix this handoff. Build an explicit preview of partial/deferred/unplaced work, retain the remaining scope and history through acceptance, and prove it appears once in the next cycle without duplicate minutes or diagnostics. Also verify observed pacing: `observedMinutesPerEffortUnit` still reads planned durations as if actual, which is not repaired by better logging alone. These remain open; full end-to-end first-pass/revision/mock completion is not claimed.

This is a proposal, not an implementation completion record. It covers student dispositions, historical snapshots and task handoffs, a server-owned preview, atomic acceptance, observed-time correction, cross-page connections and database/API/browser acceptance scenarios.

**Delivered for newly generated plans:**

A same-day paper whose original clock time has passed stays day-preferred rather than receiving a backdated clock time.

The app's initial confirmation path rejects an outdated ready preview with a recoverable conflict. Weekly proposals expire by local midnight or the existing three-hour ceiling, whichever is sooner. This does not certify every direct-RPC/cross-midnight path; database enforcement beyond existing expiry remains a future audit item.

### 29 October — Calendar → Curriculum → Tests activity connection

**Delivered:** a completed or partly completed Calendar sitting now offers its actual reading/outcome choices inside the task drawer. Saving completion keeps the drawer open. Reading-level work requires explicit coverage confirmation; a completed task already mapped to one outcome explains its automatic coverage instead. Unfinished split scope still requires confirmation. The existing revision-checked `confirm_task_scope` command remains the sole writer for these confirmations; this slice adds no competing coverage table or database migration.

- `GET /api/tests/scope/confirm?blockId=…` supplies the owned active-cycle task, current revision, canonical choices and saved subset. Its existing POST saves or clears that subset. Missing mappings are labelled rather than guessed. Calendar links to `/app/curriculum/[subject]/[reading]` and `/app/tests#scope-h`.
- Curriculum consumes the same owned task history and explicit confirmations as Tests. It shows recorded sittings, confirmed outcomes and unresolved current-week sittings. Study activity can show “Worked — awaiting retest”; it cannot create answers, confidence marks or a stronger evidence band. Confirmed history also feeds the shared signals used by Home and Coach after a week changes.
- Work timestamps come from execution events, not a task's edit time. Moving a completed task cannot make it look like new repair work or a new completion in the signals timeline. Missing historical timestamps remain unknown. Core activity, outcome metadata, split-group history, evidence-reducer inputs, candidate questions and response history now use paginated reads where changed here, rather than silently stopping at 1,000 rows. This is not a claim that every other reporting query has been converted.
- Paper preview and assembly share an eligible pool: unseen items in the allowed trust tier, active-manifest concepts, consistent stored concept/outcome/reading bindings, and outcomes actually in the reviewed blueprint. A shortage cannot borrow unconfirmed or null outcomes from the same reading. Breadth/variety selection stays within that boundary; tie/order shuffling uses Fisher–Yates. This is a structural sampling correction, not proof of psychometric fairness or semantic question quality.
- Evidence produced during the current cycle no longer retroactively turns that cycle's first exposure into revision. Calendar and Tests preserve the started/scored paper's own kind; Calendar no longer presents a fresh live-preview question count as if it described an existing frozen attempt.

**Additional live finding fixed:** the result proposed repairs on 30 October and a re-check on 2 November while claiming all three fit the cycle ending 1 November. Follow-up now displays its actual date span, and a re-check follows the **last** repair. Cross-cycle suggestions link to `/app/review-week`; `/api/diagnostic/repair` refuses to insert them into the closing cycle and performs a fresh cycle-boundary check before applying a same-cycle proposal. A lost/stale cycle decision offers the review route. These are application-level guards, not a certification of the legacy repair RPC's concurrency or direct-call permissions.

**Verification:** full `npm run verify`, production build and whitespace checks pass. The new learning-loop suite has **20 cases** covering explicit/partial/split/cancelled activity, timing, scope-safe selection, admissible bindings, history pagination, ceilings and repair-date boundaries. The hosted Auth/API suite has **19 passing scenarios**, including exact cross-page confirmation, retry deduplication, clearing, stale-edit rejection and unchanged reported minutes/evidence/confidence. It owns and removes its fixture account.

**Browser:** Chrome/Browser runtime at `http://localhost:3100`, desktop, using a dedicated verified fixture with a controlled active cycle and canonical FSA sitting. This fixture bypassed setup through test preparation; the earlier real onboarding journey is documented above. Recorded 35 minutes, finished the sitting, confirmed exactly three of five outcomes, verified all three remained Untested in Curriculum, cleared/restored coverage, reopened/completed the task, then started and submitted the five-question weekly paper with mandatory answer confidence. Its persisted items stayed within the three confirmed outcome IDs and the reviewed tier. The dummy result was 3/5, with five evidence rows, two uncovered outcomes still untested, and weakness links into weekly review. The unsafe cross-week POST returned 409 without creating tasks. The browser also reached the saved next-week preview; with no availability seeded in this fixture it honestly proposed an empty week and retained the open repair threads. **No next-week acceptance or repair execution is claimed for this fixture.** An initial new-reader schema mismatch was caught in the browser and fixed; no later relevant console error or framework overlay was observed. Mobile was not certified.

The separate `/api/tests/scope/quick-study` repeats the full duration for every outcome. Distinguish onboarding's claimed prior coverage from later subset activity instead of fabricating a repair of old rows.

The new date guard prevents the observed wrong-week insertion through the app; it does not finish this larger command.
