Check their semantic LOS alignment, answer quality and review provenance against the authorised curriculum before treating them as release-quality evidence. Structural tags and a numerical verifier do not establish that. No questions were silently remapped, promoted or fabricated here.

### 30 October — one sitting, exact time and shared learning capture

**Delivered:** the first next-work item above is implemented for new quick logs. Home, Calendar, Tests, `/app/log` and `/app/curriculum/[subject]/[reading]` share the same form. Students select the reading and the exact outcomes they covered; none are pre-checked, even when opened from a reading. They choose study, external practice or class, an optional note, a local date (today through 14 days ago), and reported minutes or **Not sure**. The reading picker is searchable. No upload/source feature was added.

**One command and one observation.** `POST /api/log-learning` validates the exact-outcome payload and calls `record_learning_sitting`. In one transaction it creates one completed activity, one linked Calendar task, exact outcome confirmations, one nullable time event and one receipt. Forty-five minutes covering three outcomes stays forty-five minutes, not three reports or rounded allocations. Unknown stays null. Identical concurrent retries replay the receipt; reusing its key for different answers is refused. `/api/tests/scope/quick-study` is a compatibility adapter to the same command, including cross-route retries. The old body without outcome IDs is refused with an explicit error. Student execution of the retired synthetic-duration writer and direct activity writes is revoked.

**Lost-response recovery.** The form freezes a pending request before sending. A user-scoped session-storage entry survives reload/navigation in that tab. If the response is lost after the save, the form offers **Retry same log**, rather than creating a new request. Known validation/access rejections can be corrected; uncertain results stay pending until checked. Confirmed success clears the pending entry and shows the saved sitting, exact count and time status. Receipt links close the log sheet before opening a Calendar drawer. Storage unavailable in the browser leaves an in-memory retry, rather than blocking logging.

**Connections and meanings**

- `GET /api/log-learning` supplies canonical active readings/outcomes, the account owner and profile-local today. The save response links the receipt to Calendar and the reading. `/app/tests#scope-h` uses the same shared log button.
- Calendar marks these as already-recorded activity. Their time labels use the actual report or **Time not reported**, with no invented planned duration. Extra sittings are separate from planned-session completion and planned minutes. Home execution, shared execution signals and review's unfinished-work list exclude those extra completed logs from their planned denominator.
- Exact confirmations enter the open cycle's eligible paper scope once. Historical logs outside that cycle still contribute activity/coverage, without entering this week's paper. A paper already started keeps its frozen scope. A new log never manufactures an answer, confidence mark, evidence event or a readiness increase.
- Curriculum shows partial exposure honestly. Only coverage of **all canonical outcomes** can derive full-reading `taught` or `studied` state for the tracker/roadmap. No whole-reading `user_module_state` row is written by the log command. A class is exposure, not independent preparation pace; class time is excluded from that pace. Unreported sittings cannot manufacture observed pace. Pace histories are paginated, and cancelled quick logs no longer poison a module's future pace.
- A dated log's learning timestamp conservatively uses that local day's start, not the moment it was typed. Logging older work now cannot falsely prove a repair took place after a test. Same-day date-only work is not proof of post-test repair timing.
- Recorded logs cannot be reopened, moved, resized, split, continued or given another time observation through normal task execution. Their exact coverage can still be corrected through the revision-checked scope command in the open cycle. **Remove learning log** uses `cancel_learning_sitting`: active time/coverage disappear, while original activity, reported time and audit history remain. Concurrent/stale cancellation and replay are handled in the command. This is a correction, not hard deletion.

**Database:** additive `20261030_learning_sittings.sql` is applied to `asylsxszdhlnzewhwtga`, the owner's confirmed disposable development project. Both environment files point there; there is no separate staging database. It adds the deferred activity link, receipts, owned RPCs and immutability/time guards. The migration runner reports **54 applied, zero pending**, with no applied-file edits. Auth-root fixture deletion exercises the real cascade relationship; no existing candidate data or historical learning rows were rewritten.

**Verification**

- `npm run verify`, `npm run build`, `npx tsc --noEmit` and `git diff --check` pass. The learning-loop suite now has **25 cases** (five new), including nullable exact time, explicit/full-reading derivation and compatibility request canonicalisation.
- **14 isolated Postgres scenarios** cover concurrency, retries, exact/unknown/boundary minutes, invalid scope/date, historical logging, verified/setup/ownership restrictions, direct-RPC guards, correction, full rollback on receipt failure and account deletion.
- **9 checks against the real development schema** pass inside a rolled-back rehearsal, including deferred foreign-key/account-root cascade behaviour. Nothing from that rehearsal is retained.
- **13 live Auth → Next → SQL learning-log scenarios** pass with a separately owned account, including cross-endpoint retries, invalid old payloads, partial/all-outcome derivation, class exposure, history, cancellation and no invented evidence. The preceding task/scope regression suite also passes **19 scenarios** after this migration. Both old learning-log E2E entry scripts now forward to controlled own-account harnesses instead of resetting a shared account.
- **Browser:** Chrome through the Browser runtime, desktop 1710×861, production build at `http://localhost:3101`. A temporary loopback proxy on 3102 deliberately discarded one successful save response. The UI retained the pending three-outcome/45-minute request through reload; retry produced exactly **one** sitting, receipt and time event. Followed its receipt into Calendar, saved another sitting with unknown time, removed the mistaken 45-minute log, and verified active totals became unknown while the one planned 45-minute task remained **0 of 1 done**.
- Continued through the reading-specific button: correct reading preselected, all outcomes initially unchecked; saved one further outcome with unknown time. Calendar opened one drawer without the log modal remaining on top. Tests showed exactly two new outcomes from that reading, excluded the untouched planned task and kept all understanding/confidence unassessed. Home showed the same two-outcome exposure with **0/1 planned tasks**. Home and Tests opened the shared log form. The final production build was reloaded and the corrected form wording inspected. No relevant console warning/error or framework overlay was observed in the final checks; the deliberately dropped response was the expected fault injection, and a temporary forwarding-unavailable page during the controlled build restart cleared on reload.

**Limits and next work:** this controlled browser fixture had setup pre-seeded and no roadmap/availability; it does not certify onboarding or new first-week generation. Mobile was not certified. No new paper was started: the fixture's confirmed outcomes had zero reviewed questions, and the existing UI correctly refused to start. A related pre-existing inconsistency remains: the small-scope preview says three questions while the start guard requires five; reading/subject Start buttons also need consistent preflight treatment. Resolve this in the paper/repair acceptance slice without widening beyond confirmed scope. Scheduled-class occurrence follow-up still uses `/app/calendar/log-class`; the quick class log does not rebuild its preview/acceptance scheduling. Partial-reading roadmap effort/prerequisite allocation remains conservative. Old logged totals, prior claimed coverage and semantic question quality were not backfilled or certified. Domain, external OAuth and email setup remain deferred.

Next: **one complete repair acceptance/re-check command**, joining same-week proposals, next-week review, capacity, provenance, thread identity and idempotent acceptance; then the usable-question quality review. The legacy direct repair RPC is not yet certified by this logging work. Temporary Browser/API accounts and their owned records are removed after QA, and private sign-in files/proxy are cleaned up. Screenshots outside the repository: `/tmp/roadmap-shared-learning-log.png`, `/tmp/roadmap-log-final-calendar.png`.

## 6. Repair loop — status at 31 October 2026

**Release criterion:** one valid diagnostic → one accepted, traceable repair plan → one correctly scoped re-check, with safe retries and week transitions.

### Applied
`supabase/migrations/20261031_repair_loop.sql` applied through the checksum runner to the development project (55 migrations, 0 pending afterwards).

### R0 findings fixed
Students could write evidence, challenges, threads, queue, proposals and responses directly (privileges revoked; `assessment_attempts` keeps INSERT only). The legacy `apply_diagnostic_repair_proposal` trusted browser task rows (execute revoked, route returns 410). Threads were counted per wrong answer (now one per concept per paper). An account-wide repair paper ignored what was actually being repaired (removed; a re-check starts only from its Calendar task). Finishing repair work could not move a thread (trigger now does: open → scheduled → in_progress → awaiting_recheck). Challenge void cancelled too much (now only unstarted work, only when no other valid evidence remains). Account deletion was blocked by thread FKs (fixed).

### Contracts
- Preview: `POST /api/diagnostic/repair/preview` saves a server-owned `coach_proposals` row (origin `diagnostic`, 2 h expiry, `source_version`).
- Accept: `POST /api/diagnostic/repair/accept` takes proposal id + command key only; locks, then compares the token; retry returns the stored receipt.
- Place re-check: `POST /api/diagnostic/repair/check` (`place_repair_check`), only once repairs are done and ≥3 days have passed, inside the open week.
- Start re-check: `POST /api/tests/start` with `kind:'repair'` + `checkTaskId`; `POST /api/tests/preflight` for re-checks.
- Re-check paper: concepts of the linked threads only, normally 3 questions per concept (≤90), minimum 5 questions; the 31 October follow-up requests 5 for a single concept so that scope can meet the minimum if enough eligible questions exist. Never borrow unrelated concepts or already-seen items. Existing attempts are resumable.

### Proven
`npm run verify` exit 0; isolated Postgres suites repair-loop 35, weekly-continuity 22, foundation 22; planner 11; real-schema rehearsal 14 checks before and after applying (always rolled back); live taking flow 64/0 against the new privileges; isolated production build passes.

### Open policy decision (not coded)
`repair_resolution_policy()` returns `{version:'unset', autoClose:false}`. Nothing closes a thread automatically and there is no "mark repaired". **Decision needed:** how many valid questions on a concept, with what correct share, resolve a gap? Smallest proposal, `repair-resolution-1`: a concept check is *supported* at ≥3 valid questions all correct, *still needs work* on any miss, *uncertain* below 3 valid; a thread closes only after one *supported* check and no newer valid miss. Enabling it is a new migration with a new version string.

### Known limits / not done
- Real bank depth is ≤5 questions per concept, so a re-check covering many concepts is thin.
- The weekly generator does not emit waiting re-checks; they are placed on demand ("Add the re-check to my calendar").
- Coach repair integration and route guards were unfinished at this checkpoint; see the later 31 October follow-up below for the implemented correction.
- Preflight covers re-checks only.
- No displacement of already-accepted tasks (a repair that does not fit waits).
- `learning-loop-test.ts` still uses `repairDates.ts`.
- Challenge submit/review flows are DB-tested only; `assessment_attempts` INSERT is still open to students.
- Browser journeys (desktop and mobile viewport) and Auth→API own-account tests were NOT run in this slice.

### 31 October follow-up — Coach integration and real student-path verification

**Changed:** the Coach no longer generates or applies its own `recheck_block` tasks. It resolves an owned active repair's valid source evidence and links to `/app/tests/[attemptId]/result#proposal-h`, reusing the existing follow-up preview/acceptance UI. Old pending Coach repair cards become stale and offer the current follow-up, without creating a task or claiming execution. The separate bad-day deferral feature was retained; its multi-task transaction/receipt design is **not** certified by this repair change.

- `/api/coach` POST, `/api/coach/history` GET/DELETE and `/api/coach/proposals/[id]` POST now use `requireApplicationAccess`, including verified email and completed setup. Unit access tests cover the shared gate; the new live suite checks every touched route signed out and with incomplete setup.
- Clearing Coach history rejects pending **Coach-origin** proposals only. It no longer rejects a diagnostic repair draft created elsewhere in the product. Proposal-decision controls show a pending state, catch lost responses and offer the recovery link supplied by the server. The Coach continues to guide rather than teach, and no model fine-tuning is claimed.
- A single-concept re-check previously requested at most 3 questions but required at least 5, so it could never start even with a sufficient eligible bank. It now requests 5 from that same concept. Two or more concepts retain a target of 3 each; shortages remain explicit and scope is never widened. This does not enable the proposed three-correct resolution policy.
- Re-check preflight and start share an earliest-day guard using the planned Calendar day, the thread's saved waiting date and **three local days after the latest recorded repair completion**. Delayed completion or moving a check earlier cannot bypass that application-level spacing. Missing completion history is reported as waiting; it is not inferred. Existing in-progress attempts still resume before fresh-start eligibility is evaluated. The start API retains its existing `no_scope` refusal code with the specific waiting explanation.
- Corrected waiting copy: later checks are still added explicitly from follow-up; the app no longer promises the weekly generator will automatically place them. Actual future Calendar retiming and automatic waiting-check generation remain work.

**Verification:** `npm run verify`, isolated production build (`NEXT_DIST_DIR=.next-repair-coach`), typecheck and whitespace checks pass. Planner/repair policy suite: **13 cases**; Coach action suite: **22 cases**. The checksum runner's fresh development dry-run succeeded with **0 pending migrations**. This follow-up adds no SQL migration and does not change the existing 55-file ledger. The earlier isolated database suites were not rerun merely for route/UI/application-level changes.

`scripts/repair-loop-http-test.cjs` / `npm run repair:http:test` is an own-account harness with explicit development-target and loopback-URL checks, no shared-account reset and no bank writes. Its **11 scenarios** cover route access, one episode per concept on repeated submit, exact Coach result linkage, retired cards, read-only state, preview provenance, unrelated-history clearing, concurrent acceptance, completion without invented time/evidence, and refusal of a future or prematurely moved re-check. It removes its Auth identity, owned rows and otherwise orphaned fixture forms/blueprints. Initial fixture attempts exposed missing legacy required `day`/`detail` fields; those were fixed in the harness, not by weakening the real schema. Fixtures now use the **reviewed** tier only; a preliminary verifier-passed fixture was removed rather than promoted or used as final evidence of ordinary paper generation.

**Browser:** Chrome via the Browser runtime, desktop 1710×861, isolated production server at `http://localhost:3103`. A dedicated verified/setup-preseeded candidate had a controlled two-question, two-concept scored fixture; this is not a normal weekly-paper generation or onboarding test. Followed Coach → exact result follow-up → saved preview showing two 30-minute Saturday repairs and a check waiting until Tuesday 3 November → Confirm changes → exact Calendar repair links. Both repairs were completed with minutes left unknown. Calendar showed 2/2, the result showed both awaiting re-check, and attempting to place the future check returned a clear next-week explanation without inserting it. Independent persistence assertions confirmed two completed repairs, two awaiting-check threads, one original evidence event per concept, no new check task and no invented minutes. No unexpected browser console warning/error or framework overlay was observed. Final waiting-state screenshot: `/tmp/roadmap-repair-awaiting-check.png`. Mobile remains unverified. No re-check was taken or thread automatically closed in this journey.

**Ponytail:** installed the requested `DietrichGebert/ponytail` text skill into `/Users/advay/.codex/skills/ponytail` using the skill installer; linked shared-agent and Antigravity skill locations and preserved the existing Claude skill. Roadmap's `AGENTS.md` enables full mode, and `CLAUDE.md` imports that file. The machine-local `.agent/skills/ponytail` link is ignored by Git. No third-party hooks, telemetry process or runtime was installed. This chat adopted the skill immediately; it is discoverable on the next Codex turn. Only this Codex chat/root agent was active in the inventory. Already-running external Claude/Antigravity sessions must load the updated instruction; hot reload of their model context was not verified.

**Still open:** automatic repair resolution requires the explicit policy decision above; automatic next-week waiting-check placement; ordinary reading/subject/weekly preflight parity; end-to-end challenge review; bank depth and semantic question review; student `assessment_attempts` INSERT; and complete bad-day Coach transaction/retry safety. The new spacing guard is in the shared server application path, **not** a new database constraint: transaction-level enforcement against concurrent dependency edits and placement-command retiming still require an additive migration/audit. No production-readiness, model-training or full mock/revision claim is made. Domain/OAuth/email remain deferred, existing learner records remain unchanged, and nothing was committed or pushed.

### 31 October — proposed completion sequence for candidates joining at any stage

The founder requires midway entry, full-first-pass/near-mock entry and the complete revision-to-exam journey, rather than a universal beginner baseline week. See [Adaptive entry to exam completion](ADAPTIVE-ENTRY-TO-EXAM-COMPLETION-PLAN.md) for the proposed implementation phases, backend/content tracks, exact route connections and 24-scenario acceptance matrix. This is a plan, not implemented capability.

Source inspection and four read-only pure-planner probes confirmed the current gap: short-horizon `skipFirstPass` still returns compressed/final, revision allocation emits generic `study` tasks, and final daily allocation without repairs emits no study/revision/mock portions. Current onboarding captures only broad stage/topic recency. The bank audit also conflates verified and reviewed inventory. No learner/database writes or Browser journey were performed for this planning task. The founder direction register now distinguishes the candidate's first week in Roadmap from their actual preparation stage.

## 9. Baseline completion and write boundary (1 Nov 2026)

**Fixtures** (`npm`-less: `npx tsx scripts/entry-scenarios-baseline.ts`, pure planner, 2026-11-02, 600 min/week). Actual today:
1. Fresh, 9 months: first_pass 20w / consolidation 4w / revision 7w / final 8w, 16 milestones. PASS.
2. Three subjects `studied`: **identical** to fresh (first_pass 20w, 16 milestones): covered subjects are not removed from the first pass. FAIL. No input exists for reported uncertainty. FAIL.
3. First pass complete (all `studied`), exam ~16 weeks: **first_pass 5w still scheduled** (only class-led `classesDone` skips it). FAIL. No mock checkpoint input. FAIL.
These are the Phase B regression targets. Calibration-unavailable behaviour is not exercised here (no bank needed for the planner).

**Ownership map:** `docs/ROUTE-OWNERSHIP.md`, generated by `npm run routes:inventory` (55 files / 67 handlers at generation; do not treat counts as fixed). Access classes: 18 verified + onboarding-complete, 5 owned-attempt, 3 reviewer, **27 signed-in only**, 2 public (`/auth/*`). "Signed-in only" is a heuristic from source text: several of those may enforce verification or onboarding inside the database command (e.g. `set_task_execution` refuses incomplete setup), which was NOT checked route by route. Unverified: `tasks`, `commitments`, `class-log/*`, `curriculum/*` writes, `diagnostic/attempt*`, `roadmap`, `signals`.

**Write boundary (`20261101_write_boundary.sql`, applied through the runner):** revoked insert/update/delete/truncate from students on `plan_blocks`, `plan_drafts`, `planning_cycles`, `task_events`; on `profiles` revoked all writes and granted column UPDATE only on `display_name, max_session_minutes, prep_mode, classes_completed_at, updated_at`, so `onboarding_status` and the other setup fields are server-only. Callers traced beforehand: every write to those tables is by the service-role client or a SECURITY DEFINER command; the only signed-in writes were the two profile routes. Proof as a real `authenticated` role (`npm run boundary:rehearse`, rolled back): 10 checks before applying (direct writes refused, cross-account refused, onboarding_status protected, Settings edit works, `set_task_execution` still works).
Still open: `exam_attempts`, `commitments`, `class_sessions`, `curriculum_*` marks/notes and `assessment_attempts` INSERT remain student-writable (not traced in this slice).

### 9.1 Boundary verification (1 Nov 2026)
- **Deployed permissions: PASS.** `boundary:rehearse --already-applied` 10/10 as a real `authenticated` role; column UPDATE on `profiles` is exactly `display_name, max_session_minutes, prep_mode, classes_completed_at, updated_at`; students/anon hold only SELECT/TRIGGER/REFERENCES on the five protected tables.
- **Earlier failures explained:** editing = expired magic link + 401 (authentication, caused by concurrent runs on the shared e2e account); onboarding = dirty profile from the same interference (alone: 84/0); calendar timeout = inconclusive.
- **Sequential live runs (separate processes, one at a time, isolated server):** onboarding 84/0, editing 40/0 (move/split/undo/history work under the boundary), auth 22/0, calendar 20/6, weekly-review 31/18, integration 38/6. The 30 failures are stale pre-change contracts, not permissions: calendar/integration still call the retired `POST /api/diagnostic/repair` (410 `RETIRED`); weekly-review/integration post the old accept body, but the route now requires a saved `draftId` (400 "Invalid request."). Their fixtures write through the service role, so the boundary cannot be the cause. **No current-contract live test exists for weekly accept or repair accept; those rest on the isolated-Postgres suites.**
- **NEW HIGH: `public.schema_migrations`** had RLS off and full privileges (incl. DELETE/TRUNCATE) for `anon` and `authenticated`. Fix written as `20261102_lock_migration_ledger.sql` — **NOT APPLIED** (apply was denied; needs the owner to run `npm run migrate -- --env=staging --apply --confirm-target=asylsxszdhlnzewhwtga`).
- **Remaining student-writable tables (RLS own-row, grants blanket):** no signed-in writer found in code → revoke candidates: `coach_messages, study_roadmaps, roadmap_milestones, user_module_state`; unresolved/unsafe: `assessment_attempts` INSERT (client-chosen status), `evidence_overrides`, `exam_attempts` (any column, bypasses replanning/stale-draft), `calendar_commitments`/`commitment_exceptions` (bypass overlap validation); intended self-report (must stay separate from evidence): `confidence_marks, curriculum_notes, flashcards`. Reference tables have RLS and no write policy (safe). Not classified: the rest.
- **Not done:** browser walkthrough (desktop + mobile), real-endpoint cross-account test, test-data cleanup audit of the shared e2e account.

### 9.2 Ledger lock, attempt-insert revoke and replacement repair journey (1 Nov 2026)
- **`20261102_lock_migration_ledger` applied** (RLS on; ACL now only `postgres` + `service_role`; no PUBLIC entry; no function references the ledger; runner bootstrap is `create table if not exists`, so it cannot reintroduce grants). Verified: 57 ledger rows intact, dry-run 0 pending / no drift, anon `GET`/`PATCH` on `/rest/v1/schema_migrations` → 401 `permission denied`. (A `service_role` key still reaches it; that key is server-only.) The first apply attempt was refused by the auto-approval classifier with only the label "Modify Shared Resources"; no fuller reason was available, and the same reviewed command was re-run once after the owner's go-ahead.
- **`20261102_revoke_attempt_insert` applied:** students can no longer create `assessment_attempts` (client-chosen status/form/paper kind). Both session-client inserts (`startAssessment`, legacy `/api/diagnostic/attempt`) now use the service role. Rehearsal 11/11 (check failed before, passes after); live taking flow 64/0 afterwards.
- **Calendar e2e rewritten for the replacement flow: 30/0.** Result → read state → retired endpoint 410 → saved preview (writes nothing) → accept → thread-linked tasks → same-key retry replays, creates nothing → check refused while repairs unfinished → complete via `/api/tasks` → threads `awaiting_recheck` (not closed). Note: with the account's cycle already over, the planner correctly returned "no room this week", places nothing; the test now widens the open cycle to exercise placement.
- **Still unresolved writable surfaces:** `evidence_overrides` (student-set band consumed by the LOS tracker; self-assertion, must not count as demonstrated), `exam_attempts` (any column), `calendar_commitments`/`commitment_exceptions` (bypass overlap validation), `study_roadmaps`/`roadmap_milestones`/`user_module_state`/`coach_messages` (no signed-in writer found, not yet revoked). `prep_mode`/`classes_completed_at` remain student-writable by design but change no accepted plan or stale-draft token.
- **Not done:** desktop/mobile browser walkthrough; cross-account and incomplete-onboarding checks through real endpoints; weekly-review/integration e2e still on the old accept body (18 + 6 stale failures) — no current-contract live test for weekly accept. Phase B stays paused on these.

### 9.3 Current-contract verification and state ownership (1 Nov 2026)
- **Weekly transition, live, current contract: `e2e-weekly-review.ts` 29/0** (answers → saved draft → accept draft): preview writes nothing and leaves originals untouched; stale draft → 409 with answers kept in `weekly_transition_drafts`; same-draft retry returns the original outcome with exactly three continuations; originals keep cycle/mapping/status/20 minutes; successors start clean; 20 + 25 = 45 attributed once per week. The empty-next-week path is only covered by the isolated database suite (the live clock window did not allow closing the second cycle).
- **Access, real endpoints: `e2e-access-matrix.ts` 40/0.** Anonymous → 401; incomplete onboarding → 403 `ONBOARDING_REQUIRED` on 13 app routes while onboarding state stays reachable and a crafted payload cannot complete setup; a second verified user cannot mutate or read the owner’s task, week, repair proposal or attempt; a non-reviewer gets 403 on item review. **Fixed:** 18 routes plus `withOwnedAttempt` and both profile routes only checked “signed in”; they now use `requireApplicationAccess` (verified email + completed onboarding). Not tested live: an unverified-email user (the magic-link sign-in confirms the address, so the case needs a different fixture). The `+e2e` account holds a `diagnostic_reviewer` role (a test-environment fact).
- **State ownership (`20261103_state_ownership`, applied):** students lost writes on `exam_attempts`, `study_roadmaps`, `roadmap_milestones`, `user_module_state`, `coach_messages`, `calendar_commitments`, `commitment_exceptions` and all `profiles` updates after every writer was traced; the validated routes (`/api/profile`, `/api/commitments*`, classes-completed) now write through the service role after the access check and an ownership check. `/api/profile` also had a latent bug (its `prepMode` enum did not match the database, so any non-empty value failed; it also cleared omitted fields) — fixed. `weekly_source_version` now also covers commitment exceptions, the active exam target and `classes_completed_at`. Rehearsal 20/20 as a real `authenticated` role; the exam-target token reaction is not exercised because no template `exam_attempts` row exists to clone.
- **`evidence_overrides`:** display-only self-report (the LOS tracker shows it; nothing in scoring, evidence, repair or planning reads it). Rehearsal proves a student override changes no evidence and closes no repair thread. Left student-writable by design.
- **Repair re-check:** retired endpoint 410, preview → accept → linked tasks → retry replay → refused while unfinished → completion → `awaiting_recheck` all pass live (calendar e2e 30/0). The eligible re-check path (placement after spacing, scoped start, insufficient-bank state, no duplicates) is proven by the isolated database suite (35 scenarios), **not through live endpoints**: the live bank has 19 eligible reviewed items, all FSA.
- **Browser (Playwright, desktop 1280×800 and mobile 390×844, disposable accounts, deleted afterwards):** incomplete account redirected into onboarding from `/app/calendar`; onboarded account → Calendar → task drawer → “Partly done” with 20 minutes persisted (partial, revision 2, 20 min); Review week → answers → preview (both continuations “Not placed: no room”, a fixture without availability windows) → save → Calendar notice; original task shows “Continued as … week of 2 Nov” and the link opens the successor (“Continued from …, 20 min reported, stays with that week”); mobile Home, next-week agenda and drawer: no horizontal overflow, drawer fits, Escape closes. Console: no errors except a missing `favicon.ico` (404). **Not browser-tested:** completing onboarding by clicking through it, the repair follow-up screens, Settings edit persistence, keyboard tab order.
- **Stale script still open:** `e2e-integration.ts` (6 failures) still posts the retired apply endpoint / old accept body.
- **Intermittent:** one taking-flow run failed “a scored attempt accepts no more answers”; two reruns passed 64/0; cause not established (detail is now printed on failure).
- **Totals this slice:** onboarding 84/0, calendar 30/0, editing 40/0, weekly 29/0, access 40/0, taking flow 64/0, auth 22/0, `npm run verify` exit 0, dry-run 0 pending. Applied this slice: `write_boundary`, `lock_migration_ledger`, `revoke_attempt_insert`, `state_ownership`.

### 9.4 Final continuity and access pass (1 Nov 2026) — completes §9.3
- **Scored-attempt intermittency — cause and invariant.** Invariant: after finalisation, answer writes are refused and stored responses do not change. `scripts/attempt-finalization-race.cjs` (`npm run attempt:race`, two real connections, committed owned fixtures, removed afterwards) proves it on the real schema in both orders: a finalising transaction makes a concurrent answer wait, then be refused (“Attempt is scored”) with responses unchanged; an in-flight answer makes finalisation wait and the attempt is scored once from the answers as they stood after that lock; a later answer writes nothing (8/8). Both `finalize_assessment_attempt` and `save_attempt_response` take `for update` on the attempt row and check status after the lock, so the database cannot allow it. The one failing run’s response body was not captured, so its exact cause is **not proven**; the only path that can return something other than 409 “closed” without violating the invariant is the new shared access check answering 503 when it cannot reach the database (`ACCESS_UNAVAILABLE`). The taking-flow assertion is now two-sided (refusal AND unchanged responses by hash), prints the response on failure, and treats a 503 as inconclusive (one repeat). No failure since (65/0).
- **Roles.** The `+e2e` account and the owner’s primary account hold `diagnostic_reviewer` (granted 18 Sep and 17 Sep, `granted_by` null, i.e. by hand, not by any script in this repository). They were not removed (cannot establish they are accidental; not test-created). Ordinary-student tests no longer use that account: `createStudent` / the access matrix create fresh students, assert they hold no `user_roles` row, and a reviewer fixture is created and deleted by the test.
- **Access matrix 51/0** (real endpoints; owner, other student, incomplete onboarding, unverified email, reviewer, anonymous). Unverified: 403 `EMAIL_VERIFICATION_REQUIRED` on 8 app routes with no rows written; public pages still work. Reviewer: ordinary student 403, reviewer passes the role gate (rejected only for an invalid decision, nothing approved).
- **Integration 43/0** (`e2e-integration.ts`, current contracts, ordinary students): A) learning → paper → follow-up → preview (writes nothing) → accept → thread-linked tasks → retry replay → weekly draft → accept → one active successor cycle, continuations keep repair links, historical minutes untouched and not copied; retired endpoint 410. B) exam target changed through the authorised route (onboarding exam step; `/api/profile` examDate only updates a legacy `exam_attempts` row that onboarded students do not have and now says so) → earlier draft refused as stale, nothing partly committed, answers still stored → same answers re-prepared and accepted: an empty next week opens as an active cycle. C) re-check: unfinished refused; spacing not reached stays waiting with nothing placed; eligible placed once, linked to exactly the finished repairs; started on the intended concepts only; resumed, no duplicate attempt; threads stay open. History was dated by moving completion events five days back; the spacing rule itself was not touched. Live bank depth was sufficient for the FSA concepts used; other subjects have none.
- **Changed-route review.** Commitment routes now also validate date format, integer weekdays and start/end order (merged with stored times) before the service-role write; user id always comes from the session; `/api/profile` writes only supplied fields; both assessment-creation paths run after the access gate with server-built forms.
- **Browser (Playwright; ordinary students; desktop 1280×800 and mobile 390×844):** onboarding clicked through all five steps at 390px with a reload resuming step 4 → Calendar; result → Review follow-up → preview (4 repairs, day selects, re-check Thu 5 Nov) → Confirm → “5 items added” → Calendar shows the 4 repairs and the re-check, whose drawer says “Finish the repair work first” when started; Settings → Your exam → Change → saved date persisted (checked in the database) and route, position and week were not cleared; keyboard: logo → nav in order with visible outlines, task opens a modal drawer with focus on Close, Escape closes it and returns focus to the task button; weekly continuity both directions (§9.3). **Fixed:** result page overflowed to 442px on mobile (grid children widening the page); static header “Nothing is added… until you confirm” contradicted the confirmed state; “See existing repair” wording; added `src/app/icon.svg` (brand green) so the tab icon no longer 404s. **Not browser-tested:** onboarding clicked through on desktop (only the redirect), Settings edits other than the exam step.
- **Applied/ledger:** no new migration in this pass; dry-run 0 pending; `npm run verify` exit 0. Fixtures: all disposable students deleted; the shared `+e2e` account still exists.
- **Remaining limits:** the unexplained single failure above; keyboard checks sampled primary controls, not every control; the re-check journey covers only subjects with reviewed items (FSA); `e2e-learning-log` and `e2e-tests-scope` need extra flags and were not run; the reviewer role on the `+e2e` account is the owner’s decision.

### 9.5 Phase B — adaptive entry and the first useful week (1 Nov 2026)
- **Contract.** Coverage stays in `user_module_state` (source `self_report`, per reading; a topic claim expands to that topic's readings as *planning scope*, never concept evidence; partly covered = `taught`, covered once = `studied`). New: `user_entry_uncertainty` (per topic: `none`/`few`/`several`/`most`, optional narrowed readings; **no row = not answered, `none` = explicitly nothing shaky**), `profiles.mock_date/mock_kind` (an optional checkpoint, never a second exam date), `profiles.entry_reported_at`. Pure model: `src/lib/curriculum/entryContext.ts` (+ `entryContextService.ts` loader, `entryToken` change token). Writes are server-owned (`savePosition` → `onboarding_replace_position`, service role only; students can read their own uncertainty and write nothing). A save that omits uncertainty/mock/readings leaves them unchanged, so an old client cannot erase them.
- **Planner.** `buildRoadmap`: first-pass weeks scale with first-pass work actually left (same pace as a fresh candidate; zero when nothing is left); covered subjects get no first-pass milestones; consolidation/revision ordering rises with reported uncertainty and, when a mock exists, exam weight; final stretch now has focus weeks (only when the starting position is known) so a prepared student with no repairs does not meet an empty Calendar; `checkpoints`, `assumptions` (partly covered ≈ 60% left; unnarrowed uncertainty starts at a topic's first readings) and calm `warnings` (mock passed / after exam / within two weeks) are returned and shown on the Curriculum roadmap. A short horizon with nothing left to learn is `tight` with prioritisation options, never "not enough time". `buildWeekPlan`: covered/decaying readings become `review` tasks ("Revise: …", lane `consolidation`, capped per phase, self-reported provenance in the rationale, objective/approach/what-to-log guidance), targeted revision of flagged covered readings is offered during the first pass within half a day; revised readings feed the (revisionary) weekly paper scope exactly as other revisited work. Revision is not a repair thread and has no assessment scope.
- **Migrations applied (checksum runner, confirmed disposable project; ledger now one entry longer ×2):** `20261104_entry_context` (rehearsed 25/25 in a rolled-back transaction: `npm run entry:rehearse`) and `20261104_onboarding_accept_guidance` (the first accepted week now carries objective/approach/completion guidance; verbatim copy of the prior function plus three columns). Dry-run: 0 pending.
- **Onboarding.** Position step: four-tile fast route (fresh / some subjects covered / first pass complete — bulk "all covered once" with exceptions — retaking or back after a break); per-topic cards with coverage chips, "how much feels uncertain" chips (nothing is preselected), optional per-reading refinement and "Revisit" picks, optional mock date + type, the plain "Covered means…" sentence. Saved progress, Back and edit-from-Settings keep answers; Settings edits show that the accepted week is not rewritten. Old stage value `most_of_first_pass` still loads/saves.
- **Evidence integrity.** Proved at database level (rehearsal), at API level (`e2e-entry.ts`) and in the browser: claims create no tasks completed, minutes, task events, evidence, answers, attempts, repair threads or proposals; previews write nothing; stale previews (edit after preview) are refused and create nothing; an edit after acceptance leaves the accepted week byte-identical while the weekly change token moves (an unaccepted weekly draft goes stale; code path covered by `e2e-weekly-review`). Roadmap progress no longer says "ahead of the plan" in week one; the ring/label now say "Covered" and that reported coverage is an estimate.
- **Before → after (pure planner, same inputs; before = the captured record `docs/evidence/ENTRY-SCENARIOS-BEFORE-PHASE-B.md` (the script that produced it was removed in Phase C because it would now run today's planner), after = `scripts/entry-scenarios-after.ts`):** (1) fresh, nine months: first pass 20w, consolidation 4, revision 7, final 8 → unchanged. (2) three subjects covered: *before* identical to fresh (first pass 20w, 16 milestones, uncertainty inexpressible) → *after* first pass 10w limited to FSA/Equity/Fixed Income, consolidation 9, revision 12, flagged topics first, first week mixes `Revise` (flagged readings) with `Study` (unfinished topics). (3) first pass complete, mock in a month, exam ~16w: *before* first pass 5w + 9 milestones, mock inexpressible → *after* no first pass; consolidation 3, revision 5, final 8; mock checkpoint in week 5; revision-only first week led by the flagged topic.
- **Verified.** `entry:test` 48/0 (pure, fixed dates; scenarios 1–6 and the short horizon), `e2e:entry` 46/0 (live API, ordinary throwaway students: partway, first-pass-complete + mock, stale preview, edit after acceptance, date/shape validation, existing user without the new fields, no-bank topic), plus onboarding 84/0, weekly-review 29/0, integration 43/0, calendar 30/0 (21/0 when run straight after another suite, see limits), `npm run verify` exit 0. Browser (Playwright, throwaway students, deleted): midway onboarding on desktop → reload keeps every answer → review → accept → Calendar → revision task drawer (purpose, why, approach, what-to-log) → Curriculum roadmap ("What this plan is based on"); first-pass-complete + nearby mock onboarding at 390px (no horizontal overflow) → accept → roadmap with the mock checkpoint; existing user edits uncertainty from Settings and it persists; keyboard toggle on a chip works.
- **Limits.** The mock appears as a checkpoint/priorities, not as a Calendar entry, and a mock week still gets the ordinary Sunday paper. No maintenance revision when a first-pass student reports nothing uncertain. Roadmap progress after week one still counts reported coverage as "covered" effort (labelled as an estimate); pace adaptation is untouched. Revision sizing is 40% of reading effort capped at 60 minutes (a planning assumption). Diagnostic availability beyond FSA is unchanged: those papers say "We can plan this area now. Diagnostic coverage is not available yet". A claim re-save still rewrites the student's own `self_report` taught/studied rows, as before (logged study is re-derived from completed blocks). Desktop browser coverage of the first-pass-complete path and mobile coverage of the Settings edit were not run separately (API covers them). `e2e-calendar` placement chain depends on the shared account's state left by the previous suite (30/0 after `e2e-onboarding`, 21/0 with the honest "no room" skip otherwise) and on weekday: planning from a Sunday evening leaves no self-study day, so live tests planned in `Pacific/Auckland` — removed in Phase C (§9.6): a real Sunday is now a supported case.



### 9.6 Phase C — reliable Calendar entry and connected mock checkpoints (1 Nov 2026)
**First useful week (one shared decision).** `src/lib/planning/pulse/firstCycle.ts` (`decideInitialCycle`, pure) is called from `getStudyWeekContext` (`schedule.ts`), so onboarding preview, activation recovery, Calendar, Home, roadmap start, Coach and the weekly tools cannot disagree. A student with **no cycle ever** starts in the current cycle while it has a study day with real free time; otherwise (self-study Sunday evening, a class-led cycle on its last day with its window gone, evening after the last window) the first plan is the **next** cycle, advanced **at most once** (no hunting for capacity: with no windows at all the student sees unplaced work and an "add availability" note). Dates come from the student's own timezone and the instant of the request. The review shows the reason before acceptance (`initialCycle.note` on `/api/onboarding/plan/preview`, shown in `PlanStep`). Acceptance stays the atomic `accept_onboarding_preview`; `confirmPreview` now also refuses (STALE_PREVIEW) a preview whose cycle no longer matches what the shared decision returns. An accepted cycle that starts after today's (accepted on a Sunday evening) is returned as the student's current cycle, so Calendar opens on it at once. An existing student's accepted cycle, even an empty one, is never touched (the decision only runs when `planning_cycles` has no row). Class-led boundaries still come from `resolvePulseWeek`. `permitsStudyPortions` moved to `weekBoundary.ts` (re-exported from `generateWeek.ts`) to avoid an import cycle.
**One checkpoint.** The mock is `profiles.mock_date/mock_kind` (Phase B) plus new `mock_start_time`, `mock_duration_minutes`, `mock_label` (all optional; a date alone is complete; clearing the date clears the timing by trigger). No second copy anywhere: onboarding Position step, Settings (edits via the same step), Calendar panel and `/api/checkpoints` all write the same columns; the roadmap `checkpoints[]` and Home/Coach read them. A mock with start time **and** length reserves exactly that interval in `dailyCapacityMinutes`; a date-only mock reserves nothing. Preparation (2×45 min before) and a post-mock check-in (45 min) are **proposals** computed by `projectCheckpoint` (pure) around the student's windows, timed tasks and commitments; each is added only by the student ("Add to my week" → existing `POST /api/tasks`), conflicts and unplaced items are listed with reasons.
**Assessment choice.** `cycle_assessment_choices(user_id, week_start, choice)` = `use_mock | keep_paper | later`; no row = undecided (shown as unresolved on Home and Calendar). `use_mock` makes the **planner** skip that week's weekly paper (`reason: mock_chosen`); it never cancels an existing, started or finished paper (Calendar says so and leaves removal to the task's own menu). Moving or cancelling the mock clears the old week's choice.
**External mock outcome.** `mock_outcomes` (taken / postponed / not_taken, optional typed result, notes; unique per planned date so retries replay). It has no score, response, concept or readiness column, writes no evidence, attempts, repair threads or proposals (asserted). Weekly review gains gate `external_mock` (outcome recorded inside the cycle, no open weekly attempt; a mock postponed to another day of the same cycle does not close it): closure uses the existing `no_paper` closure with reason `External mock (self-reported): …` (no change to `accept_weekly_transition`), unfinished activity must still be answered, and the next-week draft is built as usual. Cancel/postpone responses include `replacementOffer` (is a paper startable from completed work? otherwise says so honestly and points to no-paper closure).
**Invalidation.** Every checkpoint/choice/outcome write bumps `onboarding_context_version` (unaccepted onboarding previews → STALE_PREVIEW) and the weekly change token covers mock fields, choices and outcomes (unaccepted weekly drafts → stale). Accepted study work is never changed by any of it.
**Route map (destination · identifier).**
| Surface | Link / data | Destination · identifier |
|---|---|---|
| Onboarding review | cycle dates + note | `/api/onboarding/plan/preview` → `cycle{start,end}`, `initialCycle`; accept → `planning_cycles.starts_at_local` equals the preview's |
| `/app` Home | "Coming up" card | `/app/calendar?week=<week-start>#checkpoint` (`CheckpointView.href`) |
| `/app/calendar` | panel, grid chip | `#checkpoint` (same record); chip anchors to it; suggestions → `POST /api/tasks` |
| Curriculum roadmap | checkpoint line | `/app/calendar?week=<mock date>#checkpoint` |
| `/app/tests` | revisionary/mixed scope | `GET /api/tests/scope`; unchanged bank rules |
| `/app/review-week` | external outcome / link to record it | gate `external_mock`; link `/app/calendar#checkpoint` or the checkpoint's own `href` |
| `/app/coach` | context line + link | checkpoint `href`; Coach edits nothing |
| Settings | "Where you are" | `/onboarding/position?returnTo=/app/settings` (edits `profiles.mock_*`) |
| API | `/api/checkpoints` | GET view · PUT save/move · DELETE cancel · POST `{action:'choice'|'outcome'}`; all behind `requireApplicationAccess`, own record only, server-validated |
**Completed revision → scope.** The pure rules already held; now pinned by `npm run revision:scope:test` (20 checks) and live (`e2e-checkpoint` section I, ordinary student: declared coverage and scheduled revision give scope 0 → complete the task → still 0 until the outcomes are explicitly confirmed → eligible as **revision**, paper `revisionary`). Missed/deferred excluded; an unfinished continuation does not bring back last week's scope. `choosePaperKind` extracted from `getPaperPreview` (same rule). No eligibility or scoring change.
**Tests (all independent; skips are reported separately and never counted as passes).** `firstcycle:test` 21/0 (fixed instants: Sunday evening in Asia/Kolkata; one instant in Kolkata/Auckland/London/Los Angeles; midweek; late Saturday; no windows; class-led Tue/Thu near its boundary), `checkpoint:test` 26/0, `revision:scope:test` 20/0, `entry:test` 53/0 (adds the ten-topic case: three covered → exactly the other seven planned), `e2e:checkpoint` 79/0/0-skipped, `e2e:calendar` rewritten as four independent journeys on its own ordinary students (successful placement chain *executed*; no capacity; missing bank coverage; render purity) 30/0/0-skipped. `e2e-entry` no longer forces `Pacific/Auckland`.
**Browser (Playwright plugin, ordinary students, throwaway, deleted; desktop 1280×800 and mobile 390×844; real clicks/keys):** 1 Sunday review (notice + dates) → accept → Calendar on 2–8 Nov; 2 first-pass-complete student's mock in Calendar; 3 timing/description/length set (conflict with a Work commitment shown; proposals at 18:00 around it; focus returned to the trigger); 4 choice "use the mock" persists across refresh; 5 mock moved (Enter to submit) → Calendar, Home, roadmap agree, choice reset; 6 outcome "taken, 62%" → weekly review labelled external/self-reported → next-week preview → saved; 7 revision task done → outcomes confirmed → Tests shows a weekly **revisionary** paper (and honestly that 0 reviewed questions exist for Ethics). Escape closes dialogs and restores focus; server errors appear inside the dialog. Fixed: radio labels overflowed at 390px; dialog errors were generic.
**Migration applied:** `20261105_mock_checkpoint` (rehearsed 18/18 in a rolled-back transaction: `npm run mock:rehearse`; ledger dry-run 0 pending).
**Limits.** Not built (as scoped): maintenance revision rotation, observed pace adaptation, the full mock engine/generator, bank expansion. A mock is one record per student (no list of several). Suggestions can be added only for the open week (later weeks say so). Preparation can land on a self-study Sunday window. `use_mock` stops *new* papers being planned; an already accepted paper is removed by the student. A prior unreviewed edit of `/api/tasks` (adds one task per call) is not idempotent; the button disables while saving. Date-boundary live assertions that need a particular weekday say SKIPPED on other days (the fixed-instant suite covers them). The built-in Claude browser could not reach localhost; the Playwright plugin was used.

**Regression after the shared change (live, ordinary students):** `e2e-entry` 46/0, `e2e-weekly-review` 29/0, `e2e-integration` 43/0, `e2e-onboarding` 84/0, `e2e-access-matrix` 51/0, `e2e-home` 23/0, `e2e-roadmap` 41/0, `e2e-activation` 47/0, `npm run verify` exit 0, ledger dry-run 0 pending. Two stale fixtures were corrected, not the product: `e2e-activation` built a draft with a literal fingerprint that the existing staleness check could never accept (it now uses the real fingerprint and the shared cycle decision), and `e2e-roadmap` asserted an empty final phase (Phase B added exam-stretch focus weeks; it now asserts that no other phase runs into it). `e2e-home` failed once under load and passed on rerun (cause not established).


#### 9.6.1 Phase C1 — trustworthy checkpoint commands and assessment closure (1 Nov 2026)
**Concrete fixes.**
- **Atomic commands.** Save/move, cancel, choice, outcome (incl. postponement), add-suggestion, remove/keep-related are each ONE database transaction: `checkpoint_command()` (service role only; serialised per student by an advisory lock) writes the checkpoint, the week's choice cleanup/cancellation of an unstarted paper, the outcome history row, the stale-draft version and the receipt together. A new date for a postponement is validated before anything is recorded. Errors are never reported as "nothing changed" after a possible partial write: a transport failure says "not confirmed, sending it again is safe".
- **Identity and retries.** `profiles.mock_id` (stable) and `mock_version` (bumped by a trigger for every writer, including onboarding/Settings). Every command carries a request key and the checkpoint id/version it was made against; `checkpoint_commands(user_id, key)` stores the receipt. Same key → original receipt (also after the checkpoint was cleared or completed); same key with another payload → 409 `KEY_REUSED`; an older version → 409 `CHECKPOINT_CHANGED` (the response carries the current checkpoint); concurrent identical requests give one effect. `mock_outcomes` is now append-only history (planned date, **actual date**, recorded-at, checkpoint id, the choice in force); unique per (checkpoint, planned date); legacy rows keep a null checkpoint and were not rewritten.
- **"Add to my week" identity.** `checkpoint_suggestion_tasks (user, checkpoint, suggestion) → task`; the server recomputes the proposal from current state (client title/date/length ignored), checks it is inside the OPEN cycle and does not hit a protected commitment, then creates the task and its creation event in the same transaction. Two devices/keys → one task. After refresh the suggestion shows "Added — open the task" at the task's real day/time.
- **Outcome timing and closure.** Planned date, actual date (default = the student's local today, never in the future, correctable) and recorded-at are separate. Closure rules (`getWeeklyReviewState`): **use mock + taken** → gate `external_mock` (self-reported, no evidence); **keep paper / decide later / no choice + taken** → recorded and shown ("does not replace this week's paper") but the paper rules still apply; **postponed / not taken** (any choice) → never unlocks early closure, the reason says so and the supported paper / explicit no-paper path remains; choosing the mock without recording the outcome at the boundary gives `mock_pending`; an in-progress weekly paper is never bypassed. The outcome dialog can set "how it counts" in the same transaction while the week is undecided. A typed result is stored as text only.
- **"Use mock" with a paper present.** An unstarted weekly paper triggers an explicit dialog: **Replace** (cancels the paper: `cancelled_at` + a `cancelled` event naming the mock, row kept) or **Keep both**; a started/finished paper is preserved (choosing the mock is refused with an explanation, "keep both" remains). Calendar, Tests (new notice) and weekly review read the same state.
- **Suggestion scheduling.** Same rules as the planner: no Sunday for self-study (the mock itself may be on a Sunday), shortened to the student's maximum sitting, nothing at a clock time already past today, protected/timed commitments and the mock's own time respected, a day already full of accepted untimed work is not offered even if a clock gap exists (reason: "already full"), only addable inside the open cycle (later cycles stay visible proposals). `occupantsFor` no longer treats cancelled tasks as occupying time (found while testing).
- **Related tasks.** Moving the mock lists the preparation already added with the reason and Remove / Keep; cancelling or finishing the mock leaves them on the calendar and Calendar asks about each ("Preparation you added earlier"); started/completed work is never moved or removed. Moving the mock **inside the same week keeps** that week's choice (a bug found in browser testing: the choice was cleared on any move).
- **Cycle alignment.** `weekForDate` (schedule.ts): an existing cycle's own boundaries first, then today's resolved week (incl. a variable class anchor), then the recurring-class pattern / Monday–Sunday default for far dates; replaces the checkpoint code's own recurring-only resolution.
**Migration applied:** `20261106_checkpoint_commands` (rehearsed 33/33 in a rolled-back transaction: `npm run checkpoint:rehearse`, including a forced failure after earlier writes that rolls the whole command back).
**Evidence.** DB rehearsal 33/33; `checkpoint:test` 41/0 (adds Sunday, sitting limit, past times, full day, open-cycle, request parsing); `e2e:checkpoint:commands` 110/0 (replay after clearance; two concurrent suggestion acceptances; same key different payload; invalid postponement leaves nothing; stale cancel/edit/choice; all 12 choice × outcome combinations each asserting gate, recap, guidance and zero evidence; future taken date refused; early taking attributed; paper replaced / started preserved; Sunday and 30/20-minute limits; variable- and recurring-class alignment; moving/cancelling after suggestions; ownership); `e2e:checkpoint` and `e2e:calendar` re-run after the contract change. Browser (Playwright, desktop 1280×800 and mobile 390×844, ordinary students): add preparation with a **lost response** (fetch wrapper throws after the server saved) → same button again → exactly one task, "Added — open the task" after refresh; "use the mock" with an unstarted paper → dialog, Escape leaves everything unchanged and returns focus, Replace cancels the paper; move the mock → obsolete preparation prompt → Remove it; record "taken" 1 Nov for a mock planned 5 Nov with result → weekly review shows "External mock (2026-11-05): taken on 2026-11-01 … counts as this week's assessment" and the closure gate; record "postponed" → review says it does not replace the assessment and Preview stays disabled; Home, Calendar, Tests and Settings consistent; no horizontal overflow at 390px.
**`e2e-home` failure.** Not reproduced in 5 further runs (4 of them while another suite hit the same server); the dev-server log holds no Home error. Still **unexplained**. The assertion now records status, redirect target and the first 400 characters of the body, so the next occurrence is evidence rather than a rerun.
**Limits.** The assessment choice is per week (a single mock, as scoped); a postponement with no new date retires the checkpoint (history kept) rather than keeping an undated placeholder; an in-progress/ready weekly attempt is covered by the gate's ordering and the paper-started rules but was not built as a live fixture; related-task review compares day only (a task moved by the student is flagged until "Keep it"); "Remove it" cancels, it does not offer an automatic re-add; suggestions for later cycles cannot be added until that cycle is open. Not built (as scoped): maintenance revision rotation, measured pace adaptation, multiple mocks, bank expansion.

**C1 final run (live, ordinary students):** `e2e-checkpoint-commands` 111/0 (1 skip: the rollback case, proved by the rehearsal), `e2e-checkpoint` 79/0, `e2e-calendar` 30/0/0-skipped, weekly-review 29/0, integration 43/0, onboarding 84/0, entry 46/0, home 23/0, activation 47/0, access matrix 51/0; rehearsals 33/33 (`checkpoint:rehearse`) and 18/18 (`mock:rehearse`, its "one outcome per planned date" check updated to the new per-checkpoint rule); `npm run verify` exit 0; dry-run 0 pending. Fixtures deleted; only my own 3102 server was stopped.


### 9.7 Phase D — connected maintenance revision and rotation (3 Nov 2026)
**What changed for students.** Covered material now returns as revision instead of the same first readings every week, and a finished first pass leads into a real revision week. One **derived queue** (`src/lib/curriculum/revisionQueue.ts`, pure) ranks every covered reading (studied, decaying or verified; partly covered readings stay first-pass work). Nothing about it is stored: recency comes from confirmed study/revision activity per outcome (`confirmedActivity`), reported uncertainty from `user_entry_uncertainty`, evidence from the LOS tracker (validity and supersession rules already applied), selection history from the student's own `review` tasks. **No migration, no new table.** `selectRevisionForWeek` (`revisionQueueService.ts`) is the single place a week's selection is assembled; the planner (`buildWeekPlanWithPaper`), the weekly-review preview and every page read it, so they cannot disagree.
**Policy (deterministic).** Score = dueness (mean over the reading's outcomes against the phase interval; an outcome with no date counts as exactly due, never zero, never "forgotten") + reported uncertainty (only while due against the shorter targeted interval, so a flag yields after a revision and never holds the queue) + unsteady evidence + exam-weight relevance + subject attention + roadmap focus + checkpoint proximity − a short cool-down after a skip/removal. Recent strong evidence halves dueness for a while; it stays eligible and its age keeps growing. Ties: least recently planned first, then a per-week rotation hash (so equal readings take turns). A subject may take at most half of a week's places while others have candidates. Open repair → the reading is "covered by a repair" (no second block); a live task or a continuation → "already in the plan"/"continuing" (counts against the week's cap, never scheduled twice).
**Planning assumptions (central, `REVISION_ASSUMPTIONS`; not validated predictions of memory or passing; not a student setting).** Maintenance interval by phase 28/21/14/10 days (first pass / consolidation / revision / final), 25% shorter with a checkpoint within 21 days; targeted (reported) interval 7 days; strong-evidence rest 21 days at ×0.5; per-outcome overdue cap 2.5 intervals; skip cool-down 7 days; subject share ≤ 50%. Weekly caps reuse the existing `MAX_REVISION_TASKS` (3 / 8 / 8 / 4); a finished first pass plans as consolidation.
**Lifecycle.** Revision tasks are existing `review` tasks (title, why-now rationale from the queue, approach, what done means, "records activity, not demonstrated understanding"). Completion refreshes recency only for outcomes the student **explicitly confirmed** (existing `confirm_task_scope`); partly done refreshes only those outcomes; missed/deferred/skipped and scheduling alone refresh nothing; removal removes the occurrence and only adds a short cool-down. Continuation reserves the reading (`Reservation.revisionReadingIds`) so continuation + fresh generation is one obligation (plus the existing `displaceDuplicates` net). Acceptance, retry, concurrency and stale-draft protection are the existing `accept_weekly_transition` + `weekly_source_version` (activity changes in the closing cycle are already in the version). Assessment scope still enters only through explicit confirmation; no eligibility or 90-question change.
**Routes/services/storage.** New: `revisionQueue.ts`, `revisionQueueService.ts`, `revisionOverview.ts` (read-only Curriculum/Coach view), `RevisionStatus.tsx`, `RevisionRotation.tsx`. Changed: `generateWeek.ts` (queue replaces the uncertainty-only rank; `RevisionOutcome` returned), `weeklyReview/service.ts` (`next.revision` in state and preview), `ReviewWeekForm`/review page, Curriculum overview/subject/reading pages, `nextActionSelector` (revision next action says why), Coach situation/links, `operatingState*` (task rationale). **No new endpoint and no new write path**; `GET /api/weekly-review/state` and `POST /api/weekly-review/preview` return an extra `revision` object (same auth, ownership and callers). Storage: none.
**Before → after (same inputs; Economics flagged "most of this topic", every reading studied, consolidation cap 8, each chosen reading completed and confirmed on the Wednesday):** *before* every week `R12 R13 R14 R15 R16 R17 R18 R19` (Economics readings in order; nothing the student did changed the next week). *after* wk1 `R12* R13* R16* R17* R10 R11 R38 R39`, wk2 `R19* R18* R15* R14* R93 R92 R91 R90`, wk3 `R13* R12* R17* R16* R62 R63 R60 R61`, wk4 `R19* R18* R15* R14* R80 R81 R82 R83`, wk5 `R13* R12* R17* R16* R39 R38 R11 R10`, wk6 `R14* R15* R18* R19* R92 R93 R90 R91` (* flagged: weekly until revised, never more than half the places; others rotate, the first-week readings return in week 5 after their interval).
**Tests.** `revision:queue:test` 46/0 (fixed dates: midway maintenance, unknown recency, rotation, six-week completion, missed/partial/declined, continuation/overlap/repair, strong evidence, uncertainty share, intervals, no-bank). `e2e:revision` 47/0/0-skipped (live, ordinary students: midway first week; first-pass-complete week; four cycles through the real preview/accept endpoints with completion, partial (2 outcomes), missed, continuation, retry, second round after ageing the student's own activity by 40 days; repair overlap; stale draft after new activity; two simultaneous acceptances; short capacity shows waiting work with reasons and calm trade-offs; ready and in-progress weekly attempts keep the gate shut even with "close without a paper"; Tests scope: scheduled adds nothing, confirmed is revisionary, missed excluded; no evidence/attempt/response/repair/Coach rows created by any revision step). `entry:test` 53/0 (rewritten around the queue). A pre-existing weekday-dependent fixture in `e2e-weekly-review` (read a non-existent `tasks` field, so its empty-week check only ran when the gate was "not ready") now reads `dueTasks` and runs (30/0).
**Browser (Playwright, ordinary throwaway student, desktop 1280×800 and mobile 390×844):** midway student's Calendar (3 revision readings alongside Equity study) → revision task drawer (what / why now / how / what done means) → Done → confirm covered outcomes → Tests shows a weekly **revisionary** paper (1 revisited outcome; honest "0 of 2 questions available") → Curriculum overview rotation line and reading page "Why is this here?" (opened with Enter) → Home next action says "Revision. …why" → Review week → answers → preview shows the next rotation, continuing readings and why → save → Calendar next cycle → continued task links to its original week and back → Coach "What should I focus on?" names the next reading with a link. Mobile: reading page, review, agenda, Home and the drawer have no horizontal overflow; Escape closes the drawer with focus in it. Fixed during the browser pass: stale pre-answer rotation shown beside the real preview; "no revision fits" shown when continuing work had used the allowance; duplicate full stops and "unknown (unknown)" wording. **Not done in the browser:** closing a cycle on mobile (the accept path ran on desktop; the endpoints are covered live), and bulk answers to 19 tasks were set by script-driven clicks, not one real click each.
**Limits.** Recency is per confirmed outcome from task completion time, not from when the student studied (no study-date field exists); a reading studied only in the student's own words has unknown recency. "Reported" recency is only the onboarding "working on it now" flag. Evidence-based nudges use the tracker's bands (unsteady/stale → small raise; secure and recent → rest) and a reading with no reviewed questions says so. Trade-offs offered in the preview are described (add availability, move work, accept) but not applied by a control. The queue's `limit` shrinks by one per continuing reading, so a student who continues everything gets no new reading that week. Sittings of a long reading are separate tasks (the existing splitter), so a reading can appear as two tasks. Selection costs one extra tracker read per build (~150 ms measured; the whole build ~2 s). Not built (as scoped): measured pace adaptation, multiple mocks, a full mock engine, bank expansion, a revision settings screen.

#### 9.7.1 Phase D1 — revision correctness and regression closure (3 Nov 2026)
**The eight failures, each with its established cause** (assertion, expected, actual and server state captured before any change; no retry or loosening).
- **`e2e-roadmap` ×5 (no roadmap yet; generating without a target refused; Curriculum asks which exam; no availability says unknown; nothing is done yet).** *Fixture/order dependency.* The suite needs `--reset` on the shared +e2e account (its header says so); I ran it without. Re-run WITH `--reset`: 41/0, so not an application regression. Fixed: the suite now builds that state on its own ordinary throwaway student (onboarded through the real API, then its own roadmap/coverage/availability/exam fields cleared), no shared account, no flag. 41/0.
- **`e2e-coach` ×2.** (1) *Stale assertion:* it matched "does not yet single out", the reply says "do not yet single out"; the reply itself is correct (no repair named, no proposal, no repair link). Replaced by equivalent semantic checks (the honest "cannot point to a current repair follow-up" fallback, zero proposals, no repair-follow-up link). (2) *Obsolete by the Home redesign:* the prefilled "/app/coach?q=" card was removed from Home (Decisions §7 puts a Coach entry last); Home still links to Coach. Replaced by: Home links to Coach, and the contextual question-prefilled entries are asserted where they now live (weekly review, a reading). The suite now also uses its own throwaway student (it reset the shared account before). 43/0.
- **`e2e-onboarding` ×1 ("does not move the week boundary").** *Clock dependency in the fixture:* the ad-hoc class was placed at today+6; on a Tuesday that is Monday, so "the day before it" is Sunday, which is also the natural week end, and the old `!==` check was meaningless either way. Now: the baseline week is measured first, the near-class date is chosen so its "day before" differs from that week, the unchanged week must equal the baseline exactly, and the anchoring case is asserted against a date that really differs. Also moved to its own throwaway student. 85/0.
- **Masked empty-week assertion (`e2e-weekly-review`).** It read `state.tasks ?? state.due` (neither exists; the field is `dueTasks`), so it silently skipped on most weekdays. The second cycle is now a fixture that is closable on any weekday, the field is read, and the check runs and asserts. 31/0.
- Verdict: **no application regression among the eight.** Two were stale/obsolete assertions (replaced with equivalent coverage), the rest fixture, order or clock dependencies (removed by independent ordinary students and exact fixtures).
**Deterministic boundary coverage.** `first-cycle-test` (27/0, fixed instants) adds Monday entry and a Tuesday with an upcoming variable class (anchored, too-close and far). New `boundary-test` (10/0; real schema, injected clock via `getStudyWeekContext(userId, now)`, ordinary throwaway students): Sunday evening, Monday entry, Tuesday with a confirmed variable class and the same student after the class date, a recurring Tue/Thu class-led cycle, an accepted EMPTY cycle that is never moved, a cycle accepted on Sunday that starts tomorrow, no availability (Sunday and midweek). No client-controlled production clock exists; the seam is the existing server-side `reference` argument. **Fixed in application code:** the "next confirmed class" lookup in `getStudyWeekContext` used the real clock even when a clock was injected; it now uses the injected one.
**Activity dates.** The shared reader (`studyActivityService`/`confirmedActivity`) already dated a logged sitting by the study date the student entered (`learning_log.local_date`, up to 14 days back) and every other completion by when it was recorded; that now travels with each observation (`atKind`: `study_date` | `recorded`, optional so other callers are unchanged) and the revision queue shows it. Verified live: studied 7 days ago, logged today → 7 days old "(the study date you logged)"; completed today with no study date → today "(when you marked it done)"; scheduled then skipped → unknown; partial confirmed 1 of 3 outcomes → "Recent confirmed activity covers 1 of 3 outcomes; the rest are untouched". **Removed:** the queue used the onboarding save time as a date for subjects the student said they were "working on"; that is now a dateless report that counts as unknown.
**Evidence scope.** The queue now carries evidence per outcome (tracker band and its own check date, which already applies validity, supersession, sample size and the 21-day staleness rule). Rest (half urgency) applies only to outcomes whose own evidence is secure and recent; untested, unsteady, old and repair outcomes keep full urgency; unsteady evidence raises priority in proportion to its share; the explanation names the split ("Of 4 outcomes here, 1 secure and recent, 1 secure but older, 1 need a fresh look, 1 untested"). A reading with only reading-level counts gets no reduction. No new policy and no repair closure. Regression: secure subset + untested; mixed ages; all-secure; older secure; reading-level only (pure, 56/0), and per-outcome loading checked live against a real repair thread.
**Bank-capability wording.** `readingHasReviewedQuestions` (any reviewed item) is removed. `readingCheckCapability` uses the assessment service's own rule (`loadEligibleItemPool`: active manifest, concept-outcome binding, review tier, unseen by this student) and its own minimum (`MIN_PAPER_QUESTIONS` = 5), and the wording says what is known: *none exist*; *exist but none currently eligible*; *only N eligible, a check needs 5*; *N eligible unseen — enough to start a reading check; question depth only, not proof every outcome can be tested*. Verified on the real bank (no item promoted or created): r026 none, r100 unreviewed only → not eligible, r027 2 eligible → insufficient, r028 15 → supported (`bank:capability:test` 9/0). Planning is unaffected in every state.
**Continuation and partial scope.** *Fixed:* two continuing sittings of one reading consumed two reading-level allowances; they now count once (verified live: 2 tasks, 1 reading, allowance 8 → 7). Also verified: continued work gets no duplicate fresh obligation; partial confirmation refreshes only confirmed outcomes; an open repair keeps its identity and gets no revision block; no evidence/attempt/response/repair/Coach row from logging or completing revision; new activity makes a prepared review stale; two simultaneous acceptances open one cycle. None of this uses title matching.
**Tests.** `revision:queue:test` 56/0; `e2e:revision` 59/0/0-skipped (two runs); `first-cycle` 27/0; `boundary:test` 10/0; `bank:capability:test` 9/0; `e2e-onboarding` 85/0; `e2e-roadmap` 41/0; `e2e-coach` 43/0; `e2e-weekly-review` 31/0 (suites below).
**Browser (Playwright, ordinary throwaway student; desktop 1280×800 and mobile 390×844):** Log learning (Reading 28, all 5 outcomes, "Choose an earlier day" = 27 Oct) → saved; the reading page says "last activity: 7 days ago (the study date you logged)" and "15 eligible unseen questions are available, enough to start a reading check (minimum 5)… question depth only"; Reading 36 says questions exist but none is currently eligible; Calendar revision task → Partly done → saved → confirmed 1 of 3 outcomes → the reading's page says recent activity covers 1 of 3; review week on mobile (390px, no overflow): answers (the partly done task by real tap; the other 10 tasks' "Not done" and "Remove" selections by script-driven clicks) → preview shows the next rotation, why continuing readings fill the allowance, and what moved → Save → Calendar notice with continuing and moved work. Not browser-tested: logging on mobile (desktop only), desktop weekly closure.
**Migrations.** None (dry-run: 0 pending).
**Limits.** Recency is per confirmed outcome and only as precise as the data: a logged sitting has a study date (≤14 days back); an ordinary task completion has only its recording time, and the student has no way to enter an earlier study date for it (no date-entry UI was added). Evidence rest relies on the tracker's bands (an outcome mapped to several concepts is whatever the tracker concludes). Capability says question depth for one reading; it does not assemble a form or test per-outcome coverage. `e2e-weekly-review` still uses the shared +e2e account (needs `--reset`), and a suite that crashes before its cleanup can leave `integ+` students behind (a cleanup deletes every `integ+` user).

**D1 final run (live, ordinary students, my isolated server on 3102 then stopped; canonical types restored):** onboarding 85/0, roadmap 41/0, coach 43/0, `e2e:revision` 59/0/0-skipped, weekly-review 31/0, entry 46/0 (its "self-reported" assertion was case-sensitive and over-strict after Phase D's wording; now case-insensitive and still rejects "demonstrated"), integration 43/0, calendar 30/0, home 23/0, activation 47/0, checkpoint 75/0 (1 Sunday-only skip, covered by the fixed-instant suite), learning-log HTTP 13, task-execution HTTP 19, `boundary:test` 10/0, `bank:capability:test` 9/0, `revision:queue:test` 56/0, `first-cycle` 27/0. `npm run verify` exit 0 on its final run (one earlier run aborted inside `readiness:test` with a process "Abort trap: 6" after all revision tests had passed; that suite passes alone and on the clean rerun, so it is recorded as an unexplained one-off crash, not a result). Migration dry-run: 0 pending. All `integ+` fixtures deleted.

### 9.8 Phase 1 — adaptive weekly planning, deviation recovery and bounded cleanup (4 Nov 2026)
**What changed.** The weekly composer no longer feeds an empty `priorCompletionRates` array. `cycleObservation.ts` (pure) and `cycleObservationService.ts` read what happened to the work each closed cycle accepted, from records that already exist (cycle, tasks, task history, scope confirmations, `weekly_reviews` receipt). The cycle being closed is read too, as the student's answers will leave it, because the next week is proposed before that cycle closes. `envelope.ts` holds `ADAPTATION`, the single home for every limit (labelled planning assumptions) and `adaptEnvelope`; the legacy `pacingFactor`/`completionRateOf` are removed. `aheadBehind.ts` reconciles first-pass coverage with the ORIGINAL plan (rebuilt from the inputs saved with the roadmap; a rebuilt roadmap cannot say what was ahead).
