**Signals, kept apart.** *Capacity:* current availability and commitments (`dailyCapacityMinutes`), never history. *Execution:* proven share of the ACCEPTED workload in planned minutes (a range, not a number, when records cannot settle it). *Observed pace:* unchanged `observedPace.ts` (4 eligible modules, reported minutes only, chains once, class/revision/tests excluded; the 20 + 25 = 45 regression still holds); otherwise the labelled 85 min/unit assumption. None is understanding.
**Observation rules.** Unknown is not missed (range low/high, cycle labelled incomplete); missing minutes are never zero; partial earns only the outcomes the student confirmed (1 of 4 of 80 min = 20 min), with no confirmed scope it is "unknown extent", never 50%; an empty cycle has no ratio; removed/deferred/missed stay in the baseline; the original accepted duration is the baseline (task history); a split divides accepted work and adds none; a continuation is accepted work in the cycle that took it and each cycle keeps its own record; extra learning, student-added work and unfinished optional items are outside the baseline. A cycle never closed through the review is incomplete.
**Adaptation.** No usable cycle → opening 0.6 of the even split (labelled). One usable week → a ±small step ("not a trend"). Two or more (last 3, newest weighted most, a lone outlier damped) → bands: ≥90% 1.15, ≥70% 1.0, ≥50% 0.8, ≥30% 0.55, else 0.5. Above 1.0 is saved as `priority = 'optional'` work with an explanatory rationale (skipping it is not a miss). After a break (an empty closing cycle, or ≥7 days gap after it) → manageable restart. Incomplete weeks are set aside with the reason shown. **Behaviour change to note:** the envelope is now a real cap inside a day (`newContentCapUnits`); before, it only decided whether a day started new work, so its size was masked by day granularity. Compressed timelines (capacity-limited) are unchanged. Sitting limits, protected commitments, class boundaries, Monday–Saturday placement, continuation and repair identities are untouched; continuing work is still placed first.
**Review.** Short explanation (`AdaptationNote`), "Make this draft lighter" (server-owned: the draft stores `lighter`, ×0.75 with floor 0.4, optional extra dropped), an availability link that returns to the review (`/app/review-week` added to the return allow-list), answers preserved (latest server draft + this browser's copy, including the no-paper acknowledgement), what waits listed. Acceptance is unchanged (atomic, stale-safe, retry-safe; availability is in `weekly_source_version`). The receipt (`weekly_reviews.recap.adaptation`) keeps mode, factor, reasons, optional minutes, waiting count and the ahead summary; Calendar, Home, Curriculum and the Coach read that one record through `getCurrentAdaptation` and recalculate nothing. A week opened before this recorded nothing makes no claim.
**Before → after.** *Overloaded:* three weeks each 1 of 5 equal tasks done → before: legacy factor on an empty history = 0.6 forever; after: "lighter" at 0.55, 3 weeks read, less fresh work than the opening proposal (live), waiting readings listed; one such week alone → 0.5 with "not a trend". *Ahead of plan:* the plan's later topics already covered (FSA/Corporate Finance in the pure case) → 19 readings, ~3,884 min of first-pass work no longer remains, no first-pass study is proposed for them, maintenance revision stays, the stored plan and accepted tasks are unchanged until the student accepts; unfinished FSA continues once (no duplicate "Study:" row, original progress kept).
**Cleanup.** Removed `pacingFactor`, `completionRateOf`, `CycleOutcome`, the empty-history comment and the Phase-1-has-no-closed-cycles comment; `observedPace` returns `{minutesPerEffortUnit, modules}` (old function kept as a one-line wrapper, caller `observedMinutesPerEffortUnit` unchanged); the dead "/app/settings" availability link in the review became the real editor with a return path. `e2e-weekly-review` moved to its own throwaway student (no `--reset`, no shared account). Route/link inventory: `npm run routes:inventory`, `audit:routes` (in `verify`); retired endpoints (repair 410) and redirects untouched. No endpoint added or changed in shape (`/api/weekly-review/preview` accepts optional `lighter`), so `ROUTE-OWNERSHIP.md` needs no change.
**Tests.** `pulse-envelope-test` 36/0 (pure, fixed data: observations, adaptation, break, lighter, pace regressions, FSA/Corporate Finance ahead and unfinished); new `e2e:adaptation` 39/0 (14 required scenarios, own students); weekly-review, revision, calendar, home, coach, entry, integration re-run (see D-final line below).
**Migrations.** None. **Limits.** (1) Reported time is optional, so observed pace stays an assumption for most students until 4 finished readings carry minutes. (2) A cycle's baseline uses task history for edited durations; work added mid-week by the student is treated as extra. (3) Ahead/behind is topic-level against the original plan; roadmaps saved without inputs (older accounts) make no ahead claim. (4) The envelope is a cap, so a student with a distant exam and high capacity now gets a lighter first week than before. (5) Learning-log extra sittings are covered by the pure rule, not a live fixture. (6) Coach UI rendering of the reply was not re-verified in the browser; the API reply was.

### 9.9 Phase 2 — reviewer workspace, question production and safe previews (4 Nov 2026)
**What a reviewer can now do.** Open `/app/tests/review` (existing URL, reviewer-only): a paginated queue filtered by topic, reading, outcome, state and batch, with totals that count the whole filtered set (the first page's rows are never the total). Open any item (`/app/tests/review/[itemId]`) and see, together: the question, key, worked solution, distractor diagnoses, curriculum mapping (reading → outcome → concept, and whether the concept is signed off), the automatic check results for the *current version* (a verifier disagreement is shown as "checkers disagree", not hidden), source/batch provenance, family members side by side, text-hash look-alikes (labelled "not proof of one family"), version chain and history, and student challenges. Actions: **Edit**, **Re-check automatically** (real models, result discarded if the item was edited meanwhile), **Preview as a student**, **Approve and publish**, **Return for correction**, **Reject**, **Retire**, **Place in a family**, **Hold out for future mocks**. Approve is off, with the reasons listed, until every blocker is cleared. Also: `/app/tests/review/preview` (rehearse any slice as a paper), `/app/tests/review/batches` (durable batches with measured report), `/app/tests/review/challenges` (existing `resolve_item_challenge`), `/app/tests/manifest` (unchanged URL; now shares the reviewer nav). Four plain states everywhere: **Draft**, **Automatically checked**, **Human reviewed**, **Retired** (`itemState.ts` is the only place the words live).

**Publication is one command.** `decide_item_review(item, expectedVersion, decision, note, ack)` (SECURITY DEFINER, reviewer role checked inside) locks the item and, in one transaction, validates the version the reviewer saw, the three distinct options and key, the worked solution, an eligible mapping (concept in the *active* manifest, signed off, concept↔outcome↔reading agree), current-version automatic checks (no failure; a disagreement needs explicit acknowledgement) and the batch's source rights; then writes the status change, a named `item_review_decisions` row, the `human_reviewer` validation row and a revision row. `item_publish_blockers()` is the single source of "why is this blocked" for both the command and the screens. A repeat of the same decision is a no-op; a different or stale one is refused with its reason; `unique(item_id, item_version)` for approve/reject/return makes duplicate history impossible. A return consumes the version (the corrected item is a new version).

**Versioning and families.** A published item, or any item already in an attempt or a paper, is never rewritten: `edit_item` creates a new candidate row (`<id>-vN`, `supersedes_item_id`), the old version stays human reviewed until the new one is approved, at which point it is retired in the same transaction; a second edit while a successor exists is refused (no duplicate successors). A trigger refuses any other path that tries to change the stem, options, key or feedback of a reviewed, retired or used item. Unpublished, unused items are corrected in place; the version bumps and the old checks stop counting. `item_families` + `assessment_items.family_id`: related scenarios/numeric variants are grouped by a reviewer; **`family_id` null means explicitly unknown** (never counted as an independent family; a text hash does not create one). Hold-out is a family (or, while a family is unknown, a single item) designation, set by `set_item_holdout`, and is synced to members.

**Content boundary (end to end).** (1) Selection: every student selector uses `studentEligible()` = human reviewed **and** not held out (`itemPool`, `blueprint`, `paper` counts, `resultService`, repair-check pool, tests overview, sufficiency); `allowedTiers()` is now `['reviewed']` for every purpose, so the former "verified items for routine practice" tier is gone. (2) Paper construction: a trigger on `form_items` refuses an item that is not human reviewed (`ITEM_NOT_REVIEWED`) or is held out (`ITEM_HELD_OUT`; a future mock path sets `app.holdout_form`), so a start/reservation command cannot bypass the filters. (3) Scoring: `finalize_assessment_attempt` (patched in place from the live definition, additive migration) gave evidence to attempts of *verified* items; it now produces evidence only when every item is human reviewed or has a recorded approval (an item retired after approval keeps its evidence for an attempt already using it); otherwise the attempt is scored as practice with no evidence rows. Existing evidence is neither deleted nor relabelled. (4) Granting: a trigger refuses `review_status='reviewed'` from any role except inside `decide_item_review` (`REVIEW_COMMAND_REQUIRED`), so generators, import scripts and workers cannot grant it. (5) Preview: the reviewer preview is client-only state (no fetch in the component; verified by a row-count test across attempts, responses, evidence, plan blocks, repair threads, task events, forms).

**Ambiguous provenance (reported, not relabelled).** 19 items carry `review_status='reviewed'` with **no recorded human decision** and `reviewed_at` null: 6 hand-written seed items (`q-fsa-intro-*`, `q-fsa-income-1/2`, `q-fsa-balance-*`) and 13 pilot items from 7 Sep 2026 (`pipeline_v1`, models gpt-oss-20b / nemotron-3-super). They are the only items students can currently receive and 33 historical responses exist against them. They are left eligible (changing that would silently alter live evidence and break every suite that depends on FSA) and are shown in the workspace as "Reviewed, no decision on record", counted separately, and can be given a recorded decision by approving them. **Founder decision needed:** require re-approval before they stay eligible.

**Production pipeline.** `item_batches` (stable `request_key` — the same request never makes a second batch; target manifest/curriculum version; source provenance and rights status; prompt hash; model tiers), `item_batch_cells` (one per blueprint cell: status, attempts, lease, verdict, failure reason, defect codes, runtime, token use). Worker = `npm run items:batch -- create|run|status|report|cancel` (Next + Postgres only). `claim_batch_cell` leases one cell (skip-locked; a crashed worker's lease expires and the cell is re-claimed, bounded by `max_attempts`=2), `store_batch_cell` writes the item, its own family, check results and the cell result in one transaction with a deterministic item id (a retry cannot duplicate it), `fail_batch_cell` records the reason. Nothing a worker does can exceed "automatically checked"; a verifier disagreement lands as an automatically-checked item flagged "checkers disagree"; a failed check lands as a draft and the rejected draft is also kept in `rejected_item_drafts`. The checker is a different model family from the author (probed 4 Nov: `/models` lists models that 404 or hang; working: nemotron-3-ultra-550b for authoring, mistral-large-4 for checking). Batches default to `rights_status='unconfirmed'`, which blocks publication of their items.

**Candidate batch — measured, not approved.** Batch `Benchmark 100 (10 per topic)` (`bench-100-2026-11-04`; 100 cells, 10 outcomes per topic across all ten topics; curriculum 2027 v1.0; prompts `gen-164bf500/ver-12ca782b`; author nemotron-3-ultra-550b, checker mistral-large-4). Inputs were the company curriculum map only (reading/outcome titles from the active manifest); no book text, uploaded PDF or provider question was supplied. The *rights basis for using the outcome wording is not documented*, so the batch is recorded `rights_status = unconfirmed`: its candidates can be inspected and previewed but the publication command refuses them. I stopped the two workers I started after ~61 minutes wall clock; the batch is resumable (`npm run items:batch -- run <id>`), not finished.
- **Processed 50 of 100 cells:** 17 drafted (all "automatically checked", 0 human reviewed, each its own new family), 33 failed, 42 never started, 8 interrupted mid-run (leases expire, they will be re-claimed). **Awaiting review: 17.**
- **Failure reasons (33):** 26 `GEN_TIMEOUT` (author + checker did not finish inside the 300 s per-attempt deadline, mostly while 8 calls ran at once), 2 `NUM_OPTION_NOT_NUMERIC`, 2 `ANSWER_DISAGREEMENT`, 1 `NUM_NO_OPTION_MATCHES`, 1 `STEM_INSUFFICIENT`, 1 `GEN_SHAPE`. By item type, `calculate/calculation` 11, `interpret/data_table` 5, `calculate/data_table` 5 failed most; 41 cells needed a second attempt (cap is 2).
- **By topic (drafted / planned):** Alternative Investments 5/10, Corporate Finance 3/10, Economics 3/10, Derivatives 2/10, Portfolio Management 2/10, Equities 1/10, Ethics 1/10, Financial Statement Analysis 0/10, Fixed Income 0/10, Quantitative Methods 0/10 (cells were interleaved by topic only after a first, topic-ordered stretch, so the last three were barely reached). **Seven of ten topics have at least one candidate; this is not an all-topic result.**
- **Coverage and depth:** 16 of 343 outcomes across the ten topics have a candidate; family depth is 1 (one candidate per construction, no variants yet); unmapped scope is therefore everything else, and FSA/Ethics already hold 243 earlier automatically-checked items from September.
- **Runtime and cost:** ~61 minutes wall clock, 3 + 4 workers, ≈4 minutes per processed cell (timeouts dominate that average). Recorded model usage 80 calls, 50,006 prompt + 116,417 completion tokens (an undercount: a timed-out attempt records none). Provider cost is **not measurable** here (the NVIDIA API is not billed per token on this account); the figure to plan with is time.
- **Next feasible batch:** at about 17 candidates per hour with this concurrency and a 34 % yield (17 from 50 processed cells), the 1,500–2,500 range is not reachable by 24 Nov as configured. A realistic next step is 100 more cells with a 600 s deadline (the cause of 79 % of failures is the deadline) and 4 workers, then re-measure; plan the beta's candidate inventory from that rate, not from the range. Review time is the other limit (see the beta plan's 8–12 minutes per approval).
- **Rights/coverage gaps:** outcome-wording rights unconfirmed (blocks publication, not authoring); the eight topics outside FSA/Ethics have no signed-off concepts (created as `pending`, so even cleared candidates cannot be approved until the manifest is signed off); two candidate mock forms and any variants are not started.

**Cleanup done.** One review guard (`getReviewer()`/`reviewerRoute()`), one eligibility rule (`studentEligible`, `EVIDENCE_TIERS`), one set of state words (`itemState.ts`); removed `ItemReviewQueue` (replaced by the workspace), the `verified` tier from `allowedTiers`, the "Verifier-passed"/"gold" student and manifest wording, the `gold`/`approved` aliases in `evidencePolicy` and `bankCoverage`. No route moved, so no redirect was needed; `/app/tests/review` and `/app/tests/manifest` keep their URLs, `POST /api/diagnostic/items/[id]/review` keeps its URL (now requires `expectedVersion`; its `reviewStatus` response became `status`). New routes: `/app/tests/review/{[itemId],[itemId]/preview,preview,batches,challenges}`, `POST /api/diagnostic/items/[id]/{edit,reverify,family}` (`docs/ROUTE-OWNERSHIP.md` regenerated, `docs/ROUTE-AND-STATE-OWNERSHIP.md` extended). Test fixtures that used automatically checked items in papers now use reviewed items (`attempt-finalization-race`, `repair-schema-rehearsal`, `e2e-activation`); `e2e-tests-tab` (legacy, shared-account) updated to the new response/wording but not run.

**Carry-overs from Phase 1.** (1) *Coach click:* reproduced in the browser as an ordinary student; the click sends the message and the reply (with its link) renders; the earlier miss was not reproducible, no code change. Error path unchanged (a failed send shows the message in a role="alert"). (2) *First-week sizing:* a fresh student, 3 h a day, exam Nov 2027: the standard first week is 313 min (9 portions), "lighter" 234 min (7 portions), never empty; with a Feb 2027 exam both are 673. No cap change; known limit: new work is placed on the earliest days (Wed/Thu here) and the later days stay free. (3) *Learning-log gap:* new `e2e-learning-gap` 8/0: extra learning lowers remaining syllabus effort, the original week's execution records are byte-identical, the next plan stops asking for first-pass study of that reading, a retry is a no-op, removing the log restores the remaining work. (4) *Pace wording:* new `adaptation:note:test` (in `verify`) renders both states: "planning assumption of 85 minutes … N so far" below four readings, "about X minutes per unit of effort, from time you reported" after.

**Verified.** `npm run verify` exit 0 (final code, includes the new pure test and `audit:routes`); migration dry-run 0 pending; `migrations:test` pass. Live (own throwaway students/reviewer, deleted by recorded id; isolated server on 3102 started by me and stopped by its recorded pid; canonical types restored): `reviewer:db:test` **60/0** (boundary triggers, atomic decision, stale/repeat/concurrent decisions, forced mid-decision failure rolls back everything, versions, scoring evidence boundary, families/hold-out, batch claim/restart/duplicate/retry/rights); `e2e:reviewer` **53/0** (student and anonymous access to every reviewer route and command refused with no leak, queue totals equal the database, filters, links resolve, decisions, edit-keeps-published-version, preview writes nothing, reviewed/un-held eligibility); `e2e:learning-gap` 8/0; regression integration 43/0, activation 47/0, weekly-review 31/0, revision 59/0, calendar 30/0, home 23/0, coach 43/0, entry 46/0, adaptation 39/0 (one earlier run of this suite crashed with a `gate_closed` close error and an "Invalid request" and passed on immediate rerun; not explained), taking-flow 65/0, results 57/0; `attempt:race` 8 checks (its cleanup order was fixed). Browser (Playwright, ordinary student then reviewer; desktop 1280 and mobile 390, no horizontal overflow): student redirected from `/app/tests/review`; reviewer queue (counts 24 draft / 252 checked / 19 reviewed / 295 total), item workspace, **edit → real-model re-check (all 7 gates passed) → preview with the draft banner → approve** on a fixture, then a material edit of the published fixture produced `…-v3` while the published version stayed; batch report page; challenge queue. Screenshots in `.playwright-mcp/` (`rv-*.png`, `coach-click-p2.png`).

**Migrations (applied to the disposable dev project).** `20261107_reviewer_workspace` (batches, cells, families, decisions, revisions, guards, scoring patch, decision/edit/family/batch commands), `20261108_publish_blockers` (shared blocker function; decision command now calls it), `20261109_blockers_array_append` (a bare string concatenated to a `text[]` is parsed as an array; the first version of the blocker function failed on that, caught by the DB test). **Known limits.** (1) The 19 legacy items above. (2) `repair:schema:rehearse` now skips its scored journey because no three concepts have three reviewed questions; the repair loop on real bank content is limited by reviewed depth (FSA only). (3) Re-check runs inside the request (up to 4 minutes, abandoned safely after that); only the bulk path is a durable job. (4) A material edit always makes a new version (no "typo-only" shortcut). (5) `bankLoader`/`pilot` scripts predate versions: validations they write carry no `item_version` and count as version 1. (6) Held-out items can only enter a paper through a future mock path that sets `app.holdout_form`; none exists yet. (7) Phase 3 is not started; its input is this bank contract and pipeline, not the 17 unreviewed candidates.

### 9.10 Phase 3 — diagnostic papers, actionable results and repair follow-through (4 Nov 2026)

Built and verified against the disposable development project. Nothing committed. Phase 4 (broader Coach, linked full mocks) not started.

**Legacy eligibility decision (applied, founder may reverse).** A question enters a NEW student paper only if it is human reviewed, not held out, and a recorded `approve` decision exists for its exact current version (view `student_eligible_items`; `paper_item_defects()`; `form_items` trigger). The 19 legacy items still marked reviewed with no decision keep their status and records, are labelled "reviewed, no decision on record", and are NOT eligible. A reviewer restores one by approving it through the normal `decide_item_review` (proved on a legacy-style fixture; it needs a passing automatic check for the version, so legacy items must be re-checked first). Policy is recorded per paper in `assessment_attempts.eligibility_policy` (`approved-version-v1`; null = reserved earlier). Finalisation is unchanged: evidence for reviewed or previously approved items, so a question retired after reservation still scores for the paper that holds it (tested), while no new paper can include it. Open legacy attempts at the time of this change: 0 (query: `eligibility_policy is null and status in ('ready','in_progress')`); any future one stays completable and its result says it used the earlier rule. The unconfirmed-rights batch `8318a6ca…` stays quarantined (`rights_status`). **No candidate-batch benchmark or tuning was run in this phase;** the next one should use documented authoring inputs and record authoring time, verification time, concurrency and timeout reasons, with a longer deadline treated as a measured experiment.

**One contract.** `assessmentDecision.ts` is the single decision for weekly / reading / subject / repair / mock: purpose, confirmed scope, requested vs deliverable, timing, resume, pending (capacity, bank, unconfirmed blocks, further paper), next action. Tests, Calendar's paper strip, `/api/tests/preflight` (now every kind, returns `{decision}`), `/api/tests/start`, weekly closure and the counters (`countUnseenEligibleByModule`, `countAvailableItems`, reading capability, results retest availability, bank coverage) all read it or the pool beneath it. Behaviour changes: unconfirmed blocks no longer block the confirmed part of a paper (they are listed as pending; before, the API allowed it and the card refused); weekly open key is `cycle:pN` and any open weekly paper in the cycle resumes. Breadth: every eligible outcome is asked once before any gets depth when the paper can afford it; reading size (outcomes) ranks readings, page count only breaks ties and only when page metadata is complete; at most one question per known family; a seen family is not fresh; unknown family stays independent. Cap 90; leftover scope is an explicit pending list with a trade-off shown before starting and a further paper (`part` 2+) from what earlier papers did not ask.

**Atomic start.** `start_assessment_paper` (service role; `reserve.ts`) writes blueprint, form, pinned item versions, attempt, scope snapshot and Calendar block link in one transaction under an advisory lock; validates cycle, exact approval, hold-out, freshness, family. Retries and 8 concurrent starts resume one attempt; refusals and a forced mid-write fault leave no rows (proved). Also used by `/api/diagnostic/attempt` (module papers, Calendar paper task). `start_repair_check` now applies the same defect rule. Limit: the scope revision is compared in TypeScript immediately before the transaction (completed scope is not derivable in SQL).

**Results.** Next action leads; findings by pattern with observation counts; accuracy, confidence and timing separate; omitted (no response row, cause unknown), interrupted, disputed/voided/challenged listed as not counted; voided answers leave the score. SWOT opportunities/threats use only `lm_edges` with a written rationale; sequence-only links appear as navigation. Home's Understanding tile links to the latest result. Timed practice is a suggestion with a Coach link, not a session type (not built).

**Storage and routes.** Migration `20261110_paper_contract` (additive): `form_items.item_version`, `assessment_attempts.eligibility_policy`, view `student_eligible_items`, functions `paper_item_defects`, `start_assessment_paper`, patched `start_repair_check`/`form_items_boundary`. Route changes: `/api/tests/preflight` (all kinds), `/api/tests/start` (`part`). `docs/ROUTE-OWNERSHIP.md` regenerated; `ROUTE-AND-STATE-OWNERSHIP.md` extended. `gate_closed` now logs gate, cycle, clock and flags server-side; it did not recur in this phase (all adaptation and weekly-review runs passed).

**Verification — actual-bank limitation (live bank, no fixtures).** Eligible questions available to students: 0. Preflight/start/Tests/mock/reading all refuse with the real counts, name the short reading, keep the scope pending, create nothing, and offer "Plan the week without this paper"; weekly closure allows closing without a paper. Browser (desktop 1280, mobile 390, no overflow).

**Verification — complete success journey (test-only fixture bank, ordinary throwaway students, fixtures removed by recorded id; NOT live-bank capability).** `npm run e2e:paper` 59/0: completed scope → preflight → stale refused → concurrent starts → taking (no keys, confidence required, refresh keeps answers, server-capped timing, interruptions apart) → one result, late answer refused → result (repair first, findings, omitted) → repair preview/accept once → exact Calendar links → completion → fresh re-check (no earlier item, no family variant) → thread stays open → challenge upheld → result excludes the answer. Browser desktop and mobile of the same journey including Calendar task → Start re-check.
Other checks: `paper:db:test` 32/0 (stale approval version, insufficient depth, same family, seen family, >90, retirement after reservation, concurrency, rollback, legacy approval path), `paper:contract:test` 24/0 (in `verify`), `reviewer:db:test` 60/0, `repair:db:test` 35, `attempt:race` 8, `npm run verify` exit 0, migration dry-run 0 pending. Regression with a 72-item fixture bank: tests-tab 46, activation 47, calendar 30, home 23, coach 43, entry 46, adaptation 39, weekly-review 31, revision 59, taking-flow 65, results 57, integration 43.

**Remaining.** `repair:schema:rehearse` skips its scored journey on the live bank (fewer than three concepts with three usable questions) — covered by the fixture journey instead. `e2e-tests-scope` needs explicit flags and was not run. Free-form module papers have no open-attempt identity, so a repeated deliberate request makes a new paper. Legacy fixtures bank: `scripts/fixture-bank.cjs`; suites that need papers need it (keep it under 90 items or the mock shows "Ready"). `next-env.d.ts` differs from the committed copy only by the canonical `.next/types` paths.

### 9.11 Phase 4 — Coach actions and practical study guidance (4 Nov 2026)

Built and verified against the disposable development project. Nothing committed. Phase 5 (linked full mocks) not started. **The live bank still serves zero eligible student questions** (exact-version approval rule unchanged); everything below that needs questions was exercised on a test-only fixture bank and is reported separately.

**Integrity fix.** Accepting a Coach proposal used to mark it executed and then apply tasks one by one, so a failure midway left a half-changed week, and later tasks were silently re-read at a newer revision. `confirm_coach_proposal` (migration `20261111_coach_confirm_and_scope`) now validates every reviewed task (revision, date, not started, not a protected commitment, no continuation, ownership), applies them through the existing atomic `set_task_execution`, and writes the receipt on the proposal, all in one transaction. A repeat returns the same receipt; a stale or old-format proposal changes nothing. Proposals now carry `{version 2, moves[{id,title,revision,date,minutes}], staysDue, waiting}`. Proof (`coach:db:test`, 14/0): three-task apply, one event per task, bystander untouched, replay, 8 concurrent confirmations → one receipt, stale after an edit, started / protected / wrong-day refused, forced failure on the third task rolls everything back, another student gets NOT_FOUND. Limit: the week's existing spreading of deferred work runs after the commit and is best effort.

**Handoff gaps closed.** (1) Scope race: `paper_scope_source(user)` is read before the scope is built and compared inside `start_assessment_paper` after the student's plan rows are locked FOR SHARE; a stale source returns SCOPE_CHANGED and leaves no attempt or form (tested). Limit: confirmations inserted after the compare and before commit are not locked. (2) Freshness: an unknown family cannot count on a re-check (`UNKNOWN_FAMILY`, enforced in pool and RPC; paper pools still treat unknown as independent); questions in any OPENED paper count as exposed even if unanswered, and their family is no longer fresh, in preflight and reservation alike (tested). (3) `e2e-tests-scope` ran with its flags: 19 + 13 passed. `gate_closed` did not recur; its capture (gate, cycle, clock) stays in place.

**Intents (all resolved by code, links to exact task / result / editor).** today, missed today, availability (setup editor with a working `/app/coach` return path), lighter week (recorded adaptation, or says none), result, slow-but-correct (timed practice only when the decision permits; otherwise the honest count plus own-materials practice), mock (checkpoint), teach-me-a-chapter (boundary plus a practical method), plus earlier struggling / redo / next. Missed-today previews exactly what moves, what stays due (started work) and what waits.

**Study guidance.** `src/lib/study/guidance.ts` names scope, why now, activity with the student's own materials, what done means and what to verify; observed, possible explanation and self-report are kept apart; a verified reading link or a resource category, never an invented page. Used by repair tasks (fragile-correct vs misconception), result findings and Coach.

**Timed practice.** `attempt_kind 'practice'`: explicit reading (optionally outcomes), shared preflight, 10 questions at 75 s, strict server deadline, saved answers/confidence and the same deadline across refresh. `finalize_assessment_attempt` forces practice mode: no evidence events, no repair closure, no weekly credit; the result page says so and links back to the originating result. It uses fresh questions, so answered ones are not reused in later papers (stated in the decision). Live bank: preflight refuses with the real count and a study action.

**Prompt and evaluation.** Prompt `coach-2026-11-04.2`; typed small context; the model may only return a validated `{reply}` (one 12 s attempt via the existing provider client), cannot add links, proposals or ids; verified reply is the fallback and the reason is recorded. `npm run coach:eval` (in `verify`): 28 owned cases (tutoring, injection, readiness claims, invented sources/ids, stale state, missing bank, timing, scheduling, mock, plan, model timeout/down/invalid), 28/28 pass; failures are written per case to `scripts/coach-eval/results/<promptVersion>.json` for comparison. The evaluation simulates provider output; no live-model run was made this phase.

**Verification.** Fixture/live journey `e2e-coach-journey` 58/0 (live-bank part and fixture part). Browser (desktop 1280, mobile 390): suggestion click, preview → "Not now" (Calendar unchanged) → confirm (two tasks deferred, started one stays) → Calendar; refresh keeps the receipt; fixture student: Coach "slow" → exact result link → guidance → Start timed practice → refresh keeps answers and clock → result → back; unavailable-bank student: honest wording on desktop and mobile; no overflow. Regression with a 72-item fixture bank: tests-tab 46, activation 47, calendar 30, home 23, entry 46, adaptation 39, weekly-review 31, revision 59, taking-flow 65, results 57, integration 43, coach 44. `paper:db:test` 37/0, `reviewer:db:test` 60, `attempt:race` 8, `repair:db:test` 35, `e2e-paper-journey` 59, `npm run verify` exit 0, migration dry-run 0 pending.

**Remaining / limits.** The `today` intent took several seconds in the browser (it loads the full operating state). No live-model run of the prompt was made. Timed practice is one 10-question shape, reachable from a result's timing finding and from Coach; there is no free-form picker. The "Go to this week's paper" Coach link shows even when the bank cannot serve it (Tests then explains why). `repair:schema:rehearse` still skips its scored journey on the live bank.

### 9.12 Phase 5 — linked mocks, final preparation and exam completion (4 Nov 2026)
**Two tracks, kept apart.** *Actual bank:* 0 approved held-out questions a student could be served, so a full rehearsal is **unavailable** (accurate per-session reasons; external-mock planning, outcome recording and closure all work). *Isolated fixture bank* (`fixture-bank create … --rehearsal=auto`: 204 test-only, human-approved (real `decide_item_review`), held-out (real `set_item_holdout`) questions across all ten topics, never real inventory): both sessions assemble, run, resume, time out and finalise; the combined result leads to a debrief and a Calendar proposal.

**1. Scope-reservation race closed.** Migration `20261112`: every writer of the rows `paper_scope_source` reads (plan blocks, scope confirmations, class sessions/items, module state, evidence) takes ONE per-student advisory lock by trigger (`scope_write_lock`), held to the end of its transaction; `start_assessment_paper` takes the same lock BEFORE it compares (the old `FOR SHARE` on plan rows is gone: it never covered confirmations). `npm run scope:race:test` (two real connections, 15 checks): a confirmation or block completion inserted at the old race point makes the reservation WAIT, then refuse `SCOPE_CHANGED` with **no** attempt, form item, blueprint or snapshot left; a reservation in flight makes a new confirmation wait and the paper reflects the reviewed scope, after which the old source refuses; another student is never delayed; two concurrent writers for one student queue without deadlock; no-source starts (practice/baseline) take no lock. Applies to every start through `start_assessment_paper` (weekly, reading, subject, practice); rehearsal starts do not depend on scope; `start_repair_check` has its own per-task lock and was not changed.

**2. One canonical schedule.** `mock_appointments` (date, optional start time/length, kind incl. `internal_rehearsal`, status planned/completed/cancelled/postponed/missed, version). Backfill keeps the existing checkpoint's id and version, and every earlier checkpoint outcome history points at keeps its id (rehearsed 21/21 in a rolled-back transaction, `npm run linked:rehearse`: related tasks, history and week choice intact). `profiles.mock_*` is now a **projection** of the next planned appointment; a legacy writer (onboarding/Settings) edits that appointment by trigger. `checkpoint_command` gained `add`, addresses an appointment by id+version, keeps replay/stale/receipt semantics; moving/cancelling bumps the version, the weekly token and `onboarding_context_version` (drafts go stale) and Calendar's existing "preparation you added earlier" review lists related tasks. Timed + sized appointments reserve capacity for **every** planned appointment of a day; date-only reserve nothing. Surfaces: Home (next + others, exact links), Calendar (schedule list, add another, `?appointment=<id>`), roadmap (every planned appointment is a checkpoint), Tests (`MockSchedule`), Coach (`mock` intent links the exact appointment and the rehearsal action), Settings (schedule card), weekly review.

**3–4. The linked rehearsal.** Records: appointment (intent) · `mock_rehearsals` + two forms (`assessment_forms.rehearsal_id/session_no`, pinned `form_items.item_version`) · two `assessment_attempts` (`attempt_kind='mock'`, own 135-minute limit, `deadline_at` NULL until explicit start). Blueprint `rehearsal-2026-11-05.1`: each session = exactly 90 questions shared across the topics by exam-weight midpoint (largest remainder), spread across readings, held-out + exact-version approved + unseen + KNOWN family, one question per family across BOTH sessions, questions pinned to another open rehearsal excluded. The **one** capability decision (`decideRehearsal`) gives per-session, per-topic shortfalls (e.g. "Session 1 has 24 of 90 questions: Equities 2 of 11; …"); 180 approved questions in two topics is a named shortfall. `start_rehearsal` (service role) validates and pins both forms in one transaction; the hold-out exception is a transaction-local setting set only inside it (direct form insert → `ITEM_HELD_OUT`; no request field exists; routine papers, practice and re-checks read `student_eligible_items`, which excludes held-out). Taking flow reuses `AttemptRunner`: the hub (`/app/tests/rehearsal/[id]`) shows ready / in progress / break / complete and discloses the confidence-interaction difference; Start sets the deadline once (concurrent starts → one deadline); session 2 only after session 1 ended; an answer before Start is refused; confidence stays mandatory; refresh keeps answers, confidence and deadline; out-of-time sessions are finalised from saved answers (omissions have no response row, no invented confidence, no evidence), an empty one closes `expired`. A browser bug found and fixed: the auto-submit fired every second on a slow response (guarded). Hub render is dominated by `getAttemptResult` (several seconds in dev; computed once now).
**5. Results and debrief.** Combined view only for two ended sessions (a partial shows the finished session and the remaining action, no overall %). Valid answered/correct, omitted, set-aside (disputed/voided/challenged), confidence (incl. sure-and-wrong) and timing are separate. Debrief = at most five evidence-backed items (gap → timing → fragile → omitted), one first action; one Calendar proposal covers both sessions (`previewRepairFollowUp({alsoAttemptIds, limit:5})`, rest kept for later, existing accept command, existing repair-resolution policy, re-checks from ordinary families). External mocks keep the actual-date/self-reported workflow and create no evidence.
**6. Designation.** `cycle_assessment_choices.appointment_id`: one appointment counts per week (UI says which; "another appointment counts" on the others; replace-unstarted-paper confirmation unchanged). Weekly gate `rehearsal`: a designated rehearsal whose second session finished **inside the cycle** closes the week; credit follows that finish (shown on the result and the review). Partial/postponed/missed does not satisfy the gate (`mock_pending`, existing external rules unchanged).
**7. Final stretch and exam.** `examPhase` (own date + own statement): long horizon / final preparation (≤56 days) / final week (≤7) / exam day / awaiting status / sat. `finalStretch` builds a balanced list even with no repair (debrief, fixes, maintenance revision, a selected check, logistics, rehearsal) within the weekly budget, with "waiting, on purpose" reasons; the Practical Skills Module is an optional administrative reminder. After the date Home **asks**; `POST /api/exam` (`record_exam_status`) records "I sat the exam" (never in the future, replay-safe, undoable), pauses Home/Calendar/Coach/review prompts, keeps all history, and offers optional PSM status, a self-reported result and a reviewable retake route (existing exam editor). Never calls an internal score a pass.
**8. Cleanup.** `POST /api/tests/start {kind:'mock'}` → 410 (decision/start code removed; historical mock attempts keep result links); Coach no longer offers "Go to this week's paper" when the bank cannot serve one (says "View assessment availability"); the Coach receipt remains distinct from post-confirmation week spreading (unchanged).

**Migrations applied:** `20261112_linked_mocks`, `20261113_rehearsal_pinned`, `20261114_fix_projection_guard` (found by the fixture run: an unset-setting read failed a profile INSERT), `20261115_exam_completion_command`; dry-run 0 pending.
**Evidence.** `rehearsal:contract:test` 12/0 (pure blueprint); `linked:rehearse` 21/0; `scope:race:test` 15/0; `rehearsal:db:test` 14/0 (guard rails); `e2e-rehearsal-journey --part=fixture` 85/0 (appointments, capability on every surface, concurrent reserve/start, clocks, resume, timeout, break, combined result, debrief→Calendar, challenge, designation + boundary credit, final stretch, exam). Browser (Playwright, desktop 1280 and mobile 390, no horizontal overflow): Calendar schedule → Prepare my rehearsal → Start session 1 (real clicks answering, refresh keeps clock) → Submit → break → Start session 2 → timeout (clock set 25 s ahead) → combined result → Review follow-up → Confirm changes; Tests shortfall state; final-week page; "I sat the exam" → paused Home; external mock "Record what happened".
**Limits (stated plainly).** The actual bank still serves **no** rehearsal. The final stretch is a read-only, actionable list on Home/Exam, not new planner-generated Calendar blocks. The rehearsal hub is slow in dev (result analysis). Retirement of a pinned item after reservation relies on the form's pinned version (not separately exercised). A cycle-boundary rehearsal was tested by moving the completion time, not by sitting across a real boundary. Two abandoned test connections from an earlier failed run kept `sr-…@example.invalid` throwaway users from being deleted until they time out. Held-out fixture inventory (204) supports one full rehearsal per student; a student needs ≥2× each topic's session allocation of distinct families.

### 9.13 Phase 6 — private-beta readiness (5 Nov 2026)
Results are separated into **hosted** (the disposable development project), **local rehearsal** (a throwaway Postgres on loopback) and **fixtures**. Full operations detail: `docs/BETA-OPERATIONS.md`.

**Beta database (local rehearsal; hosted beta project pending).** `.env.local` and `.env.staging.local` identify the SAME disposable project, so they do not establish environment separation; no distinct beta project or credentials exist yet, and `scripts/beta/install-hosted.cjs` refuses the development project. Finding: `supabase/schema.sql` is not the true baseline (its `plan_blocks.id` is `uuid`, the live schema's is `text`; migration 20260905 fails on it). So the installation baseline is generated from the live schema: `npm run beta:baseline` writes `supabase/baseline/schema-snapshot.sql` (public schema, policies, triggers, explicit table and function grants including restricted-function ACLs, hash-checked), `reference-data.sql` (topics, exam windows, curriculum structure: 10/8/102/343; no accounts, attempts, items, concepts or batches) and `baseline-manifest.json` (the 72 covered migrations with checksums). `npm run beta:install:rehearse`: stub platform objects → baseline → runner dry-run **0 pending, ledger 72/72, 0 checksum mismatches** → inventory compared with the live source in 10 categories (tables 86, columns 951, constraints 411, functions 69 with definition hashes, policies 89, triggers 19, views 2, RLS flags, table grants 226, function grants 136): **all identical**. A bug caught by that comparison: `pg_dump -x` loses `REVOKE … FROM PUBLIC` on functions, which would have made service-role-only functions executable by anon on a fresh install; the generator now emits the explicit ACLs. `npm run beta:restore:rehearse` (10/10): dump → restore into a separate database → records, schema, privileges identical; a student reads only their own rows, another student none, anon nothing, direct writes refused, a command after restore works, service-role-only functions still refused to students. **Hosted checks still needed:** create the beta project; run `install-hosted.cjs` preflight/apply; dry-run 0 pending; Supabase Auth URL/SMTP settings; `/api/health` on the deployed app; a Supabase-side backup restore.

**Admission, verification and recovery.** Migration `20261116`: `beta_invitations` (email, role, expiry, status, bound `accepted_user_id`) and `beta_settings.admission` (`open` default; the beta sets `invite_only`). A trigger on `auth.users` **refuses an account for an email with no live invitation** (the database boundary: no page or API can bypass it); a second trigger accepts the invitation only when the email is VERIFIED and binds it to that user id (a reviewer invitation grants `diagnostic_reviewer` only then, through the role table); an accepted invitation cannot be taken by a different identity. API boundary: `requireApplicationAccess` and the four onboarding routes answer `403 NOT_ADMITTED` (fail closed); page boundary: `/app` and `/onboarding` layouts redirect to `/not-invited` (plain steps for expired, used and wrong-address cases); candidates still pass mandatory onboarding before the app. `node scripts/beta/invite.cjs` records invitations (`--role`, `--days`, `--revoke`, `--list`, `--mode`) and **never sends email**. `npm run beta:admission:test` 15/15 (local DB boundary, incl. a migration written after the baseline applying through the runner); live API/page/DB checks in `e2e:beta`. Redirects use the browser's own origin and the existing `safeNextPath`; the environment binding is Supabase Auth's Site URL + redirect allow-list (documented, hosted setting). **Real delivery:** one password-reset request for the authorised test identity was accepted by Supabase Auth; my narrow inbox search found nothing yet and a broader inbox read was denied, so delivery, sign-in, reset-link and expired-link behaviour in a real inbox is **not verified** (owner or hosted check). External invitations remain pending.

**Account controls.** `GET /api/account/export` (own rows only; every table with a `user_id`, plus repair history; says what is not included: question wording, provider backups) — `OWNED_TABLES` is compared with the live schema so a new owned table cannot be forgotten. Deletion is `POST /api/account/delete` with the typed email and a sign-in within 10 minutes (the old immediate `DELETE` returns 410); failure removes nothing and says so; pending reminders are cancelled first; `account_deletions` keeps only a SHA-256 of the id and a time. Verified live: no row remains in any owned table, the other student untouched, the session dies. Privacy and Terms replaced with **beta drafts grounded in actual data use** (Supabase, an email provider, an optional AI model provider for Coach wording, retention, controls, what stays after deletion); support/privacy contact is `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPPORT_EMAIL` (unset = an honest note, flagged by `/api/health`) — **owner review of both drafts and the support address are on the release checklist**.

**Reminders.** Opt-in assessment (evening before a mock/rehearsal) and weekly-review-ready reminders (`reminder_prefs`, `reminder_jobs`, migration 20261116). Pure rules (`reminders:test` 18/18): timezone, quiet hours incl. wrap past midnight, exam-complete pause, durable job keys. Scheduler re-derives wanted jobs from live state (moves cancel `obsolete` jobs); the worker claims jobs by lease (`claim_reminder_jobs`, one winner per row), re-checks relevance at send time (opt-out, moved/finished, exam sat → cancelled with a reason; quiet hours → rescheduled), sends through a transport and records `sent` / `failed` / **`uncertain`** (timeout after the request left: never auto-retried). Link = the exact appointment or `/app/review-week`. `POST /api/reminders/run` is authorised by `CRON_SECRET` only. Verified with a **controlled transport** (no email sent): one job after repeated syncs, move reschedules, exactly-once send, retry sends nothing, uncertain recorded, quiet hours defer, exam-complete and opt-out cancel (`e2e:beta`, 35/0 together with the controls above). Real-provider delivery is pending provider configuration.

**Operability.** `GET /api/health` (database, schema version vs `EXPECTED_SCHEMA_VERSION`, admission mode, config flags; no secrets or student data), redacted one-line JSON trace events (`src/lib/ops/trace.ts`) for auth verification, paper/rehearsal start and submit, reminders, account deletion; deployment sequence, recovery, support checklist and release checklist in `docs/BETA-OPERATIONS.md`.

**Phase 5 carry-overs.** Four leftover race fixtures (`sr-…`) deleted by recorded id once the abandoned session cleared; removal verified. The race harness now sets lock, statement and idle-in-transaction timeouts on every connection, rolls back in-flight connections first in `finally`, and handles SIGINT/SIGTERM. The final stretch links an accepted repair or revision task when one exists, otherwise the existing proposal flow (the follow-up proposal or the next weekly review), and writes nothing (`stretch:test` 5/5).

**Late findings and the rest of the evidence (Phase 6).**
- *Baseline gap found by the admission test:* triggers on `auth.users` (the invite gate, the invitation acceptance and the signup profile trigger) live outside `public`, so a `public`-only snapshot would have installed WITHOUT the admission gate. The generator now carries them (inventory category `authTriggers` 3/3) and seeds `beta_settings` (`open`; the beta sets `invite_only` explicitly). Baseline now covers 73 migrations; install rehearsal 11/11 categories identical, restore 10/10, admission 15/15.
- *Phase 5 carry-overs:* Calendar suite on an isolated ordinary approved bank (70 items, created and destroyed by recorded id): **30/0** (one earlier run crashed on a transient magic-link sign-in in journey 3 and passed on rerun); the same suite on the ACTUAL bank (no approved question) passes its non-paper journeys and fails the four paper-dependent assertions with the accurate reason "bank can serve 0 fresh human-reviewed questions" (6 passed / 4 failed) — that is the unavailable case, not a defect. Retirement after reservation (`e2e-retirement`, 11/0): the pinned form, stems, versions and saved answers are unchanged; the student can still answer and submit; the retired item with a recorded approval still counts as evidence under the established rule; the existing challenge sets it aside. Shown on a weekly paper (same pinning/finalise/challenge path as a mock session); a 180-question rehearsal was not rebuilt for it.
- *Browser (Playwright, desktop 1280 / mobile 390, no horizontal overflow):* landing shows the invitation-only note; Settings shows reminders (opt-in saved by click), Download my data, Delete Account; the deletion dialog stays disabled until the account email is typed, then deleted the account and returned to the landing page (row removal verified; the one console error is the expected 403 from signing out an already-deleted session); reviewer account reaches the workspace; a student is redirected away from `/app/tests/review` and refused by the review APIs (403). Entry journeys earlier in the phase: `e2e:beta` 35/0.
- *Measured (development server, remote database; not the beta host):* Coach `today` 8.8 s → ~5.8 s after starting its independent reads together (remaining cost is the number of sequential reads; not blocking, not eliminated). The rehearsal hub was measured at 6.7–13 s under load in Phase 5 and its double result computation was removed then; it could not be re-measured here because the held-out fixture bank is gone. **Live-provider Coach check:** one free-form message returned in 14.8 s with `fallbackReason: "empty"` — the configured model returned nothing usable, so the verified deterministic reply was served (the fallback works; the live model did not produce a validated reply on this call). `/api/health` answers in ~0.3 s and reports `support contact`, `email provider`, `reminder worker`, `site url` as NOT configured here.
- *Not done / blocked on the owner or hosted access:* distinct beta project and its install; Supabase Auth URL/SMTP settings; real inbox delivery for sign-up, verification, reset, expired-link and reminder emails (one reset request was accepted by Supabase Auth, but my inbox search found nothing and a broader inbox read was denied); credential rotation confirmation; owner approval of Privacy/Terms and the support address; the cohort stays closed (no external invitation was sent).

### 9.14 Onboarding entry journey (5 Nov 2026)

Fixed: open sittings are selectable until they close and booked dates are validated against the sitting and today (`lib/onboarding/examWindows.ts`, shared by the page, `saveExam` and the form); the preview version is read before the planning inputs, a draft that was overtaken by an edit is retired at birth, concurrent requests share one build, and acceptance refuses a draft from an older setup inside the transaction (migration `20261117`); acceptance is off while the preview is building, failed or invalidated, with Rebuild preview and plain-language errors; edits opened from Review return to Review; Review shows booked vs target month, saved classes with the next one-off's date/time and whether it set the first cycle, one assessment vocabulary (`lib/tests/assessmentStage.ts`, also used by Calendar and Home), grouped split sittings; compact coverage summary for "first pass complete"; self-study Sunday labelled as paper and review; no-availability completes with the 90-minute default shown; next/previous week from a variable-class cycle uses `adjacentWeek` through `weekForDate`; accepted empty cycles are recognised from draft provenance (`lib/planning/cycleProvenance.ts`) with an Add availability link to `/onboarding/week?returnTo=/app/calendar`; Home no longer says "all caught up" for that state; the roadmap milestone is labelled separately from the topics scheduled.

Measured (dev): build about 4.5–6 s (week plan about 1.8–2.3 s, roadmap inputs about 0.7–1.3 s, week context about 0.3–0.7 s); reuse about 0.4–1.5 s. No cache added. A context computed before the version read cannot be passed into generation without reopening the race.

Open: adding windows to an accepted empty cycle does not itself plan that cycle (Calendar keeps the accepted-empty notice); journeys 2 and 6 were covered by the API suite (`e2e:onboarding`, 86/0), not clicked in a browser; the actual bank still serves 0 questions, so every Review shows "Coverage unavailable".

### 9.15 Home reliability and simplification (5 Nov 2026)

State model: `lib/state/homeModel.ts` builds one ordered work list from the cycle's blocks (today, overdue, undated) with explicit tie-breakers; the selector, attention queue and Today list all read it. Assessment availability is separate from its due day (`confirm_scope`, `upcoming`, `ready`, `running`, `unavailable`, `scored`): confirm-scope no longer counts as a sufficient bank, so "paper is ready" only appears for a paper that can be started, on its due day. The bridge closes the week for all four canonical gates (scored paper, no-paper, external mock, rehearsal). Attention items are deduplicated by identity (`ATTENTION_FOR_ACTION`), have producers for confirm coverage, scored result, unplaced and overdue work, and are returned whole (the page shows three). Partial work is counted as partial with its reported minutes. Deferral returns what actually happened. Progress is four separate statements (`progressSummary.ts`): reported coverage (readings), understanding (too little / out of date / disputed, never "No tests yet" after a paper), this week, confidence (no ring under five answers).

Page: next action, three remaining rows, three decisions, compact progress with detail on request, week and roadmap folded. Failed loaders render a retry section instead of an empty state. Exam sat: follow-up and history, no countdown or checkpoint.

Verification: `home:state:test` (33), `e2e:home` (23), `e2e:home:journeys` (19, fixture; two runs after one transient 503/loader failure that did not recur), full `verify`.

Open: proposals and repairs open Coach with a contextual question, not the selected object (Coach has no object selection); a deferral of today's work is not rescheduled automatically; live scored-paper, running-paper and repair journeys were not run in the browser (no question bank).

### 9.16 Empty-week planning, atomic setup, and the Home handoff contract (5 Nov 2026)

**Flow (built).** Onboarding without study times → accepted empty week → Calendar explains it and offers *Add study times* (`/onboarding/week?returnTo=%2Fapp%2Fcalendar`) → saving returns to Calendar (no impact screen when the week holds no planned work) and offers *Review a plan for this week* → `POST /api/planning/current-week/preview` prepares a versioned proposal (`plan_drafts`, purpose `current_week_plan`, bound to the existing cycle) from the same planner and task mapping as the first week (`lib/planning/draftTasks.ts`) → `POST /api/planning/current-week/accept` calls `accept_current_week_plan`, which adds the tasks to the SAME cycle row. `POST …/dismiss` = *Keep the week empty*. Saving study times never plans; only accepting does. The cycle's identity and dates are never touched, the original onboarding receipt (`plan_drafts` accepted row) is untouched, and the accepted proposal row is the record of the later planning action; a repeat returns its stored receipt.

**Refusals (all in the database, one transaction).** Not the owner / not the cycle's draft; stale revision; cycle no longer active (`CYCLE_CHANGED`) or ended (`CYCLE_ENDED`, points at weekly review); the day changed; setup version changed; or `current_week_source_hash` moved (tasks incl. recorded learning and manual work, commitments, availability, classes, reported coverage, sitting length). Each refusal returns a rebuild action. Concurrent acceptance serialises on the draft row: one result.

**Atomic setup (migration 20261118).** `onboarding_commit_step(user, step, args)` does the exam/route/position/week change and the version increment in one transaction, locking the profile row first (the row acceptance locks). `saveExam/Route/Position/Week` use it; `bumpContextVersion` is gone. Not covered: `profiles.diagnostic_day`, a derived snapshot for older readers, is still written right after the route commit (planning derives the day itself).

**Mock profile path (migrations 20261119, 20261120).** Setup/Settings clearing the next mock bypassed the Calendar rules: a started rehearsal could be cancelled, and cancelling left its unstarted rehearsal and week choice behind. Both now hold on that path (`REHEARSAL_STARTED`). 20261119 itself broke every profile INSERT on the development database (it read an unset setting without `missing_ok`; signup failed) until 20261120 corrected it; the beta baseline does not include 20261117–20261120 yet.

**Contract for Home (read `state.weekPlan` from `getCurrentOperatingState`; present only when the active cycle has no tasks).** Source: `lib/planning/currentWeek/state.ts`, `getCurrentWeekPlanState`.

| `weekPlan.kind` | Meaning | Action (label → destination) |
|---|---|---|
| `no_study_times` | No windows saved (mode none/varies) | Add study times → `/onboarding/week?returnTo=%2Fapp%2Fcalendar` |
| `ready_to_plan` | Windows saved, valid time remains, no proposal | Review a plan for this week → `/app/calendar?plan=review` (opens the review panel) |
| `proposal_ready` | Current proposal open (`draft.id`, `draft.revision`) | Review the plan → `/app/calendar?plan=review` |
| `proposal_stale` | Proposal exists but the week/setup moved | Rebuild the plan → `/app/calendar?plan=review` |
| `plan_accepted` | A reviewed plan was added to this cycle | none |
| `intentionally_empty` | Kept empty (same source state) or accepted empty in weekly review | Review a plan for this week → `/app/calendar?plan=review` |
| `no_usable_windows` | Windows exist, none remain. `reason`: `later_week`, `assessment_day_only` (self-study Sunday), `time_used`, `nothing_to_add` | Edit study times → `/onboarding/week?returnTo=%2Fapp%2Fcalendar` |
| `cycle_ended` | Active cycle's last day has passed | Review the week → `/app/review-week` |
| `not_applicable` | The week has planned work, or no accepted cycle | none |

Assessment unavailable is a separate dimension: `state.assessment.state === 'unavailable'`; wording `PAPER_UNAVAILABLE_BANK` ("A weekly paper is unavailable for this scope because checked questions are missing."), links: View assessment availability → `/app/tests`, plan around it → `/app/calendar`. Home should not show `weekPlan` actions as "Start …"; they are planning actions. After acceptance Calendar and Home read the same blocks, so both reflect it on the next request.

**Open.** The proposal ignores minutes already taken by tasks added by hand (acceptance refuses if any appear after preview, but a proposal built beside existing tasks does not subtract them); a class-led starting-fresh student gets "nothing to add" (the planner does not schedule new content for classes-lead routes), so class/window overlap was proven at capacity level (180 → 60 min), not by proposed tasks; beta baseline needs regenerating.

### 9.17 Home correctness: time, closure, assessment controls, exact objects, deferral (5 Nov 2026)

**Reported time (`lib/state/reportedTime.ts`, `homeModel.ts`).** Planned, reported and remaining are three numbers. A report with no minutes is unknown (`reportedMinutes: null`, `timeUnknown`), never 0. Repeated updates add; Undo reverses exactly the report it undid (`undid_event_id`, an undo of an undo restores it; older undo events reverse by the type they name). Daily activity is dated by when the report was made (student's local day), so late reporting on an earlier task counts on the day reported while the task's history stays with its own week. A partly done task whose reported time reaches its plan stays open and says its planned allocation is used (`plannedUsedButOpen`). Confidence: the percentage's denominator is the "sure" answers (`calibration.sureSample`); under five, no percentage and the sample is stated.

**Recommendation and closure.** Overdue work sorts by date and time before the partly-done tie-break. `state.closure` carries the canonical gate with its reason (scored paper, external mock, rehearsal, permitted no-paper closure, or what is pending). A scored result stops being recommended once opened (`assessment_attempts.result_viewed_at`, migration 20261121, set by `POST /api/tests/attempt/[id]/viewed`) and no repair from it still needs a decision. Timer wording comes from the attempt record (`lib/tests/clockPolicy.ts`: a timed session cannot be paused; an untimed one can). Early papers stay secondary until due; starting early is in Tests, resume is primary.

**Assessment tasks.** A paper or re-check task gets Start / Resume / See result / Waiting / Unavailable (`lib/state/assessmentControl.ts`, from the assessment decision and `planRepairCheck`), never Done/Partly done, on Home and in the Calendar drawer. Authoritative boundary: migration 20261122 makes `set_task_execution` refuse completed/partial/in_progress for `diagnostic` and `recheck` tasks (`SYSTEM_ASSESSMENT`, 409); deferring and skipping remain. The external-mock reporting flow is untouched.

**Exact objects.** `/app/coach?proposal=<id>` and `/app/coach?repair=<id>` (validated UUIDs, read by owner; another student's id and a missing one both read "not available"). The card shows status, the exact changes, Calendar links for repair work and the re-check; Confirm uses the existing confirm endpoint. Home links use these.

**Deferral and redistribution (migration 20261123).** The old `redistributeWeek` added minutes to the first row of every remaining day on every call (capacity 9999, no sitting limit, any reading, repeatable). Replaced by `redistributeMissedTask`: only into later untouched sessions of the SAME reading, within each day's real free time and the student's sitting limit (180 ceiling), at most once per missed task (receipt per source task), all-or-nothing in `apply_redistribution`, receipt of what was committed. Undo or choosing another time takes back exactly those minutes (`reverse_redistribution`) and says what was kept. Deferral of today's task moves nothing automatically. "Choose another time" is the Calendar drawer's existing move flow; placing a deferred task reopens it (`placed` event) with history, reported minutes and reading kept. Coordinate with the Calendar builder: `moveTask` now reopens deferred tasks; `setExecution` returns `outcome`.

**Measured (dev, TRACE_READS=1, `scripts/home-measure.ts`).** Home: 249 → 148 database reads per request (140 → 39 exact repeats), about 4.0–4.5 s → 3.1–3.5 s, by sharing getStudyWeekContext, getSignals, getActiveRoadmap, getLosTracker and getCompletedCycleScope within the request (`lib/ops/perRequest.ts`, no cross-request or cross-user state). Remaining repeats are calendar_commitments (15), profiles (12), planning_cycles (12) from per-day capacity and per-service reads.

**Verification.** `home:state:test` 57, `pulse:redistribute:test` 7, `e2e:home` 23, `e2e:home:journeys` 44 (e2e account; recorded-id cleanup), `e2e:home:assessments` 24 (actual bank 4, fixture bank 20; fixtures removed). Browser (e2e account, ordinary student fixtures): desktop and 390px, keyboard operation, refresh persistence for unknown/overrun partials, Choose another time → confirm → refresh, two-tab stale refusal with typed minutes kept, Coach proposal selection (dismiss persisted), missing and cross-account objects. Fixture-bank journeys (running paper → resume → result → repair → re-check) were verified over HTTP/HTML against the fixture bank, not clicked in the browser.

**Open.** Placing a deferred task on an earlier day is allowed by the existing drawer (it will read overdue); redistribution does not subtract hand-placed timed sessions beyond day totals; the beta baseline does not include migrations 20261117–20261123; proposals created by the repair loop do not record `applied_task_ids`, so their card links the accepted work through the repair object instead.

### 9.18 Onboarding/current-week closeout and Home integration (5 Nov 2026)

**Save outcomes.** `EmptyWeekPlan` now checks the actual dismissal outcome; a failed, malformed or lost response cannot report “kept empty”. Accept and dismiss retain the original draft/revision and proposal in owner-keyed session storage until the outcome is confirmed. Refresh preserves an uncertain save even if the server has already accepted the plan. Retrying returns the stored receipt. A dismissal racing an acceptance reports the accepted receipt. The `plan=review` deep link is consumed, so keeping a week empty does not reopen its proposal on refresh. Ended/changed cycles link to their recovery destination rather than offering an unusable rebuild. Both command routes validate UUIDs and revision bounds and handle missing drafts consistently.

**Inputs and capacity (migration 20261124).** Current-week source identity includes commitment exceptions and recurrence bounds, the accepted cycle, planning profile fields, learning/activity and assessment inputs. The existing per-user scope barrier now covers the added input writers. Parent setup/closure/checkpoint commands acquire it before their row locks. The acceptance command validates verified/setup/admission access in SQL, then checks the entire proposal under the lock against current input identity, cycle dates and the actual clock. Remaining capacity unions overlapping availability windows, subtracts protected occurrences (including skips/moves and recurrence limits), mocks and elapsed time. A plan that no longer fits is refused as stale; out-of-cycle/invalid tasks refuse the whole proposal. No invalid task is silently dropped.

**Atomic derived setup (20261125).** `diagnostic_day` is now written inside `onboarding_commit_step` for exam/route edits; the second application write is gone. The active cycle's closing day wins. Otherwise the snapshot follows the longest-gap class rule, a nearby confirmed variable class, or Sunday.

**Profile and repair writers (20261126).** `/api/profile` and `/api/profile/classes-completed` use the restricted, service-only `update_planning_profile` command. It acquires the planning lock before taking the profile row lock, preserves omitted fields, and cannot patch protected onboarding fields. Class completion requires an explicit boolean; malformed bodies cannot accidentally mark classes finished. Repair threads participate in the input barrier. Coach/repair/redistribution parent commands acquire it before their row locks as well. The full-schema tests exercise both orders of profile edit versus acceptance, including the no-deadlock case.

**Class-led and Home connections.** A class-led fresh starter is directed to log a class, see the known schedule, or add the next class instead of receiving an unhelpful “nothing to add”. One-off variable classes are recognised by class logging. Home consumes `weekPlan` as its planning action, with its own label and destination; the older availability/next-class decisions no longer duplicate it. Intentionally empty weeks retain planning as a secondary option. Calendar surfaces a failed current-week status read with Retry. The next-class deep link now works in an empty Calendar as well as the grid, responds to repeated navigation, and consumes only its action parameter without navigating the newly opened dialog away.

**Accepted dates.** Browser testing a class-pattern change exposed a separate defect: `getStudyWeekContext` could recalculate a different current range and call an accepted week unplanned. Live context now uses the active cycle's actual dates/timezone until closure, including an ended cycle awaiting review. Date navigation continues to resolve the requested date. `weekForDate` shares the same stored-cycle reconstruction.

**Verification on the final implementation.** Full `npm run verify` exits 0; clean isolated production build passes; the migration dry-run reports 0 pending with no checksum drift. Focused results: current-week pure 24, Home pure 61, full-schema current-week concurrency 18, live current-week 30, boundary scenarios 12, onboarding HTTP 90, Home HTTP 23 and Home journeys 44 — all zero failures. Earlier setup concurrency 13 and onboarding/mock checks 16 also passed during this slice. Profile HTTP checks prove persistence, omitted-field preservation, explicit completion/reset and malformed-body refusal; current-week HTTP checks prove malformed UUID/body/revision refusal and missing-draft responses. The onboarding history assertion now reads real cycle columns and throws on failed snapshot reads instead of comparing two empty results.

**Browser evidence (ordinary disposable students).** Self-study: empty accepted week → Home Add study times → save → review → keep empty, refresh and deliberately reopen. Injected failed dismissal retained a retry after refresh. An acceptance committed but received deliberately invalid JSON: refresh preserved the original request, retry returned its receipt, and exactly nine tasks existed afterwards. The mobile viewport was 390px with a 390px document width. Class-led: saved study windows → log covered outcome → preview a 15-minute consolidation → confirm → Calendar task. A one-off variable-class context was also checked against the service. Empty next-class dialog opened, closed, reopened by keyboard and stayed closed after refresh; accepted Wednesday–Tuesday dates remained intact after removing the recurring pattern. No errors/warnings appeared in the final normal-origin browser session. Screenshots are in `/tmp/roadmap-save-confirmed-mobile.png`, `/tmp/roadmap-class-followup-mobile.png` and `/tmp/roadmap-next-class-dialog.png`.

**Environment and cleanup.** Migrations 20261124–20261126 are applied through the checksum runner to the owner-confirmed disposable development project `asylsxszdhlnzewhwtga`. No production/deployment/account-provider work was performed. Temporary students were deleted by recorded ID; the owned dev server, test proxy and temporary local database were stopped by recorded handles. Only this session's two generated Next directories were removed; canonical generated types were restored. No commit or push.

**Limits and next plan.** This closes the current-week/onboarding handoff, not every Calendar or assessment feature. Current-week proposal preparation is still for accepted weeks without planned work; merging new plans into an already populated week is not implemented. The live question bank was not changed or published. Fixture-bank paper/re-check browser completion, timed-slot redistribution, applied repair-proposal task links, remaining request-read duplication and baseline refresh are covered in `HOME-CALENDAR-CLOSEOUT-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md`, drafted from the new Home handoff. That plan has not been executed. Deployment, OAuth/domain/email operations remain deferred.

### 9.18 Home and Calendar final integration (6 Nov 2026)

**Ledger.** `schema_migrations` is at `20261127`; 0 pending. 20261124–20261126 (current-week consistency, atomic closing snapshot, profile writers) were already applied by the Calendar work and were not touched.

**Redistribution against real slots (migration 20261127; `lib/planning/pulse/redistribute.ts`).** A timed sitting grows only at its END and only while the whole extended interval stays inside a study window, clear of every occupant (`occupantsFor`: commitments with moved/skipped occurrences, classes, other timed tasks) and mocks with a start time, and not already under way; day-preferred work still fits the day's remaining budget. Same-reading matching, sitting limit and the 180-minute ceiling are kept. The commit (`apply_redistribution`) now takes the shared planning lock (`scope_lock`), refuses unless `current_week_source_hash` (taken before planning) still matches, and refuses an overlap of two timed tasks even from a caller that skips the planner; the service recomputes once on a stale refusal, then says nothing moved. `reverse_redistribution` takes the same lock. Messages state the minutes still deferred when absorption is partial.

**Past-day placement (audit).** No founder decision allows retrospective placement (the only related rule forbids inventing past Calendar tasks or time). `moveTask` now refuses to move unfinished work to a day before today ("Choose today or a later day… use Log learning"); already-dated work may be resized or stay; completed work and all history/links are unchanged. The drawer lists only today and later for unfinished work.

**Applied repair proposals link their real tasks.** `lib/coach/selection.ts` reads `accept_receipt.taskIds` (written by `accept_repair_proposal`), shows each task's state (open/done/removed/continued-as), says "no longer available" for a deleted task and "added no tasks" for an empty receipt, and links nothing for pending/dismissed/stale/expired proposals. No new field or receipt table; `applied_task_ids` stays unused.

**Other fixes found in the browser pass.** "Complete missing logs" no longer outranks a session still to do today or a due re-check (only overdue-only work lets it lead). The re-check drawer said to start it in Tests; the Start button is in the drawer, and the text now says so. Capacity inputs (windows, commitments, exceptions, mocks) are read once per request instead of once per day.

**Measured (dev, same fixture, `scripts/home-measure.ts`, TRACE_READS=1).** Home database reads 152 → 128 per request, exact repeats 43 → 19 (249 / 140 before request-scoped sharing). Timings were 2.3–5.3 s and are not comparable run to run: a second builder's dev server was running on the same machine. profiles (12) and planning_cycles (12) repeats remain.

**Baseline.** `beta:baseline` regenerated from the verified chain (84 migrations covered); `beta:install:rehearse` (installed vs source identical in every category incl. 158 function grants), `beta:restore:rehearse` 10/10 and `beta:admission:test` 15/15 pass on a throwaway local Postgres.

**Fixture-bank browser journey (ordinary student, desktop, test-only reviewed bank, removed by recorded id; actual bank unaffected).** Home before due (paper secondary) → Tests "Start weekly paper" early → mandatory confidence (Next disabled until chosen) → refresh resumes at the first unanswered question with answers kept → Home "Resume your diagnostic paper" to the same attempt with the real clock policy → submit → one scored result (8 evidence events) → Home result with `result_viewed_at` set yet still primary while 5 repairs need a decision → "Review follow-up" preview (no tasks, one pending proposal) → "Keep for later" → preview → Confirm (6 items) → Coach `?proposal=` lists the 6 tasks from its receipt, opened a Calendar drawer by keyboard; `?repair=` shows Scheduled → re-check refused before repairs and before spacing, cannot be marked done → after the (fixture time-shifted) spacing Home shows Start check → drawer Start re-check by keyboard → 15 unseen items, no shared item or family with the paper.

**Not run or not clicked.** Actual-bank journey in the browser (verified over HTTP in `e2e:home:assessments`); week closure by external mock and by rehearsal (unit and earlier-phase tests only); interrupted connection after commit; changed classes / next class outside the displayed week; the 390px width for the paper/result/repair pages (only Coach cards and Home were checked at 390px); a second-tab stale edit on the paper pages.

**Observed, not changed.** After "Keep for later" the next preview in the same page says "Nothing fits in this week" because the kept repairs are excluded until reload. With the 547-item bank present the Tests page took up to 89 s once and hit a statement timeout once on this machine under contention; the eligible-items query itself runs in 0.3 s, so the cause is not isolated.

#### Curriculum audit: starting map (nothing redesigned)

Current actions on `/app/curriculum` and what they change: **exam target picker** (`POST /api/roadmap`) re-bases the roadmap; **coverage edits** (`POST /api/curriculum/coverage`, Roadmap view) change reported coverage, the roadmap and revision rotation, not the accepted week and never evidence; **confidence marks** (`/api/curriculum/marks`) feed calibration only; **manual status override** (`/api/curriculum/overrides`) changes a reading's band with a recorded reason; **notes** and **flashcards** are study aids with no effect elsewhere; **readiness tracker** links to the reading page, `Tests#scope-h` (confirm coverage) and Tests; **revision status** links "Open it in Calendar".
