**Findings reproduced and fixed**
- **A. Deletion.** Storage cleanup now walks nested folders and every page under `<userId>/` (collect first, remove after, so offset paging does not skip), through the Storage API only; ownership is exactly the `<user id>/` prefix (a `<user id>x/` lookalike and loose files are untouched); an unlistable folder fails the cleanup instead of deleting the account over uninspected files. The route is phase-aware: `CLEANUP_INCOMPLETE` / `DELETE_FAILED` now carry `accountDeleted:false` and `filesRemoved`, and the text says the account and database rows are still there, files already removed cannot be restored and a retry continues. Both audit writes (`failed`, `deleted`) check the returned `{error}` as well as exceptions, log without secrets and never change the authoritative outcome. Proof: `account-storage-cleanup-test` 7/0 (pure: folders, 250 files over several pages, nested, partial failure then retry, other student's objects); `settings-delete-combined-http-test` 7/0 on real Storage + Auth + Postgres (105+ nested files, real cascade failure through a temporary trigger → 500 with `accountDeleted:false`, `filesRemoved` = all, account/rows intact, retry succeeds, one failed + one deleted audit row, another student's object kept). The 12-minute aged-session test's wording assertion was updated but it was **not rerun** (auth code unchanged).
- **B. Setup versioning on the server.** `commitStep` (so every internal caller too) now refuses a completed student's planning edit with a missing or malformed `expectedVersion` (`version_required` / `version_invalid`, 400, nothing written); first-time onboarding and name edits are unaffected. The route passes the raw value (no more silent `null`). After commit, a failed next-step read or any later failure returns the authoritative committed version with `followUpUnavailable` and `impact:{unavailable:true}` (never a generic failed save). The impact is bound to its own save: before-snapshot must belong to version v−1 and the after-snapshot to v, otherwise `unavailable`; unreadable scheduled-minute reads are errors, not zero load. `/api/profile` is name-only (no consumer used the planning branch except two tests, updated): retired fields are rejected explicitly (`RETIRED_FIELD`), GET returns only `name`. Lock order: `onboarding_commit_step_checked` took the profile row lock before the inner command's `scope_lock`; it now takes `scope_lock` first (new migration). Proof: `settings-setup-version-http-test` 26/0 (missing/malformed/stale refused with nothing changed, four rounds of checked edit vs commitment write vs class transition with no 5xx, and six overlapping two-connection rounds of a `scope_lock`-then-row-lock writer vs the checked command with no 40P01 and both complete). The old function was not shown deadlocking (no red run for the lock-order test).
- **C. Classes finished / undo.** `set_classes_finished` now increments the existing `onboarding_context_version` on a material transition only (series ended/restored or date changed; a repeat does not). Undo restores a series' remembered end only while it is still active and still ends on the finished date; a series the student edited or archived since is kept and returned in `kept` (reason edited/archived); an undo that would put a class over a booked mock is refused whole (409 `UNDO_CONFLICT`, conflicts listed, `reviewHref:/app/calendar`, nothing changed). Proof: `settings-classes-finished-http-test` 21/0 (version bump, no-op repeat, stale editor refused, edited series kept, newly added series, archived untouched, mock conflict atomic then succeeds, concurrent finish+undo coherent).
- **D. Reminders.** A job is now moved by one atomic, lease-guarded write to `uncertain`/`dispatching` BEFORE the provider is called, so a crash, timeout or failed recording can never auto-resend; the outcome is recorded with retries and reported honestly (`uncertain` tally) when it cannot be; a late worker whose lease was replaced fails that write and sends nothing. `savePrefs` returns `sync:'unknown'` when a source is unreadable; timezone read, job read, cancel and revive/reschedule writes now throw instead of being ignored. `reminder-worker-scoped-test` 25/0 (new: provider accepted then record lost → uncertain, never retryable and never claimed again; dispatching marker visible and unclaimable during the call; timeout after acceptance). A job left `uncertain/dispatching` with no provider id may never have been sent: it needs an operator look; no UI for that exists. Real post-save-reload and source-read failures still have no executable injection (a public failure flag is prohibited): covered by code and by the pure `unknown` rule only.
- **E. Export dedup.** No schema relationship links a mock task to an appointment, so the date-based omission was removed: two appointments on one date and an unrelated mock task on that date export as three events with distinct UIDs; preview count equals parsed events. `settings-export-http-test` 28/0.

**Migration acceptance:** `npm run beta:upgrade:rehearse` (baseline + checksum runner applying 20261219–20261223 on a throwaway local Postgres): 0 pending after, ledger 110 rows, 0 checksum mismatches, tables/columns/constraints/functions/policies/triggers/views/RLS/table and function grants all equal the live schema; `beta:restore:rehearse` 10 checks passed. Rollback is the runner's per-migration transaction (not separately fault-injected).

**Browser:** the existing Settings spec still passes at desktop and 390px (6/6 each) after these changes; the first combined run had one unreproduced desktop failure in the calendar-download step (preview count vs parsed events) that passed on two reruns.

**Not done (concrete consequences)**
- No new browser coverage: lost deletion response, combined deletion failure, two-tab version refusal for the new refusals, class-undo conflict and the changed controls are proven at endpoint level only.
- The field-level effect matrix (exam target→booked/moved with Home/Curriculum/Calendar/Coach agreement and refused-then-re-prepared proposal; availability conflict leading to a Calendar review proposal; coverage/uncertainty effects; Tests scope) was not extended; only the earlier §9.35 render-level proof stands.
- Keyboard/focus/IME composition checks on edited controls were not done.
- Lost-response reconciliation for setup edits is limited: a retry after a lost commit is refused as stale (`setup_changed`) and the UI tells the student to reload; it does not compare the saved payload to say "that was yours".
- Fault injection of `readCanonicalState` after commit has no executable test (no public flag); the code path exists.
- Retention and legal wording remain draft owner decisions; Google/reset-email return paths unexercised.

### 9.38 B3: safe class scope and Calendar class editing (15 Nov 2026) — PARTIAL at that point; its recovery, effective-date, history-failure and browser gaps are closed in §9.39 (read both)
Nothing committed. One additive migration, **20261224_class_change_command**, applied to the disposable project through the checksum runner (the only pending file). Journey B stays partial (B4 items untouched).

**§1 Partial scope (pure + live endpoint evidence)**
- Reproduced: a lone or repeated `continued` class item became a 0.5-weight target; a next-week continuation (which resets `class_session_id`) was admitted because provenance was looked up only for the active cycle; `priorClass` made earlier part/continued items a "completed first pass".
- Representation: only a class item logged "All of it" (`full`) says the whole outcome was covered. `part` and `continued` are progress and stay in `pendingPortions` (now carrying `extent`). Historical `continued` rows are read the same conservative way for FUTURE reservations; reserved/scored papers keep their frozen scope. No rows were rewritten.
- Provenance follows task lineage (`class_session_id` through `continued_from_task_id`, any week); nothing restores an old `class_session_id`. Class items from earlier weeks are read only to keep a portion visible (with its original class date). Explicit whole coverage = the existing scope confirmation on the class-lineage task (surfaced as an unresolved `class_part` block with the confirm form), a separate whole-outcome task, or a later `full` class item. `priorClass` now counts only earlier `full` items; earlier part consolidations no longer make the first whole coverage a revision.
- Evidence: `tests:scope:test` 75/0 (new: lone/repeated continued, successor, study→review, split, explicit confirm, separate task, prior whole); `scripts/class-scope-rollover-test.ts` 16/0 live through the REAL weekly preview/accept (successors have null `class_session_id`, scope still excludes both outcomes, pending visible with original date, confirmation makes only one outcome a NEW target and reaches the blueprint, clearing it returns to pending, a later `full` class confirms the other, evidence count and last week's rows unchanged). Not run against the old code (no red run). Two outcomes of one reading stand in for the "two concepts": no concept-level selection exists, and none is claimed.

**Calendar class editing — final contracts**
- `GET /app/calendar?commitment=<id>&occurrence=<YYYY-MM-DD>[&week=]` (helper `calendarClassHref` / `calendarClassSelectionHref`): server-validated by `getOccurrenceSelection`; malformed, foreign, archived, non-recurring or non-session links show `[data-class-unavailable]` and open nothing. Entries carry `commitmentId` + ORIGINAL `originalDate` (not title/displayed date). Cancelled classes for the viewed week are listed with links so they can be restored.
- `POST /api/commitments/[id]/preview` (read-only) and `PATCH /api/commitments/[id]` with `action` ∈ `occurrence_move | occurrence_cancel | occurrence_restore | series_from`, `operationKey`, `fingerprint`, optional `expectedToken`, `acknowledgeExceptions`. DB functions `class_change_preview` / `class_change_apply` (service role only): strict validation (impossible dates, non-sessions, bad times/ranges/weekdays, past dates, no-change, overlap with same series) with no mutation; `scope_lock` first; receipts in `checkpoint_commands` (`class_change`): same key+payload replays the receipt, same key+different payload = `KEY_REUSED`; stale fingerprint = `STALE_PREVIEW` with the fresh preview; booking guards still run in the same transaction (BK409 → rollback, series/exception intact). `series_from` ends the old series the day before the effective date and creates a successor (earlier classes untouched); later exceptions that still match are copied, the rest stay inert and must be acknowledged (`EXCEPTIONS_UNRESOLVED`); it moves `onboarding_context_version`. Retired: the unchecked `scope:'occurrence'` writer (→ `USE_CHANGE_PROTOCOL`), and whole-series day/time edits of a class (→ `USE_FUTURE_EDIT`); other series edits are now strictly validated. Callers updated: `calendar-integrity-test.ts`, `e2e-editing.ts`.
- Evidence: `scripts/class-change-http-test.ts` 41/0 (validation, read-only preview, double click, lost-response replay, key reuse, stale, mock booked after preview, restore over a newly booked slot, six staggered edit-versus-book rounds both outcomes seen plus a deterministic change-then-book BK409, series_from with carried/inert exceptions and version bump). Browser `tests/e2e/lifecycle/class-editing.spec.ts`: desktop 2/2 and mobile-390 2/2 (invalid links, keyboard open/Escape/focus return, move with injected lost response and Retry, one exception, recovery link to the exact task and an explicit move, reload of the moved class, restore refused over a new booking then succeeding, second-tab booking after preview refused with entries kept, future Tue→Mon change with old week unchanged and accepted cycle/tasks unchanged). One earlier desktop run hit a signed-out page at the first navigation (login race), not reproduced.
- Restore/upgrade: `beta:restore:rehearse` now applies every post-baseline migration BEFORE the dump, restores into a separate database and exercises class_change (preview, apply, replay, grants): 15 checks. `beta:upgrade:rehearse` run on the final tree (see verification).

**Not done (user consequence)**
- Recovery is the existing task drawer, not a reviewed draft: after a change the affected unfinished timed tasks are named and "Review changes to my week" opens the first one; the student moves each explicitly. There is no one-step reviewed adjustment draft, no "accept once", and no deferred/unplaced handling.
- Export identity: after `series_from` the future sessions belong to a new commitment id, so their calendar-file UIDs change from the effective date; earlier UIDs are preserved.
- Multiple classes on one day cannot be logged independently in the class log (date-level); not changed.
- Not browser-proven: Home/Tests-scope/Coach agreement on the saved occurrence, IME composition, partial class-log → next-week continuation → explicit confirm in the browser (proven at endpoint level only), moved-occurrence ICS identity.
- Carried exceptions are not considered by the series conflict preview (conservative: may report a conflict a carried skip would remove).
- `calendar-integrity-test` "closed week reads as history" (3 checks) fails on this tree; not investigated (page code untouched by this slice). `e2e-editing.ts` could not be run: the shared +e2e account holds a stale "Coaching mock" booking from another worker.

### 9.39 B3 closeout: reviewed study-work recovery, effective-date/exception correctness, history failures, browser proofs (15 Nov 2026) — DONE for the closeout scope below; the gaps are listed, not hidden
Nothing committed. Two additive migrations, applied to the disposable project through the checksum runner (the only pending files): **20261225_class_recovery** and **20261226_class_restore_order** (a pg_get_functiondef patch: a restore with nothing to restore answers NO_CHANGE before the elapsed-slot rule). 20261224 is untouched. Settings final acceptance, B4, Journey C and real-bank activation were not touched; Journey B stays partial.

**Reviewed recovery of existing study work — final contracts**
- Entry: the success dialog of a saved class change (receipt = `checkpoint_commands` row, `command='class_change'`, key = the operation key) offers **Review changes to my week** → `calendarReviewHref(key)` = `/app/calendar?plan=review&change=<operationKey>` (`plan=review` without `change` keeps its old empty-week meaning). The page validates the key server-side (`getClassChange`: the student's own receipt of kind class_change; malformed/foreign/other-kind → `[data-recovery-unavailable]`, never another change). The class change is already the authoritative outcome; recovery failing, being dismissed or never opened does not touch it.
- Preparation: `POST /api/planning/current-week/preview` with `{ reason: 'class_change', change }` (allowlisted; any other reason → 400; no body = the original empty-week path, unchanged). Writes ONE `plan_drafts` row (`purpose='class_change_recovery'`, same table, fingerprint = `current_week_source_hash`, revision, planning date, cycle bounds); never a task. A partial unique index (user, cycle, fingerprint, change key) where ready makes a repeat for unchanged inputs return the same draft; changed inputs supersede the old ready draft. Render/GET writes nothing; the panel prepares through the POST.
- Planner (`src/lib/planning/classRecoveryPlan.ts`, pure, 14 checks): affected = unfinished not-started timed tasks the database reports overlapping protected time or a booked mock in the EFFECTIVE schedule (`class_recovery_overlaps`, built on `protected_occurrences` so skips and moves count), plus day-preferred work on a day whose load no longer fits (`current_week_available_minutes`; lowest priority, latest, first). Placement: real study windows minus protected time, mocks and every timed task (including the ones moving, so sequential strict moves cannot collide); never earlier than the original date or than today/now; never after a later sitting of the same reading; only dates the route/exam horizon allow; durations never changed. Started/recorded/protected/continued work and papers/mocks are shown as constraints (`kept`), never moved. Work that does not fit is `unplaced` with its minutes. Nothing is inferred as completed or missed.
- Acceptance: `POST /api/planning/current-week/accept` with `{ draftId, expectedRevision, reason:'class_change', choices:{taskId:'keep'|'defer'} }` → RPC `accept_class_recovery` (authenticated, scope_lock first, draft `for update`, profile then cycle locks, `class_recovery_problem` revalidation against the live source hash). Each move goes through the existing `apply_task_move` boundary (strict about overlaps, history event, revision check) in the SAME transaction; "set aside for weekly review" uses the existing `set_task_execution(..., 'deferred')`; any refusal raises and rolls EVERYTHING back (a changed task, mock, class, availability, exam or cycle makes the draft stale as a whole: `STALE_PROPOSAL`, `rebuild:true`). The draft closes `accepted` with a receipt (`moved`, `set_aside`, `kept`, per-task old→new); a repeat/lost response/double click returns the stored receipt with `replayed:true`. `POST …/dismiss` with `reason:'class_change'` → `dismiss_class_recovery` ("Keep my current study times"): nothing moves, the class change stays.
- Visible states (Calendar `ClassRecoveryPanel`): "class saved, changes are only a proposal"; old → proposed with reason; "No room for these" with explicit keep/set-aside choices that survive a rebuild; "Left where they are"; what stays unchanged (count); accepted receipt with links to the moved tasks; dismissed; failed-to-prepare (class saved, nothing moved, Try again from the same receipt); unconfirmed accept (retry the same request). Unresolved overlap stays discoverable: a Calendar banner (`[data-recovery-pending]`) and a Home/Coach attention item (`class_recovery` in the operating-state attention queue) until the student accepts or the overlap is gone.
- Changed task identity: only `local_date`/`starts_at`/`placement` (via apply_task_move) or `execution='deferred'` (via set_task_execution) change; ids, titles, scope, lineage, reported minutes, confirmations, history are untouched; one `moved` event per moved task.

**Series correctness**
- Effective dates: `getStudyWeekContext` now reads class series with BOTH `starts_on` and `ends_on` against the asked-about local date in the student's zone (previously: end-date cutoff only, against the machine's yesterday, so an old series and its future successor were combined). `weekForDate` uses the same rule; beyond every known cycle it chains `prospectiveWeek` forward from the last accepted/current cycle: a cycle's pulse day comes from the pattern in force on the day it STARTS and it ends on the first such pulse day at least seven days after its start (steady state = exactly 7 days; the one cycle that begins after a pattern change may be longer; a cycle spanning the change keeps its own pulse day; no overlap, no gap). Accepted cycles and the existing class-cycle algorithm are untouched. Proof: `series:effective:test` 15 checks (before/on/after the effective date, long before, not-yet-started and ended series, 400 consecutive dates each in exactly one cycle, no overlap/gap, 8+ weeks ahead, no premature paper-day shift).
- Carried exceptions: `class_change_preview` now computes old/new schedules, conflicts and affected work from the EXCEPTION-ADJUSTED schedule (`class_effective_occurrences`: skips removed, carried moves at their real slot), checks booked mocks beyond the displayed 8-week horizon (the horizon is stated: `horizonEnd`), and `class_change_apply` inserts the successor as `archived`, copies the carried exceptions, then activates it — so the booking guard judges the FINAL schedule, not an intermediate one (a carried skip over a booked mock now saves; a carried move into a booked mock is refused). Race protection and scope-lock order unchanged.
- Moved dates: `class_occurrence_state` (shared by the command and the UI) decides editability from where the class ACTUALLY is: move/cancel need the actual (moved) start in the future and no class log on that date; restore needs the ORIGINAL slot in the future and unlogged. A class moved out of a past slot into the future stays editable but cannot be restored into the elapsed slot; an elapsed, never-moved or logged class stays protected. Checked in Europe/London, Pacific/Auckland and America/Los_Angeles at 00:30 and 23:15 today.
- Export identity (existing export reader + independent `ical.js` parse): a single moved occurrence keeps its original UID (same UID set, only its time changes); historical UIDs/times survive a structural series change. Limitation (one-way snapshot): after `series_from` the future occurrences belong to a new commitment, so their UIDs from the effective date are new; subscribers re-import the snapshot. Not changed.

**Three "closed week reads as history" failures (calendar-integrity)**: captured (page 200, no history/future notice, none of the history strings). Cause: a FIXTURE defect, not a product regression. Onboarding on a Sunday evening chose a future first cycle (starts tomorrow); the fixture's "closed" week was active-start minus seven days, i.e. ended TODAY, and a viewed week that still contains today is correctly not labelled past. The fixture now ends the closed cycle before today in the student's zone (asserted), and a boundary check proves a viewed week containing today is never labelled past. All completed-work, assessment-summary, no-current-actions and return-to-current-week assertions are kept. `calendar:integrity:test` 44/0.
**`e2e-editing.ts`**: no longer uses the shared +e2e account (another worker's stale booking); it creates its own ordinary student (registered at creation, deleted at the end, also on failure), uses the accepted cycle's ahead days, and removes only that student's own seeded "Work" block. 40/0 (task concurrency, conflict, split, undo and class-edit assertions kept).

**Evidence**
- Endpoint/race: `class:change:test` 41/0 (still); `class:recovery:test` 52/0 (draft-only preparation, reuse, foreign/other-kind receipts, concurrent double accept, lost-response replay, history events, deferral, stale refusal moving nothing, rebuild, dismissal, carried skip over a mock, carried move into a mock, far mock, moved-from-past editability, same-day both sides of midnight in three zones, logged-day protection, export identity); `class:recovery:plan:test` 14/0; `series:effective:test` 15/0.
- Browser (`class:editing:browser`, Playwright, owned dev server, three throwaway students per project, all deleted): **desktop 7/7** in the final full run (`docs/evidence/class-editing-browser-2026-11-15-desktop-lbmwhzcpzyzwn.json`, 34 steps, 0 unexpected failed requests/console errors). **390px: 6/6 in one full run, plus the partial-class journey 1/1 after a real fix** (`…mobile-390-lbmwhzohj3cmf.json`); the full mobile project was not repeated end-to-end after the fix. Steps: deep link/keyboard/lost-response/restore/stale/conflict (kept); this-and-future (kept); **recovery**: save, review, nothing moved while preparing and reloading, one draft, keyboard choice, lost accept response retried → "already saved", exact placements and one history event, deferred no-room task, unaffected tasks untouched; **failed preparation** (class saved, nothing moved, Try again from the same receipt), **dismissal** (Calendar banner + Home attention item, fresh proposal on re-open), **two-tab task change** and **two-tab mock booking** each refuse the whole adjustment with nothing moved and keep the student's choice across Rebuild, then accept once; **future pattern** (cancel a Thursday, book a mock into its slot, change this-and-future with new times: no false conflict, skip carried, earlier classes untouched, forecast 11 weeks ahead uses the new time), moved-from-past editability (Move/Cancel offered, Restore not, protected history for an elapsed unmoved class); **IME composition** (Enter confirming a candidate does not send; text kept), Tab inside the dialog, Escape returns focus; **partial class log through the UI** ("Part of it" + "Continued from before", form says continuing is not completion) → recorded one-week time fixture (`shiftStudentTimeline` receipt; dates, lineage and minutes preserved) → real weekly command (successors have no class link, lineage only) → Tests scope shows both portions pending with the ORIGINAL class date and a confirmation form each → explicit whole-outcome confirmation through the UI leaves only the other portion pending → Home's week count equals the database/Calendar count, Calendar shows both continuation tasks, the class-log context no longer lists the logged class, the Coach's class-log answer points at the editor and never offers the logged class.
- Real defect found by the 390px run and fixed: the class-log form widened the page to ~594px (a long select option sized the grid column); `.class-log` grids now use `minmax(0,1fr)` (`src/styles.css`). Pending-portion copy corrected: the confirmation form appears once the continuation task is finished (the text said "confirm below" with nothing below).
- Restore/upgrade: `beta:upgrade:rehearse` clean on the final tree (functions 136/136, policies 94, triggers 39, RLS 99, grants matched, 0 checksum mismatches); `beta:restore:rehearse` 20 checks (later migrations applied before the dump, restored into a separate database; occurrence-standing function, recovery reader, grants and the recovery command's callable-by-student/anonymous-refused behaviour verified on the restored database).
- `npm run verify` exit 0 on the final tree (includes the new `class:recovery:plan:test`, `series:effective:test`, `tests:scope:test` 75/0, `currentweek:state:test` 24/0, `home:state:test` 63/0, `classlog:test` 51/0, `studyweek:test` 69/0, `pulse:weekboundary:test` 35/0, `firstcycle:test` 36/0); `tsc --noEmit` clean. Also run and passing on a fresh owned student: `calendar:integrity:test` 44/0, `e2e:editing` 40/0.
- Cleanup: owned dev server stopped by its recorded pid only; `next-env.d.ts` restored; scratch scripts and `test-results` removed; every throwaway student (tags cr, cr2, ed, cp, ce, co, cc) deleted by id in `finally` blocks (none leaked this time); the seven older `integ+` accounts and `integ+latepoor…` (another worker's) untouched; generated browser-evidence JSON from interrupted/duplicate runs removed, the four that back this section kept.

**Not done / limitations (user consequence; reproduction)**
- A recovery proposal is built from the saved schedule and the student's own accepted cycle only; work in a LATER cycle is never touched (cross-week continuation remains the weekly review's job). A class change whose overlap falls on a day with no study window leaves that work flagged, not moved.
- Sibling order inside one day is not re-checked when a same-reading sitting is moved within the day (shortcut noted in the planner; it only constrains dates).
- SUPERSEDED by §9.40 (founder decision §19, 15 Nov): the minimum-seven-day transition rule described above was replaced; the first affected unaccepted cycle now closes before the next class of the changed schedule even if shorter than seven days.
- Browser coverage gaps: a class change in a second tab and an availability change after the preview are covered by the same source-hash freshness check (it includes commitments, exceptions and availability windows) but were NOT exercised directly; only a changed task (endpoint and browser) and a newly booked mock (browser) were. A carried MOVE into a booked mock is endpoint-proven only. The 390px project was run end-to-end once and the partial-class journey separately after the CSS fix. One hydration-mismatch console message appeared once in a CPU-loaded 390px run (message text truncated by the recorder) and did not reproduce in three later runs; the spec now waits for network-idle before keyboard activation and logs 1,800 characters of any console error.
- Coach agreement is proven for its class-log answer (points at the same editor, never offers the class just logged); its generic "what is next" answer does not name task ids, so task-level Coach/Home agreement is proven through Home's week count and Calendar, not through the Coach text.
- Same-day classes still share one date-level log (not occurrence-level): not claimed.
- Real-bank scope/paper results were not exercised (this slice uses no bank); the browser partial-class journey proves scope visibility and explicit confirmation, not a scored paper (B4).

### 9.40 Phase 1: feasible, scoped study blocks from class learning (15 Nov 2026) — DONE for the package below; gaps listed
Nothing committed. Additive migrations applied to the disposable project through the checksum runner: **20261227_class_log_feasible** (apply_class_log replaced from its effective definition, same signature/grants) and **20261228_recovery_sitting_order** (accept_class_recovery patched in place). 20261224–26 untouched. Settings, B4, Journey C, late-mock recovery and question-bank work were not touched.

**Reproduction (from the current code, before editing).** `consolidation.ts` had a fixed 90-minute ceiling and never received `profiles.max_session_minutes`; `previewClassLog` built each day's room with `dailyCapacityMinutes(..., cycle.timezone)` — whole-day capacity, no clock — and the planner treated a day's "capacity" as a sum, so an 88-minute follow-up was one 88-minute task and 132 minutes could land on a day whose only window (09:00–13:00) had passed at 19:54, with a "comfortable" verdict. The persisted tasks carried only `rationale`; `apply_class_log` set no `lm_id`, no objective/approach/completion text, no group. I did not capture the old planner's output by running it (the tree had already been edited when I reached that step and reverting was ruled out); the owner's audit steps are the reference, and each symptom is now a named regression below.

**Final contracts**
- *Rooms* (`pulse/capacity.ts`): `dailyFreeRanges(user, weekday, date, tz, notBefore)` returns the contiguous free ranges (windows counted once, touching windows merged, minus classes/commitments/timed mocks, and for the clock's own date minus time already passed); `dailyCapacityMinutes` is exactly their total (no behaviour change). The class-log service subtracts accepted open load per date (a timed task its exact interval, an untimed one from the largest gaps; completed/skipped/deferred tasks do not occupy room) — the same sources the weekly planner and Calendar use.
- *Planner* (`classlog/consolidation.ts`, pure): input `days[{date, capacityMinutes, gaps}]`, `maxSittingMinutes`, per-item `requiredMinutes` (what is still owed) and `existing` (group, count, last sitting). A sitting is at most `min(90, student limit)` and at most the largest gap it sits in; sittings of one item are near-equal and ordered by date (88 at 45 → 44 + 44; 100 at 45 → 34/33/33); exact minutes (floor 5, the database minimum), no rounding up to 15; ONE sitting of an item per day (existing unique index `plan_blocks_class_outcome_day_idx`), so a split item needs as many days as sittings and the rest is owed; when everything cannot fit it uses every usable minute instead of leaving slivers. Output: tasks with `key`/`after` (declared order), `sitting{index,count|null}`; carryover with `remainingMinutes` and `placedMinutes`; verdict `comfortable|tight|over` from REAL remaining capacity.
- *Preview* (`POST /api/class-log/preview`, a pure read; service `previewClassLog(user, input, now?)` — `now` is an injectable service argument, not a production bypass): tasks now carry `sitting`, `objective`, `rationale`, `approach`, `completion`, `verification`, `reference`, `readingId`, `key`, `after`, `groupId/groupParts/groupNew`; plus `carryover[{title, reason, remainingMinutes, placedMinutes}]` and `summary{maxSittingMinutes, placedMinutes, remainingMinutes, usableMinutes}`. Work placed by an earlier preview is not planned again: an earlier session's item re-enters only for `required − already placed` (retry/next preview owes exactly the remainder, and later sittings continue the numbering inside the SAME scope group).
- *Fingerprint* now also covers the student's sitting limit and the exact minutes owed, on top of every placed task's day/length/mapping/lane/semantic note and the carried-over work.
- *Confirm* (`POST /api/class-log/confirm`, keyed, atomic, retry-safe as before; the client never sends task rows; task ids are derived deterministically): RPC `apply_class_log` takes the scope lock first, then the cycle lock; replays a key; and **refuses with `PREVIEW_CHANGED` (nothing written)** when a reviewed task is dated before today, is longer than the student's limit (generated work only), or its day has fewer usable minutes left than the reviewed set needs (`current_week_available_minutes`, Calendar's own rule). The route answers 409 `preview_changed` with a rebuilt preview and the UI keeps the input. It writes the session, items, tasks (with `lm_id`, objective, approach, completion, verification, reference, `dependencies` = the sitting that must come first) and one `task_scope_groups` row per item with ≥2 sittings (extended, never replaced, when later previews add the rest). The database does NOT re-derive already-accepted load (the service does, under the fingerprint); a concurrent task insert between preview and confirm is therefore judged only by the day-capacity rule above.
- *Task meaning* (`classlog/taskText.ts`, pure; fills existing fields, no content/pages/answers): scope = mapped outcome + Reading + the bounded part (the student's own note quoted); why-now = class date, or the revision reason, labelled a plan assumption; approach = recall → check against own notes → application exercise from own material, split across sittings so none reads alike and each says it continues the previous one; done-means allows partial reporting and says it records this sitting only; verification says a later weekly paper can test the outcome once every sitting is done AND the student confirms (full), or that a part/continued portion stays pending until logged "All of it" or confirmed whole. Calendar drawer shows objective, why, approach, done-means, **How it can be checked**, shared-task progress and **Curriculum / Tests links scoped to the same reading** (new `verification_intent`, `reading` on entries).
- *Class-log UI*: preview states the sitting limit, minutes placed, minutes not yet placed, and per unplaced item the minutes still owed and why; "comfortable" now means real remaining room; unplaced work is described as owed and re-offered, not lost.
- *Accepted vs unplaced*: placed sittings are saved (their group expects the settled count, or placed + estimated remainder); the remainder is not saved as tasks, is reported, and re-enters the next class-log preview (and the weekly review carryover contract is unchanged).

**Scope and history invariants (all proven below).** One class session per key (double confirm/lost response → same session and tasks); previews write nothing; every sitting keeps class session, outcome, reading and group; a part/continued portion stays pending however many of its sittings are completed, and after a week boundary (continuation has lineage only: `class_session_id` null, no second attendance, original class date kept); explicit whole-outcome confirmation on the open week's block still works and confirms only that outcome; closed-week tasks keep cycle, state and minutes; reported actual minutes are recorded separately from the plan's minutes; no frozen paper, approval, scoring or repair rule was changed.

**§4 same-day order.** Declared order is `plan_blocks.dependencies` (the sittings that must come first). The recovery planner now keeps predecessors before dependents (never earlier on a later date, never overlapping/after on the same date, completed/cancelled predecessors do not constrain, no declared order = no constraint, a shared reading or a title is never an order) and decides predecessors first; `accept_class_recovery` re-checks every pair involving a moved task after the moves and rolls the whole acceptance back (`STALE_PROPOSAL`) on an inversion, so a stale or tampered draft cannot bypass it. Proven by 6 pure cases and a database case with a tampered draft.

**Approved boundary correction (founder §19).** `prospectiveWeek` now closes a cycle the day before the next class that STARTS a cycle under the schedule in force on that class day: steady state stays seven days; the first affected unaccepted cycle can be shorter (a one-day cycle the day before the first class of the new schedule); self-study keeps Monday-start/Sunday-paper; accepted cycles are never re-derived (only dates after the last known cycle are produced). `series-effective-test.ts` 20/0 (adjacent, no overlap/gap over 400 days, shorter-than-seven transition proving the next-class boundary itself, normal following cycles, right-after-accepted-cycle one-day cycle, forecast across the change and beyond eight weeks).

**Evidence**
- Pure: `classlog:test` 80/0 (88→44+44, 100→34/33/33, exact minutes, fragmented gaps, one-per-day, partial placement with exact remainder, retry owes only the remainder, no-room verdict, 20.4 min at a 20-minute limit [regression: it placed nothing], limit below the minimum piece, elapsed 19:54 Kolkata, local midnight, London spring-forward gap 00:59Z/01:01Z, text specificity/no invented pages), `class:recovery:plan:test` 20/0 (order cases), `series:effective:test` 20/0.
- Live (real HTTP + Postgres, ordinary throwaway students, injected clock only as a service argument): `classlog:feasible:test` 53/0 — 45-minute cap with real placement and one confirmed session, double confirm and lost-response replay, saved dates/durations/group/order/semantic fields; elapsed window (19:54, midnight, no room before the paper day); overlapping and touching windows once, a booked mock and a protected commitment each removing exactly their interval, "from now" on the day itself; small capacity (20 minutes placed, exact remainder owed, later preview owes only the remainder, then exactly the remainder is planned into the same group); stale: sitting limit changed, booking added (both refused as `preview_changed` with a rebuilt preview, nothing written), and the database directly refusing a past-dated task, an over-limit sitting and a day with too little time left, then accepting a set that fits; part + continued at a 20-minute limit → reported (the student's own actual minutes recorded separately from the plan's minutes) → recorded one-week time fixture → real weekly command → no leak, original class date visible, explicit confirmation of the continued portion only; ordered siblings with a tampered reviewed draft refused by the database and nothing moved, an order-preserving move accepted.
- Delivered B3 suites rerun on the final tree: `class:change:test` 41/0, `class:recovery:test` 52/0, `class:scope:rollover:test` 16/0, `calendar:integrity:test` 44/0, `class-editing` browser desktop 7/7 and 390px 7/7 (two runs of one test after fixing it: see below).
- Browser (`classlog:followup:browser`, desktop and 390px, one throwaway student each, no ignore rules except one named allowance: the drawer's own scope read cancelling its in-flight request through its AbortController): class log (All of it + Part of it) → preview shows the 45-minute limit and sittings ≤ 45 with "sitting 1 of n" → nothing written by previewing → confirm → one session, exactly the previewed sittings, one scope group, declared order, dates after the class and not past → exact drawer (scope, class date, method, done-means, verification, shared-task progress, Curriculum link `/app/curriculum/<topic>/<reading>` and Tests link) → "Partly done" with 12 minutes (plan minutes unchanged, actual recorded as a separate event) → reload keeps it → Home's week count equals the database, the Curriculum reading page renders, Tests shows the part as pending with its class date and not the full outcome → keyboard/focus/Escape; width checked as `clientWidth ≤ configured viewport` and `scrollWidth ≤ clientWidth`.
- Test repairs forced by the corrected behaviour: the B3 partial-class journey (`class-editing.spec.ts`) used to pass only because the old planner put follow-up work on an elapsed day of a cycle ending tomorrow; it now builds a cycle with real room for its throwaway student (recorded fixture) and closes it for the weekly-command step. `scripts/e2e-class-log.ts` still targets the shared +e2e account (reset-based) and was NOT run or updated.
- `npm run verify` exit 0 and `tsc` clean on the final tree. `beta:upgrade:rehearse` clean (functions 136/136, policies 94, triggers 39, RLS 99, grants matched, 0 checksum mismatches, 115 ledger rows); `beta:restore:rehearse` 24 checks including the restored class-log command (callable by a student, refuses a foreign cycle, refused to anonymous, carries the feasibility and order checks).

**Unresolved / limits (consequence)**
- A follow-up with more sittings than usable days owes the rest (one sitting per item per day is a database invariant); the student sees the minutes still owed and the next class-log preview offers them again. Reproduce: log a large outcome with only one free day.
- Generated sittings stay day-preferred (untimed); contiguity is enforced against free gaps, but no clock time is assigned.
- The weekly planner's own `splitIntoSittings` sittings still carry no `dependencies`/group (unchanged), so §4's order guarantee covers sittings that declare order (class-log follow-ups), not older split tasks.
- `apply_class_log` judges each day's capacity without the already-accepted load; the service does that under the fingerprint, so a task added in the same instant as the confirm could over-fill a day (no past/limit/clock violation is possible).
- `e2e-class-log.ts` and `e2e-lifecycle.ts` class-log steps were not rerun; Journey B's class-log browser step was not rerun after the preview-text change (its button/heading text is unchanged).
- Coach/Home deterministic agreement was re-checked only through the delivered B3 spec; no new Coach behaviour was added or claimed.

**Affected regressions on the final tree**: classlog-feasible 53/0; e2e-class-log (now an owned throwaway student) 60/0 on four consecutive runs; class-change 41/0; class-recovery 52/0; rollover 16/0; calendar-integrity 44/0; e2e-editing 40/0; coach-actions 45/0; weekly-continuity pass; class-editing browser 14/14.

**Defects found by this work and fixed**: (1) concurrent same-key confirms could answer `preview_changed` instead of replaying (found when `e2e-class-log` failed 2 of 3 runs; fixed by re-checking the key before refusing; regression added); (2) after Escape the drawer lost focus when opened by deep link (`useFocusTrap` now lands on `main`/`h1`).

**Intentionally unchanged / limits (stated, not hidden)**
- Generated sittings are day-only; a later real time is checked by the existing protected-time and declared-order rules. Unknown legacy order stays unknown (no backfill). A class logged before `planned_minutes` existed is priced at today's pace for the open cycle only and stamped on first allocation; older legacy items in closed cycles are not carried.
- `apply_redistribution` (resize of timed work) relies on its existing overlap check for order; a lengthened timed predecessor overlapping its timed successor is refused there, other cases are covered by `apply_task_move`.
- Coach `confirm_coach_move` maps an `ORDER` refusal to the existing "task not movable" stale reason (nothing moves).
- A deliberate re-estimate of planned minutes is not offered (nothing silently rewrites them).
- The packing check is conservative, not an exact bin-packing optimum; the planner and the database use the same placement rule and the database has a best-fit fallback.
- Real-bank assessment availability was not exercised (zero eligible bank does not block planning).
- Journey B's wider assessment leg was not rerun.

**Cleanup**: my dev server stopped, `next-env.d.ts` restored, `test-results` removed, a stray local Postgres from an accidental `--help` run stopped and its temp dir removed, nine run-owned students (`ca*`/`co*`, from interrupted runs) deleted by exact address; older `br/cch/ord` accounts untouched.

### 9.42 Owner-side Phase 1 closeout and Phase 2 handoff (16 Nov 2026)

**Disposition:** the bounded Phase 1 study-follow-up loop is closed after additional fixes and independent reruns. This is not certification of the whole application, question-bank readiness or production authentication. Phase 2 implementation has not started. Nothing committed or pushed; unrelated dirty work preserved.

**Additional defects reproduced and fixed**

- **Existing overload disappearing from capacity.** Both TypeScript `roomOfDay` and SQL `class_day_packs` ignored an accepted unsplittable sitting if it fit none of the available gaps. A 40-minute accepted sitting alongside two 30-minute gaps could therefore leave those gaps available for new work. They now refuse additional generated allocation on that overloaded day, preserving the existing work for review rather than silently splitting/ignoring it. The new closeout suite failed against the old SQL and passes against the patch.
- **Incomplete obligation history.** Class-session/item/allocation readers, group-obligation readers and the class catalogue now paginate in stable order and batch identifier lists. Read errors propagate. A real owned fixture with 1,001 historical items and allocations plus a separate unallocated item is fully counted without inventing debt. A controlled second-page failure is refused rather than reported as an empty/partial history.
- **Incomplete retry identity.** Same-key retries now compare the class note, portion notes and studied-in-class flags as well as the date/kind/outcomes. Changes are refused instead of falsely acknowledged as saved. Input ordering and blank/default equivalence are handled deliberately. Read errors cannot masquerade as a successful receipt. The database checks the same fields and replays an identical entry after its original cycle closes; the active-cycle requirement still applies to new entries. The service also re-checks replay when a concurrent save makes preview unavailable.
- **Legacy open-cycle rollover rejection.** A pre-upgrade item with null `planned_minutes` appeared in the reviewed next-week preview but was rejected by acceptance. The saved row now carries the reviewed estimate, which is stamped once in the atomic weekly acceptance. A read/preview still writes nothing. Its regression failed before the migration, then passed through the real weekly command.
- **Cancelled class siblings becoming phantom parts.** When cancelled class work had been fully reallocated and completed, the old cancelled member prevented the scope group from ever completing. For measured class obligations, the summary now uses non-cancelled terminal members and still requires zero unallocated minutes. Ordinary split groups and unknown legacy groups retain their conservative policy. The closeout fixture failed before the reader fix and passes afterwards; this does not convert a PART into whole-outcome coverage.
- **Validation and proof quality.** Class-log input rejects impossible calendar dates before SQL. The authority suite now observes a waiter blocked by the exact holder PID and command, rather than any advisory-lock waiter. The feasible-suite small-room fixture now uses one genuinely free future weekend occurrence, requires exactly 20 placed minutes, and enlarges that same window to prove allocation of the remainder. Its earlier elapsed/Work-blocked-day setup could pass the placement assertion with zero tasks. Browser login setup now asserts the destination and presence of a session cookie before using the app.

**Database:** additive `20261231_phase1_closeout` applied through the checksum runner to the owner-confirmed disposable development project `asylsxszdhlnzewhwtga`. It patches the effective function definitions with checked replacement anchors, preserving other changes and grants. Rolled-back rehearsal succeeded before apply. Latest dry-run: **0 pending**. SHA256: `d8994d65961936e5ed9353d095edc03aec740a2eceb4e8858f4d745d3c629277`.

**Final evidence** (same final application code; no question-bank fixture required):

| Command / proof | Result |
|---|---|
| `npm run classlog:closeout:test` | 14 passed, 0 failed; real owned records, 1,001-item history, conservative packing, retry identity, closed-cycle replay and cancelled/replaced scope. |
| `npm run classlog:obligation:test` | 30 passed, 0 failed; includes two rollovers and the new pre-upgrade acceptance case. |
| `npm run classlog:authority:test` | 41 passed, 0 failed; exact-blocker concurrency checks, authoritative allocation and movement/order. |

| Browser class-followup spec, localhost:3157 | **6/6**, three complete journeys at desktop and 390px; final full run 5.1 minutes, unexpected-error checks enabled. Playwright used because the Browser plugin/skill was unavailable. |

Machine-readable proof, source hash, command details and browser evidence paths: `docs/evidence/phase1-owner-closeout-2026-11-16.json`. Tested modified/untracked source fingerprint: `20524702df1cb9a1` (full algorithm/hash in that artifact).

**Failed runs retained honestly:** the first new closeout tests exposed the SQL packing and closed-cycle replay failures; the obligation regression exposed the legacy rejection; a further closeout case exposed the cancelled-member problem. The feasible-suite fixture correction took two iterations: first choose real free time, then expand that same occurrence instead of adding windows outside the bounded fixture cycle. An initial browser run was 5/6 with a 401 during the sixth fixture's login/context setup, before its class journey. Its exact authentication cause is unclassified. Login assertions were tightened and a fresh complete desktop/mobile run passed 6/6; authentication implementation was not changed and this is not a claim that provider/session intermittency is solved.

**Intentional limits:** generated sittings remain day-only; conservative packing is not an optimisation guarantee; unknown legacy order is not fabricated; pre-upgrade closed-cycle missing estimates are not reconstructed into invented debt. The wider scored Journey B leg and real-bank readiness were not rerun here. Existing reported time, partial-scope safeguards, frozen papers, access controls and accepted history remain intact.

**Cleanup:** all registered students cleaned by their owning suites; run-prefix account check found zero remaining. The owned port-3157 server was stopped using its recorded wrapper PID, its isolated output removed, and generated type paths restored. Upgrade/restore clusters cleaned themselves. No other server, owner account, older fixture account or question bank was reset.

It covers partial versus whole coverage, complete history reads and error states, assessment startability across pages, completed external-mock context, planning-language interpretation and honest roadmap/week explanations. **Stated precisely, not hidden**
- Narrowing (final two weeks) is proven by a preview with an injected clock. Acceptance cannot run under a fake database clock, so it is proven against the real clock only for the horizon CUT (dates), not for the clock-dependent wording.
- There is no separate "paused" study policy in the canonical exam helpers (a paused exam attempt is an attempt state); the exam-sat state is the one that stops new allocation.
- The exam edit was driven through the canonical `/api/onboarding/exam` route that the Settings editor calls, not by operating the Settings form itself in this spec.
- The first request to a cold dev route can fail once with a retryable 503; test helpers retry that single command (receipt-protected). Two setup failures during this closeout were my loopback proxy's keep-alive handling (fixed; logged as `upstream_error` now), not application failures. One browser run overlapped `npm run verify` and was reset by machine load; it was rerun alone and passed.
- Source-sensitive caution: the closeout spec and proxy are local test tooling; they are not started by the application and are not part of `npm run verify`.

### 9.47 Phase 4 (partial, 17 Nov 2026): one inventory, rights that follow packs, a first grounded slice, a real-provider pilot — NOT software-complete

Delivered and proven: (1) **Rights gap closed.** `--rights-ref` previously made a batch `cleared` from any string, regardless of the packs' own rights, which removed the "rights not cleared" publication blocker. Now `createBatch` (`rightsClaimProblem`) and the database command (migration `20270106_batch_rights_follow_packs`, sha256 `220ffc146b8a805f760c4ba057aabd0a2ed81a84ce5ba09bccbd20538436fd24`, applied to the disposable project, patches the effective `create_item_batch`) refuse `cleared` unless every pack used records cleared rights, an authority and a permitted use. Proof: `grounding:test` 22/0, `batch:integrity:db:test` 38/0 (new pack-decides cases). (2) **One inventory.** `bank-audit.ts` now reads only `getCapacityReport`; the status-only `getBankCoverage` and the 20,000-target apportioning were removed; `capacityReport` gained reading and outcome rows, the paper-ready threshold now uses `MIN_PAPER_QUESTIONS` (was a hard-coded 5), and `npm run bank:matrix` prints topic → reading → outcome → concept with pack, sign-off, candidates, approved routine/held-out and families. (3) **Authoring checklist.** `items:batch packs` checks every pack against the active edition, outcome, concept sign-off and rights; `items:batch provider-check` validates base URL, model access and JSON shape with one tiny request per tier before spend; `--readings` targets a pilot. (4) **First grounded slice (private, git-ignored):** three packs for FSA Reading 32 Inventories (`fsa-r032-a/b/c`), source `2027_CFA_L1V4_FSA_ONLINE.pdf` sha256 `b84e7a95…a7d6e7`, PDF and printed pages recorded, edition 2027, rights `unconfirmed`/internal authoring. All three concepts are exact-mapped but not signed off, so drafting is allowed only as quarantined candidates; none can publish. (5) Synthetic large-bank paging measurement in `bank:depth:test` (8/0): 20,000 rows = 41 ordered pages; a failed page throws, never reads as zero.

**Dated actual inventory (17 Nov 2026, active 2027 v1.0):** 303 raw candidates, 260 automatically checked, 303 quarantined, 17 provisional families; **0 approved routine, 0 approved held-out, 0 confirmed families; 0 of 102 readings paper-ready; 0 of 181 concepts re-check-ready; 343 outcomes below the 3-family minimum.** Source packs: 3 (all R32, rights unconfirmed).

**Real-provider pilot (NVIDIA NIM, configured base URL):** provider-check passed (reasoning 1.1 s, verifier 6.0 s). Pilot batch `627ed74a-2a72-40b7-a487-9e90bb5909e3`, FSA R32, 3 cells, ceiling 12 requests / 30,000 tokens / 15 min, 1 attempt: **0 usable candidates.** Cell 1 `GEN_TIMEOUT` after 240 s (reasoning generator did not return in the bounded time), cells 2–3 `BUDGET_EXHAUSTED` (11 of 12 requests and the 30,000-token reservation used; provider-reported tokens were not recorded because the timed-out call returned none). Stopped at the first budget; no retry escalation. **Corrected reading (18 Nov 2026 review):** the observed facts are the 240 s whole-cell deadline, the `GEN_TIMEOUT`, and the exhausted allowance. That deadline spanned generation AND verification, and the old code recorded no per-stage or per-request evidence, so "the authoring model alone stalled" is **unproven**. "Tokens used" in the original figures meant the *completion-token allowance reserved* before requests (a ceiling for requested completion tokens), not measured consumption; prompt tokens and billed cost are unknown. Nothing is reconstructed for that run. The stage/request evidence added in §9.48 is what will establish the cause on a future, separately approved run.

**NOT done in this pass (still required for software-complete):** the versioned authoring/evaluation package with adversarial items and semantic-check wording; reviewer-screen additions (source reference, verifier limits, duplicate concerns on one view) and concurrent-approval/version-edit proof beyond existing suites; the supported fixture-backed student loop and its desktop/390px browser journey and negative browser cases; fixture-bank capacity tests for skewed/prior-exposure cases; full `npm run verify` and upgrade/restore rehearsals on the final tree (only tsc, migrations:test, grounding, depth, worker-sim, batch-integrity were run). Content-ready is blocked externally: rights authority for the 2027 reference material, human concept sign-off for R32, and human review of any candidate.

### 9.50 Owner completion of Phase 4 and Phase 5 handoff

The owner requested direct completion of remaining Phase 4 work and a Phase 5 prompt, plus a way to remove the publisher-rights dependency. This pass preserves all unrelated uncommitted work and existing publication policy. No commits, deployments, provider calls or real question/concept approvals.

**One remaining software gap fixed directly:** §9.49's limitation was a real transactional gap. `planRepairCheck` checked the planned day, advertised due date and last completed repair plus three local calendar days, but `start_repair_check` could reserve a new paper without repeating that test. A date/completion change between reads could escape the server's preflight.

- New additive `20270111_recheck_start_date_guard` patches the effective command with checked anchors. It retains the scheduling lock, locks linked thread rows, reads the student's timezone, verifies a completed repair event, and computes the latest of the planned date, advertised UTC-encoded date and last completion plus three local days before any reservation writes. Refusals write no blueprint/form/attempt/items/link. Existing attempts still resume before this new reservation gate.
- Keeps the exact-version/family eligibility, 90-question ceiling, ownership and frozen-scope logic. Service-only ACL is restated. The server maps `RECHECK_NOT_DUE` to an actionable waiting response. No user-supplied clock is trusted by the database.
- SHA-256 `bcbf072ba26a8ef485b417581f8651ffa0ebabc08a653fc26bf85e7c9ffb9f65`. Applied through the checksum runner only to the authorised disposable project `asylsxszdhlnzewhwtga`. First rehearsed against the effective hosted definition inside a rolled-back transaction; trust/serialization/resume/ACL anchors preserved and original definition restored after rehearsal.

**Proof**

| Check | Result / meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Old command negative control | `REPAIR_START_WITHOUT_GUARD=1 npm run repair:db:test` fails the new future-date assertion because the old command starts the paper. |
| Isolated repair suite on new guard | **44 scenarios pass**: future planned day, premature completion spacing, later advertised date, missing completion, UTC-encoded due date under an Auckland session for Kolkata/London/LA students, eligible start and existing-attempt resume. |
| `recheck:timezone:test` | **36/0**; includes three direct calls to the deployed database command after changing the advertised date following preflight. Each refuses with the exact due date and no attempt/link. Three run-owned students cleaned. |
| Browser reviewer journey | **4/4 desktop + 4/4 at 390px**, registered fixture content. |
| Browser student journey | **6/6 mobile + 6/6 desktop on rerun**, including score → repair → spacing → fresh scoped re-check → score, under a server timezone different from the London student's. |
| Installation/upgrade | Both commands pass: **105 baseline + 24 later migrations, 129/129 ledger, zero checksum mismatches, 152 matching functions, 216 matching indexes**, matching schema/ACL/reference categories. |
| Restore | **28 checks pass**, nothing persisted. |
| Project gates | `npm run verify` exits 0; canonical TypeScript clean; dry-run **0 pending**. |

The first combined browser run was **19 passed, 1 failed**, not a clean pass. Its desktop weekly-preview response safely refused as changed (409) and committed nothing; the test waited for a save button that therefore did not appear. The exact source of that change was not established. The unchanged desktop journey then passed all six steps. No assertions/allowances were weakened or test history suppressed. A transient test-authoring typo in the new deployed guard probe was corrected before its final 36/0 run; production logic was unaffected.

Browser plugin unavailable in this session; installed Playwright used. Setup/accounts, fixture bank and history shifts are seeded; reviewer/student actions are UI-driven, as their evidence records state. Provider disabled on the owned server. These are software tests, not real-content availability. Actual bank re-read after fixture cleanup: **303 raw, 260 auto-passing, 303 quarantined, zero approved routine/held-out and zero approved families**.

**Publisher dependency:** `docs/CONTENT-AUTHORING-WITHOUT-PUBLISHER-DEPENDENCY.md` documents the supported route: independently authored first-party knowledge packs, or suitably licensed inputs, with truthful author/rightsholder/use provenance. The current schema already supports that route; publisher permission is not a universal requirement for original content. The three unconfirmed book-derived packs are not relabelled and no rights override was added. “We are not teaching” is not treated as blanket permission for book ingestion or derived cards. Ordinary users' private notes/manual cards and planning do not depend on resolving those packs. Current third-party-content limitations and supporting primary sources are recorded in the memo.

**Next phase drafted, not built:** `docs/FINAL-PHASE-5-CURRICULUM-NOTES-FLASHCARDS-AGENT-PROMPT.md`. It follows the owner's tracker/notes/flashcards scope, preserves safe note autosave and diagnostic evidence, simplifies navigation, connects exact work/results/repairs, and addresses the actual card defects found in code: conditional hook after empty-queue return, failed reads shown as empty, repetitions used as a non-monotonic retry token, immediate Again requeue despite a ten-minute due time, and unguarded concurrent edits. Generation uses selected permitted note text or authorised first-party packs, never hidden unconfirmed books. No Phase 5 application changes were made here.

**Cleanup:** owned server stopped by recorded wrapper PID, canonical types restored/rechecked, only owned `.next-phase4-owner` output removed, both locks released. Run-prefix account check found zero remaining students; fixture bank records report zero remaining items/concepts/users. Existing shared server/accounts/output were untouched. Evidence record: `docs/evidence/phase4-owner-final-closeout.json`.

### 9.53 Owner completion of Phase 5 (18 Nov 2026)

The owner requested direct completion of any remaining Phase 5 work, followed by the Phase 6 prompt. No commits, deployment, migrations, external model calls or real-content approvals. The real bank still has zero eligible questions.

**Additional defects reproduced and fixed in this pass**

| Finding | Smallest complete fix | Proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Valid JSON `null` on coverage produced a server error | Reject null/non-object/array payloads before reading fields. Existing validated object/batch semantics and diagnostic protection unchanged. | Old handler: **55 passed, 1 failed** in extended HTTP suite; final **56/0**. |
| Draft fields remained editable while a submitted snapshot saved, so the eventual result could overwrite a newer edit | Freeze submitted draft fields/selection/excerpt and prevent Close during save. Manual create/edit fields and edit Cancel get the same pending-write protection. | Initial browser negative control failed the disabled-field assertion against the old UI. Final desktop/mobile delayed-commit tests pass and leave exactly one saved card. |
| Closing/unmounting a generation or note load could allow its late response to return into the next open panel | Abort reads/generation, ignore obsolete responses using a request epoch, and key the drafter by exact scope. The generation excerpt stays fixed while its request runs. | Browser: close delayed generation, observe its cancellation, reopen from note, no stale drafts. |
| Failed/unconfirmed card edit discarded its retry key | Retain the exact request key on unconfirmed errors; release it only after success or an authoritative stale/not-found response. | Browser: actual update commits, reply is dropped, Retry uses the same key, one card remains at revision 2 with the intended text. |

- `npm run verify`: **exit 0**; canonical TypeScript clean; migration dry-run **0 pending**. No schema change, so the existing §9.52 install/upgrade/restore proof is retained rather than repeated.

### 9.56 Phase 6 — Home, Calendar and Settings connected acceptance (18 Nov 2026)

**The defined Phase 6 software scope is complete.** This closes §9.55; it does not declare the whole app, real question bank or operational launch complete. Phase 7 is drafted and unstarted. No migration, content approval, provider call, real email, commit or push was made in this continuation. The authorised database is disposable development; the file called staging targets that same project.

**Changes and student experience**

- Home retains one canonical next action, partial minutes, truthful defer/undo receipts, exact task/result links, compact progress and folded detail. Exam countdown uses the student's local date. An unreadable exam state cannot advertise an invented active study action. Required failures have a retry boundary using this installed Next version's `retry` API.
- Calendar keeps clock-timed events separate from day-only sittings. Week details are folded so the agenda is reachable near the top on a phone. Today, previous/next and presentation tabs preserve useful navigation. Closed weeks are read-only history; future weeks are labelled forecasts and cannot create accepted study by clicking empty space. Classes, commitments and mocks can still be arranged prospectively.
- Date/time/length fields provide a keyboard equivalent to drag/resize. A 44-minute sitting remains 44 minutes. Pending commands freeze the reviewed fields. Refusals keep the choice and link to the exact protected occurrence, appointment or ordered sibling, including a conflict discovered at the database-command boundary. Unknown/foreign event links say unavailable.
- Founder decision §20 is implemented: a deliberate placement outside usual windows or above the usual sitting limit needs one explicit warning/confirmation for that edit. Day-only work longer than any saved contiguous window also warns. It changes neither Settings nor generated limits; protected bookings, cycle boundaries and declared order remain hard constraints. Generated work remains day-only where the existing planner chooses it.
- Setup saves distinguish authoritative success from unavailable impact comparison. They display the saved version and offer current-state recovery without fabricating a before/after comparison. Stale edits and lost responses keep the student's input. Null/non-object command bodies fail validation rather than producing a server error.
- Display-name saves preserve newer typing, protect IME confirmation and update Home plus both navigation views from the canonical profile. Long names no longer stretch Home or navigation. Account and navigation share explicit this-device sign-out, honest failure messages and a sign-in focus target when the installed Auth client has cleared local credentials.
- Calendar export starts with the displayed week; Settings uses the student's local date. Pending preparation freezes the selected range. Files remain one-way snapshots, with timed/all-day semantics, exclusions and privacy boundaries. Personal export now includes the four missing owned collections (`flashcard_commands`, `flashcard_draft_requests`, `mock_reports`, `mock_report_commands`) using their real primary keys and complete pagination.
- Failed/lost deletion remains unconfirmed until known; a failed sign-in alone is explicitly not proof of deletion. Auth/Storage partial failures and retry preserve the established honest outcomes. Reminder choices, quiet hours and unconfigured delivery are tested without sending mail.
- Coach's deterministic handling of “when is my exam?” now selects exam context rather than an unrelated log intent. No teaching capability or model-dependent authority was added.

**Requirement → proof**

| Area | Acceptance evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Fresh baseline / partial / defer / undo / two tabs | Home browser journey, both viewports; task rows and events checked after consequential actions |
| Midway three-covered-subject entry and Settings effects | Completion browser journey, both viewports; uncertainty, sitting limit and booked target edited through the UI; accepted task snapshot and evidence unchanged |
| Class-led work / occurrence recovery / owed work / continuation | Six selected class browser tests, both viewports; full/part/continued, order, stale preview, no room and reviewed rollover. Existing authority/continuity proofs retained; no new class identity model claimed |
| Mature February / poor external mock / final week / exam sat | February mature-entry two tests, recovery/closeout four tests and completion failed-read/final-history journey; seeded history/scores are identified, consequential proposals and controls are UI-driven |
| Actual unavailable bank / fixture scored loop | Twelve student browser tests across desktop and 390px: start/resume, required confidence, lost answer retry, result, repair, refused early check, consistent dated-history fixture, weekly review and fresh re-check. Actual bank remains unavailable |
| Settings / account / downloads | Sixteen final Settings browser tests; completion account-failure tests at both widths; version/CAS 26/0, classes-finished/undo 21/0, account HTTP 17/0, Auth/Storage failure 7/0, impact-failure 9/0 |

IME, Escape/focus, reduced motion and a reflow-equivalent width check; this is not a claim of testing every assistive technology or native browser zoom gesture |
| Shared logic / regressions | Placement tests 74/0; Coach interpretation 123/0; canonical gate comparison 18/0; full verification, final TypeScript/route audit, isolated production build and read-only migration preview |

Final source fingerprint: **`4abb25659408f27e`** across 855 listed source/test/script/migration/config files. Final exact command outcomes, artifact names and seeded-versus-driven steps are in `docs/evidence/phase6-final-completion-2026-11-18.json`. Field/route connections: `docs/PHASE-6-FIELD-AND-CONNECTION-MATRIX.md`. Before/after images: `docs/evidence/phase6-screens/`. Browser plugin unavailable; installed Playwright was used.

**Measurement and first-week sizing**

Home no longer composes the whole next-week draft just to read its closing gate. The existing reader has a gate-only projection; full weekly review still receives the original complete preview. The 18-check comparison proves identical gates with current/ended weeks, external mock and no cycle, with no accepted-work or evidence writes. Calendar reuses the already-loaded roadmap input for progress. No global caching or planning-policy change.

Five warm development samples per page (one cold excluded): ordinary Home 145 → 108 instrumented server reads, p50 4,418 → 2,663 ms; long-history Home 158 → 114, p50 4,463 → 2,805 ms. Calendar 127 → 113 and 135 → 119; Settings stays at 13. Full p95 and sample data are retained. These are small development snapshots, not production SLOs or a controlled causal A/B experiment. An overlapped diagnostic run is excluded. A wall-clock gap between portions of the final measurement and earlier long stalls remain unclassified; no operating-system or network cause is claimed.

The midweek fresh-student probe gives 171/171/146/228 minutes across its four remaining study dates, within supplied 180/180/180/240-minute windows, with no sitting above the 45-minute limit. This preserves the current effort model and provides explicit day totals/lighter/move choices; it does not introduce a weekly-hours target or silently redistribute accepted work.

**Failures retained rather than hidden**

Red regressions caught duration rounding, incorrect Coach exam intent, missing export tables, Calendar receipt detail and a day-only fragmented-window warning. The new long-name browser check reproduced Home overflow before the selector/flex correction. An intermediate Sidebar edit failed TypeScript and was corrected before final verification. Some initial suites failed from bad time/room assumptions; corrections are recorded as fixture/harness changes, not product fixes.

The strict sign-out test observed HTTP 204 followed by cancellation of the unused logout response during full navigation. The installed Auth client deliberately resolves that command at response headers (`noResolveJson`). The narrow allowance requires the real 204, POST/local scope and only `ERR_ABORTED` in that exact step; it also proves the first session is refused and a second session remains active. Pre-header failures and other statuses remain failures. Other allowances name deliberate injected faults or identifiable cancelled read-only Next/drawer requests; no blanket 503/API exclusion was added.

**Boundaries and next phase**

The real bank after fixture cleanup is 303 raw / 260 automatically checked / 303 quarantined, **0 approved routine and 0 approved held-out**, with no real paper or re-check capacity. Human content review and legitimate content-use decisions remain external; fixtures do not establish them. No real-provider quality or email-delivery claim is made. Retention/deployment/domain/OAuth/payment operations remain deferred.

Independent same-day class identity and the separately identified manual task-creation transaction/receipt gap belong to the explicitly scoped Phase 7, along with the full A/B/C first-week-to-exam sweep and final cleanup. Phase 6's successful move/resize controls do not prove that creation command atomic. The Phase 7 prompt names this directly rather than hiding it in a general “polish” item.

Final cleanup and gates are recorded in the evidence file: only owned processes/fixtures/output were removed, generated types restored, owner server PID 84412 preserved. No new schema was introduced, so install/upgrade/restore proofs are retained from the preceding schema phase rather than falsely reported as fresh runs.

**What a student can now do**
- **Add a study task** from the Calendar toolbar, choosing the day explicitly and leaving the time open (or click a slot). If the reply is lost, pressing Add again does not create a second task. A sitting above the usual limit or outside saved windows asks once and never changes Settings; protected bookings and the cycle stay hard limits. Repairs, re-checks and mocks cannot be added by hand. Past days now point to Log learning.
- **Log two classes on the same day** as two entries: the form lists each actual class by name and day, a logged class is greyed out, a moved class keeps its original identity, and a clearly separate "not on my timetable" session can be added. Older date-only logs are shown as unresolved and are never guessed onto a class.

**Verified-first findings**: `POST /api/tasks` really did two separate writes with no receipt; class identity really was date-only (`getClassLogContext` collapsed same-day classes into one logged date). Both confirmed on the tree before changing anything.

**Changes**: migrations `20270116_manual_task_command` (`apply_task_create`, service role only) and `20270117_class_occurrence_identity` (occurrence columns, per-student unique key, `apply_class_log` patched from the live definition; the 7-argument form is a wrapper), applied to the authorised disposable project only; a series change "from this date" is followed so the successor's first occurrence is the class already logged; the shared one-time warning helper is now used by move and create; three components with no importer were removed.

The restore rehearsal's definition check targeted the old 7-argument body and was retargeted.

**Calendar navigation.** One repair deep link remained on the result while Calendar's server read was still pending. The callbacks now use local redirects after checking the normalised destination. A rapid double click on mock recovery could land on the adjacent “Not now” control as the primary button shrank. The pending guard and disabled controls preserve the save. The Coach also timed out while reading the whole study plan for a selected report. It now answers that intent from the owned report and exam status, including when the report was withdrawn. The reply makes no claims about upcoming appointments from that path.

Changing the mock recovery save and the Calendar failure retry came from observed browser failures. The late-Friday fresh candidate legitimately got only two readings in the earlier fixture. The rerun used the ordinary setup editor to supply a longer Saturday window, with the same sitting limit.
